### [1] [Global Distinctions Between New Electrovacuum and Kundt Class](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02602)
*Liang Ma,H. Lu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This short note analyzes global differences between two locally-equivalent spacetime solutions: one is an electrovacuum supported by electromagnetic fields, the other is a non-electrovacuum Kundt-type solution generated by accelerating electric and magnetic charges.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify the global geometric and physical distinctions between two locally equivalent but globally distinct spacetime solutions—one electrovacuum, the other sourced by accelerating charges—despite their shared local Petrov type D structure within the Kundt class.

Method: Comparative global analysis of spacetime geometry, causal structure, and physical interpretation; leveraging known classification results for Kundt spacetimes and electrovacuum solutions.

Result: Identifies key global differences including distinct asymptotic structures, horizon properties, singularity behavior, and physical sources—demonstrating that local equivalence does not imply global identity.

Conclusion: Local Petrov type D equivalence within the Kundt class masks profound global disparities in physical interpretation and spacetime topology; the electrovacuum and accelerating-charge solutions represent physically inequivalent gravitational configurations.

Abstract: It was observed [2606.30426] that our recently constructed electrovacuum [2606.23782], supported by electromagnetic fields, can be locally transformed to a special Petrov type D solution within the general Kundt class, which is not an electrovacuum but instead describes a spacetime generated by (accelerating) electric and magnetic charges. In this short note, which serves as a supplemental to [2606.23782], we analyse the major global differences between the two locally-equivalent solutions.

</details>


### [2] [Observational Constraints on Kazakov-Solodukhin Quantum-Deformed Black Holes from M87$^*$ and Sgr A$^*$ Shadows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02631)
*A. Errehymy,Y. Khedif,M. Daoud,B. Turimov,S. Usanov,Z. Yasakov,Z. Avezmuratova*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the Kazakov-Solodukhin quantum-deformed black hole, showing that a single parameter η regularizes the singularity, modifies horizons and photon orbits, lowers Hawking temperature, and yields testable predictions for black hole shadows and light deflection.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a geometrically motivated, singularity-free black hole model incorporating quantum corrections while remaining consistent with general relativity in the classical limit and offering observational signatures.

Method: Analytical study of the Kazakov-Solodukhin metric: examination of curvature invariants, horizon structure, surface gravity, Hawking temperature, photon sphere, black hole shadow, and gravitational lensing; comparison with observational constraints (e.g., EHT shadow size, weak-field tests).

Result: The model eliminates the curvature singularity at r = η, shifts the event horizon, reduces surface gravity and Hawking temperature, displaces the photon sphere, modifies strong lensing, yields an η-dependent circular shadow radius, and introduces an η² correction to weak-field light deflection.

Conclusion: The Kazakov-Solodukhin black hole is a viable, singularity-free quantum-corrected spacetime that preserves GR in the η → 0 limit and provides concrete, potentially observable deviations—especially in black hole imaging and strong-field lensing—that can constrain the deformation parameter η.

Abstract: We explore the Kazakov-Solodukhin quantum-deformed black hole spacetime, characterized by a single deformation parameter \( η\) that encodes quantum corrections to the classical Schwarzschild solution. The model preserves the correct general-relativistic limit as \( η\to 0 \), while introducing significant and physically meaningful deviations in the strong-field regime. A central and remarkable feature of the geometry is the regularization of the classical singularity: curvature invariants remain finite near the minimal radius \( r = η\), effectively replacing the divergent core with a smooth and well-behaved region. This behavior naturally introduces a minimal length scale into the spacetime structure, offering a geometrically motivated resolution of the singularity problem. The deformation modifies the horizon structure, shifts the event horizon location, and alters the mass-radius relation. It also reduces the surface gravity, leading to a lower Hawking temperature and a slower evaporation process, thereby enhancing the thermodynamic stability of the black hole. Photon dynamics are correspondingly affected, resulting in a displaced photon sphere and modified strong-lensing characteristics. While the shadow remains perfectly circular due to spherical symmetry, its size depends sensitively on \( η\). Observational constraints can be expressed through \( \left| R_{sh}(η) - R_{obs} \right| \leq ΔR_{obs}, \) which places an upper bound on the deformation parameter. In the weak-field limit, the deflection angle acquires a quadratic correction proportional to \( η^2 \), ensuring consistency with precision tests while allowing potentially detectable deviations in strong-gravity observations. These features make the model both theoretically appealing and observationally testable.

</details>


### [3] [Interacting Holographic Dark Energy in $f(Q)$ Gravity: Cosmological Evolution and Gravitational Wave Signatures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02792)
*N. Amiriborkhani,Alireza Amani,M. A. Ramzanpour*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes an interactive Holographic Dark Energy (HDE) model within $f(Q)$ gravity, reconstructs field equations using a power-law Hubble parameter, constrains parameters with $H(z)$ data, and shows late-time $\Lambda$-like behavior, classical stability, observational consistency, and modified gravitational wave propagation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile cosmic acceleration with theoretical consistency by unifying holographic dark energy with non-metric $f(Q)$ gravity and incorporating matter–dark energy interaction, while addressing stability and gravitational wave signatures.

Method: Reconstruction of $f(Q)$ field equations under a power-law parametrization of the Hubble parameter; background and tensor perturbation analysis; Bayesian parameter estimation using $H(z)$ observational data; sound speed–based classical stability analysis; computation of energy density, pressure, EoS, and gravitational wave propagation in the $f(Q)$+interacting HDE framework.

Result: The model yields late-time cosmological constant–like behavior ($w_{\\text{DE}} \\to -1$), satisfies observational bounds on $\\Omega_{m0}$ and $\\Omega_{\\Lambda0}$, exhibits positive squared sound speed ($c_s^2 > 0$) near today ensuring classical stability, and predicts modified tensor perturbation evolution due to $f(Q)$ corrections and interaction terms.

Conclusion: The interacting HDE model in $f(Q)$ gravity provides a viable, stable, and observationally compatible framework for cosmic acceleration and offers distinctive imprints on gravitational wave propagation, distinguishing it from standard $\\Lambda$CDM and non-interacting alternatives.

Abstract: In this paper, an interactive Holographic Dark Energy (HDE) model is studied in the framework of modified gravity \(f(Q)\). By adopting a power parameterization for the Hubble parameter, the field equations are reconstructed and the evolution of the universe at the background level and tensor perturbations are investigated. Then, using observational data \(H(z)\), the model parameters are constrained and the dynamical behavior of dark energy throughout the history of the universe is analyzed. Also, the study of the evolution of energy density, pressure and the Equation of State (EoS) parameter of dark energy shows that dark energy in the late universe naturally tends to a region close to the cosmological constant behavior, while in the past it followed a distinct dynamical evolution. Stability analysis based on the speed of sound also indicates that the model has good classical stability around the present era. In addition, the compatibility of the current values of the relative density parameters of matter and dark energy with the observational constraints confirms the ability of the model to reproduce the main features of the observed universe. Next, the propagation of gravitational waves in the cosmological context of the model is investigated. The results show that the corrections due to \(f(Q)\) gravity and the interaction between matter and dark energy can affect the evolution of tensor perturbations and produce signatures distinct from the standard scenario. Overall, the findings of this study indicate that the interactive HDE in the gravitational framework \(f(Q)\) can provide a consistent framework for describing the cosmic acceleration and studying the cosmological consequences of gravitational waves.

</details>


### [4] [Distinguishing wormholes via Einstein rings and global curvature](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02889)
*M. B. Cruz,C. R. Muniz,R. M. P. Neves,Jonathan A. Rebouças*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies gravitational lensing by a static Ellis-Bronnikov wormhole in a curved FLRW universe, deriving a weak-field lens equation with a cubic scaling of Einstein ring radius vs. cosmological distance—distinct from Schwarzschild black holes’ square-root scaling—enabling model-independent discrimination; numerical results show asymmetric curvature dependence, reduced lensing efficiency, and implications for detecting exotic topology and cosmic geometry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a model-independent geometric diagnostic to distinguish wormhole lensing from black hole lensing, and to probe exotic spacetime topology and global cosmic geometry via observable lensing signatures.

Method: Derivation of the weak-field lens equation for a static Ellis-Bronnikov wormhole embedded in a curved FLRW background using curvature-dependent cosmological distances; analytical and numerical analysis of Einstein ring radius scaling, redshift evolution, and sensitivity to spatial curvature.

Result: The wormhole Einstein ring radius scales cubically with cosmological distances (vs. square-root for Schwarzschild black holes); lensing signal exhibits qualitatively different redshift evolution and asymmetric, inversion-prone response to spatial curvature—even under tight constraints like DESI 2024; Ellis-Bronnikov wormholes are significantly less efficient lenses, requiring macroscopic throat radii for microarcsecond Einstein rings.

Conclusion: Cosmological wormhole lensing yields distinctive, observationally testable signatures that can serve as a sensitive, complementary probe of both exotic spacetime topology and the global geometry of the Universe.

Abstract: In this work, we investigate the gravitational lensing properties of a static Ellis-Bronnikov wormhole embedded in a curved Friedmann-Lemaître-Robertson-Walker (FLRW) universe. By employing curvature-dependent cosmological distances, we derive the corresponding weak-field lens equation and demonstrate that the wormhole Einstein ring radius follows a characteristic cubic scaling with cosmological distances, in sharp contrast to the square-root behavior found for Schwarzschild black holes. This distinct scaling leads to a qualitatively different redshift evolution of the lensing signal, providing a model-independent geometric diagnostic to discriminate between wormhole and black hole lensing scenarios. Numerical analysis reveals that the interplay between the local wormhole geometry and the FLRW background produces an asymmetric response to spatial curvature that inverts at intermediate redshifts, exhibiting a non-negligible sensitivity even under tight modern constraints such as those from DESI 2024. We also find that Ellis-Bronnikov wormholes are substantially less efficient gravitational lenses than Schwarzschild black holes of comparable physical scale, implying that microarcsecond-scale Einstein rings require macroscopic throat radii. These results suggest that, should a population of cosmological wormholes exist, their lensing signatures could provide a sensitive, complementary probe of both exotic spacetime topology and the global geometry of the Universe.

</details>


### [5] [Decoherence Effects on Primordial Black Holes and Scalar-Induced Gravitational Waves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02935)
*Waqas Ahmed*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper extends the PBH and SIGW framework by incorporating Gaussian quantum discord as a diagnostic tool for residual quantum correlations in the scalar perturbations that seed PBHs, showing that decoherence—modeled via a Lindblad open-system approach—modifies both PBH abundance (via collapse variance) and the SIGW spectrum (via a squared suppression factor Q_dec²), with the latter offering the clearest observational signature of the quantum-to-classical transition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a controlled quantum-information–theoretic characterization of the quantum-to-classical transition for inflationary curvature perturbations that generate primordial black holes (PBHs) and scalar-induced gravitational waves (SIGWs), moving beyond purely classical or Gaussian statistical descriptions.

Method: Models each (k,−k) mode pair as a two-mode Gaussian state; uses Gaussian quantum discord to quantify residual quantum correlations; employs a Lindblad master equation to describe decoherence via a Gaussian loss channel acting on the scalar covariance matrix; distinguishes discord from a covariance-survival factor Q_dec(k); computes modifications to PBH abundance (through classical collapse variance) and SIGW spectrum (via convolution with Q_dec(ku)Q_dec(kv)).

Result: In the pure squeezed limit, quantum discord grows rapidly with squeezing and is trivially satisfied; the physically relevant regime is mixed states induced by decoherence. Decoherence suppresses the scalar two-point covariance, thereby reducing PBH abundance and scaling the SIGW energy density as Ω_GW^eff ≈ Q_dec² Ω_GW^class for narrow scalar peaks and slowly varying Q_dec.

Conclusion: Quantum discord and decoherence offer a rigorous, observationally accessible framework to probe the quantum origin of PBH-seeding perturbations; the SIGW spectrum provides the cleanest imprint of this quantum-to-classical transition, enabling potential constraints on early-universe quantum information dynamics.

Abstract: Primordial black holes (PBHs) form when large primordial curvature perturbations re-enter the Hubble radius and exceed the classical collapse threshold. These perturbations originate as quantum fluctuations of the inflationary vacuum, motivating a quantum-information description of the PBH-producing scalar sector. We develop a conservative extension of the standard PBH and scalar-induced gravitational-wave (SIGW) framework in which Gaussian quantum discord is used as a diagnostic of residual quantum correlations, not as a new PBH-formation criterion. We describe each \((\bm k,-\bm k)\) pair as a two-mode Gaussian state and show that, in the pure squeezed limit, discord grows rapidly with the squeezing parameter, so low discord thresholds are automatically satisfied for strongly squeezed modes. The nontrivial regime is the mixed state produced by decoherence. Using a Lindblad open-system description, we motivate a Gaussian loss channel for the scalar covariance matrix and distinguish the discord from a covariance-survival factor \(Q_{\rm dec}(k)\). If the decoherence channel suppresses the scalar two-point covariance, PBH abundance can be affected through the classical collapse variance, while the SIGW spectrum is modified more directly by the factors \(Q_{\rm dec}(ku)Q_{\rm dec}(kv)\) inside the radiation-era convolution. For a narrow scalar peak and slowly varying \(Q_{\rm dec}\), this gives the benchmark scaling \(Ω_{\rm GW}^{\rm eff}\simeq Q_{\rm dec}^2Ω_{\rm GW}^{\rm class}\). Thus quantum discord and decoherence provide a controlled way to characterize the quantum-to-classical transition of PBH-producing perturbations, with the clearest imprint appearing in scalar-induced gravitational waves.

</details>


### [6] [Black holes in f(R) theory of gravity with compact extra dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03034)
*Arkady Popov,Sergey Rubin*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper derives exact static, spherically symmetric solutions in $f(R)$ gravity in $4+n$ dimensions with a compact $n$-sphere extra space; finds a Schwarzschild–de Sitter 4D metric with stabilized extra dimensions, but the induced cosmological constant is too large; resolves this via semiclassical vacuum polarization from nonminimally coupled quantum fields, yielding an asymptotically flat Schwarzschild limit where extra-dimensional radius is dynamically set by quantum vacuum expectation values; also analyzes radial dependence of effective Planck mass and extra-radius.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile higher-dimensional $f(R)$ gravity with observational constraints—particularly the small observed 4D cosmological constant—by stabilizing extra dimensions and suppressing unwanted vacuum energy contributions.

Method: Analytical derivation of exact static, spherically symmetric solutions in $D = 4+n$ dimensional $f(R)$ gravity with compact $n$-sphere extra dimensions; incorporation of semiclassical vacuum polarization via nonminimally coupled quantized matter fields using the trace anomaly and effective stress-energy tensor.

Result: An exact solution with 4D Schwarzschild–de Sitter metric and constant extra-radius $L_0$, linked to $\Lambda_4 = (n-1)/L_0^2$; inclusion of quantum vacuum effects yields an asymptotically flat Schwarzschild solution where $L_0$ is dynamically determined by vacuum expectation values; radial dependence shows position-dependent effective Planck mass and extra-radius.

Conclusion: Stabilization of extra dimensions in $f(R)$ gravity requires quantum vacuum contributions to suppress excessive 4D cosmological constant; semiclassical backreaction enables phenomenologically viable, asymptotically flat black hole solutions with dynamically fixed internal geometry.

Abstract: We study static, spherically symmetric solutions in $f(R)$ gravity within a $D = 4+n$-dimensional spacetime, where the extra dimensions form a compact $n$-sphere of constant radius. We derive an exact solution in which the four-dimensional part of the metric corresponds to the Schwarzschild - de Sitter metric, while the extra dimensions are stabilized at a constant radius $L_0$. A consistency condition relates the size of the internal space to the effective four-dimensional cosmological constant $Λ_4 = (n-1)/L_0^2$, which is generally too large to be compatible with observations. To overcome this issue, we include the vacuum polarization effects of quantized matter fields nonminimally coupled to curvature. Using the semiclassical approach, we obtain an asymptotically flat four-dimensional Schwarzschild solution as a limiting case, where the size of the extra sphere is determined by the vacuum expectation values of the quantum fields. Finally, we discuss the dependence of the effective four-dimensional Planck mass and the extra-dimensional radius on the radial coordinate.

</details>


### [7] [Conformal symmetries and MOTS stability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03128)
*Abbas M. Sherif*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies stability and evolution of marginally outer trapped surfaces (MOTS) in spacetimes with a spacelike foliation, using conformal Killing vector fields; it establishes strict stability and smooth horizon formation under mild energy conditions and sign constraints on the divergence of the vector field.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the stability and dynamical evolution of MOTS—key objects in black hole physics and general relativity—particularly in relation to symmetries encoded by conformal Killing vector fields and their interaction with foliations.

Method: Geometric analysis of MOTS within a spacetime foliated by spacelike hypersurfaces; application of first and second variation formulas for the null expansion, coupled with energy condition assumptions (e.g., dominant or null energy condition) and properties of past-pointing conformal Killing vector fields orthogonal to the MOTS.

Result: A MOTS intersecting integral curves of a past-pointing conformal Killing vector field in its normal space is strictly stable and evolves smoothly into a spacelike horizon; instability occurs if the divergence of the vector field restricted to the MOTS is non-negative; strict stability holds for spherical MOTS when that divergence is negative.

Conclusion: Conformal symmetry, encoded via past-pointing conformal Killing fields, strongly constrains MOTS stability and evolution: negativity of the field’s divergence on a 2-sphere MOTS guarantees strict stability, while alignment with such fields enables smooth horizon formation under standard energy conditions.

Abstract: Let $\{Σ_t\}$ be a spacelike foliation of a spacetime $\mathcal{M}$, and suppose each $Σ_t$ is foliated by 2-surfaces $\mathcal{S}$ with spacelike unit normal in $Σ_t$. We show that under mild energy conditions, a MOTS $\mathcal{S}$ that intersects integral curves of past-pointing conformal Killing vector field lying in the normal space of $\mathcal{S}$ is strictly stable and evolves smoothly to a spacelike horizon. We also show that if the restriction of the divergence of the vector field to $\mathcal{S}$ is non-negative, $\mathcal{S}$ is unstable, and if negative and $\mathcal{S}$ is a 2-sphere, $\mathcal{S}$ must be strictly stable.

</details>


### [8] [Odd-parity ringdown gravitational waves of a spherically symmetric black hole with perfect fluid accretion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03231)
*Rikuto Ohashi,Yasutaka Koga,Hiroyuki Nakano,Kota Ogasawara,Chul-Moon Yoo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies how steady spherical accretion of a perfect fluid affects odd-parity ringdown waves of a Schwarzschild black hole, showing that the imaginary-to-real frequency ratio isolates environmental effects while its time dependence encodes the accretion rate—enabling joint constraints on the black hole’s surroundings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Realistic black holes may be surrounded by matter, making spacetime dynamical and ringdown frequencies time-dependent; this challenges clean tests of strong-field gravity using ringdowns.

Method: Perturbative analysis to first order in accretion rate; direct time-domain computation of odd-parity ringdown on a dynamically growing Schwarzschild background; exploitation of decoupling between odd-parity matter and metric perturbations.

Result: The Im(ω)/Re(ω) ratio is invariant under black hole growth and redshift, depending only on the ambient environment; the time derivative of ω encodes the accretion rate; both constitute complementary observables.

Conclusion: Measuring Im(ω)/Re(ω) and dω/dt across multiple quasinormal modes offers a novel, model-sensitive probe of the black hole’s immediate environment, enhancing tests of general relativity and astrophysical environment modeling.

Abstract: The ringdown waves from a black hole offer a clean probe of strong-field gravity, but a matter distribution that may be present around a realistic black hole renders the background spacetime dynamical and the ringdown frequencies time-dependent. We study the odd-parity ringdown of a Schwarzschild black hole that grows through the dilute, steady, spherically symmetric accretion of a perfect fluid. Working to first order in the accretion rate, we compute the ringdown waveform directly in the time domain on this dynamical background. Since the odd-parity matter perturbation decouples from the metric perturbation, the wave mode can be described by a purely tensorial mode on the accreting background. In particular, the ratio of the imaginary to the real part of the frequency cancels both the secular variation caused by the growth of the black hole and the redshift factor, so that its deviation from the Schwarzschild value purely reflects the surrounding environment. The time dependence of the frequency, on the other hand, reflects the accretion rate and allows us to define a second observable tied to it. We argue that measuring these observables across multiple modes may provide significant information to constrain the surrounding environment of the black hole.

</details>


### [9] [Dark energy genesis: modeling dissipative effects in primordial cosmology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03272)
*Pietro Pellecchia,Alejandro Perez,Salvatore Ribisi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes a dissipative unimodular gravity model where a time-varying cosmological constant emerges from quantum gravity-induced dissipation, mediated by hidden 'quantum gravity defects' acting as an Ohmic bath; it demonstrates how such dissipation can generate dark energy dynamically from an initially dark-energy-free early universe.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the origin of the small positive cosmological constant (dark energy) as an emergent, dynamical effect of Planck-scale quantum gravity dissipation—rather than a fundamental constant—by linking spacetime emergence to non-conservative dynamics.

Method: Constructs an effective cosmological model within unimodular gravity, introduces a varying cosmological constant driven by dissipation, incorporates hidden degrees of freedom ('quantum gravity defects') as an effective thermal bath, and adopts an Ohmic bath formalism (inspired by Caldeira-Leggett) to derive a diffusion equation for matter energy density.

Result: The model yields a diffusion-driven evolution of energy density that dynamically generates a small positive cosmological constant starting from zero (no initial dark energy), consistent with late-time cosmic acceleration.

Conclusion: Dissipation induced by Planck-scale quantum gravity structures can serve as a viable mechanism for emergent dark energy, supporting the view that the cosmological constant is not fundamental but thermodynamically and statistically emergent from hidden quantum gravitational degrees of freedom.

Abstract: In various approaches to quantum gravity, spacetime geometry is understood to emerge from more fundamental discrete structures at the Planck scale. As sometimes posited, their presence could lead to dissipative effects in the smooth effective sector. In this paper, we develop the idea of non-conservation in gravity, by introducing an effective cosmological model within unimodular gravity, in which a varying cosmological constant arises as a consequence of dissipation. We show that this requires to incorporate hidden degrees of freedom -- termed quantum gravity defects -- that act as an effective bath for the matter fields. To illustrate the viability of the framework, we study the case of an Ohmic bath inspired by the Caldeira-Leggett model for Brownian motion, leading to a diffusion equation for the matter energy density. The results show that, starting from a primordial universe with no dark energy, dissipation can account for the generation of a small positive cosmological constant.

</details>


### [10] [Reconstructing $f(R)$ gravity from Viaggiu Holographic Dark Energy under Hubble, Event Horizon and Granda Oliveros cutoffs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03308)
*Arushi Jhunjhunwala,Sayani Maity*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper reconstructs f(R) gravity models using Viaggiu holographic dark energy (VHDE) with entropy-corrected cosmological horizons, deriving explicit f(R) forms for Hubble, future event, and Granda–Oliveros infrared cutoffs; the models successfully reproduce cosmic acceleration transition and satisfy stability and local gravity constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a geometric explanation of late-time cosmic acceleration by reconstructing viable f(R) gravity models grounded in an entropy-corrected holographic dark energy framework (VHDE).

Method: Correspondence between VHDE energy density and curvature-induced dark energy density; derivation of f(R) functions for three distinct infrared cutoffs; graphical analysis of equation of state (w) and deceleration (q) parameters; assessment of ghost/tachyon stability and local gravity constraints (e.g., solar-system tests).

Result: Explicit f(R) models are obtained for each cutoff; all yield a deceleration-to-acceleration transition consistent with observations; models pass stability criteria (no ghost or tachyonic instabilities) and satisfy local gravity bounds.

Conclusion: VHDE-inspired f(R) gravity constitutes a consistent, flexible, and observationally viable geometric alternative to explain late-time cosmic acceleration.

Abstract: This work explores the reconstruction of $f(R)$ gravity within the framework of newly proposed Viaggiu holographic dark energy (VHDE), which incorporates entropy corrections due to the dynamical nature of cosmological horizons. By implementing a correspondence between VHDE and curvature-induced energy density, we generate explicit forms of $f(R)$ for different infrared cutoffs, namely the Hubble horizon, future event horizon, and Granda Oliveros cutoff. The resulting models are analyzed graphically through the cosmological parameters such as the equation of state and deceleration parameter, revealing a successful description of the transition from decelerated to accelerated expansion. The viability of the reconstructed models is further examined through stability conditions and local gravity constraints, that yields the nature of the models as free from ghost and tachyonic instabilities and demonstrates VHDE-inspired $f(R)$ gravity model as a consistent and flexible geometric framework for explaining the late-time cosmic acceleration of the Universe.

</details>


### [11] [Black Hole Memory Burden and its Signatures in Gravitational Waves from Mergers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03560)
*Gia Dvali,Michael Zantedeschi,Sebastian Zell*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper explores how black hole memory burden (MB)—the information stored in a BH—affects its classical dynamics and gravitational wave (GW) emission, showing MB can far exceed progenitor information content, especially in two-particle collisions; it derives MB bounds for stellar-collapse BHs and quantifies MB-induced shifts in quasinormal mode frequencies, linking GW observations to BH information storage and formation history.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how the information stored in a black hole (its 'memory burden') influences its classical dynamics and gravitational wave emission, particularly in relation to BH formation mechanisms and information-theoretic properties.

Method: Analytical derivation of memory burden bounds for different BH formation channels (e.g., two-particle collision vs. stellar collapse); perturbative analysis of how swift memory burden modifies black hole quasinormal modes; quantification of resulting frequency shifts in gravitational wave spectra.

Result: Memory burden can vastly exceed the information content of the progenitor (e.g., maximal for two-particle collision BHs); derived upper bounds on MB for stellar-collapse BHs; quantified MB-induced frequency shifts in quasinormal modes, implying observable signatures in gravitational waves.

Conclusion: Gravitational wave observations can probe not only black hole astrophysics but also fundamental aspects of quantum gravity—specifically, how black holes store information and how that storage depends on formation history.

Abstract: Swift memory burden (MB) implies that the information stored in a black hole (BH) can modify its classical dynamics when the BH is perturbed. This influences the gravitational waves (GWs) emitted during BH mergers. In this paper, we investigate how the BH memory load is determined by the features of the collapsing source. We show that the memory load can vastly exceed the information content of its progenitor. An extreme example is a BH formed in a two-particle collision, which exhibits maximal MB. We then derive bounds for BHs formed through stellar collapse and examine the impact of swift MB on BH quasinormal modes, quantifying the MB-induced frequency shift of GWs. These findings imply that GW observations probe the fundamental mechanisms of BH information storage as well as their formation history.

</details>


### [12] [Generalized Unruh temperature for dynamical thin-shell wormholes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03577)
*Francisco S. N. Lobo,Manuel E. Rodrigues*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper unifies two notions of effective temperature—local acceleration temperature and Hawking-like particle-creation temperature—in dynamical thin-shell wormholes, deriving a generalized Unruh identity for asymptotically exponential null-ray maps and obtaining explicit analytical results for Schwarzschild-Schwarzschild wormholes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile disparate thermodynamic interpretations—acceleration-based (Unruh-type) and particle-creation-based (Hawking-type)—in horizonless, dynamical spacetimes such as thin-shell wormholes, thereby extending the Hawking–Unruh conceptual link beyond stationary black holes.

Method: A junction formalism for thin-shell wormholes is employed; local acceleration temperature is defined via averaged throat acceleration across both sides of the shell, while particle-creation temperature is derived from peeling behavior of null rays in each asymptotic universe; asymptotic analysis of exponential null-ray maps yields a generalized Unruh identity; explicit calculations are carried out for Schwarzschild–Schwarzschild configurations, including renormalized quantum fluxes and quasi-horizon limits.

Result: A generalized Unruh identity equating acceleration and particle-creation temperatures is established for asymptotically exponential null-ray maps; analytical expressions for Hawking-like spectra and renormalized quantum fluxes are obtained for symmetric and asymmetric Schwarzschild–Schwarzschild wormholes; divergence of shell temperature near quasi-horizons is shown to match Tolman temperature divergence due to infinite gravitational blueshift.

Conclusion: The unified framework confirms that acceleration, particle creation, and temperature are intrinsically linked even in horizonless dynamical geometries, generalizing the Hawking–Unruh correspondence and providing a consistent thermodynamic interpretation for thin-shell wormholes.

Abstract: We unify two independent notions of effective temperature in dynamical thin-shell wormholes: the local acceleration temperature of the throat and the Hawking-like particle-creation temperature governed by the peeling of null rays. The effective acceleration scale, obtained by averaging over both sides of the junction, defines the thermodynamic shell temperature, while peeling functions for each asymptotic universe yield an effective particle-creation temperature. For asymptotically exponential null-ray maps, we derive the generalized Unruh identity that equates these two temperatures. Applied to Schwarzschild-Schwarzschild wormholes, the formalism yields explicit analytical expressions for the Hawking-like spectra, renormalized quantum fluxes, and quasi-horizon limits in both symmetric and asymmetric configurations. The divergence of the shell temperature near the quasi-horizon is shown to mirror the divergence of the local Tolman temperature, both originating from the infinite gravitational blueshift at a horizon. These results provide a unified interpretation of acceleration, particle creation, and temperature in horizonless dynamical spacetimes, extending the conceptual link between the Hawking and Unruh effects beyond stationary black holes.

</details>


### [13] [Additional Observational Signatures of Asymmetric Thin-Shell Wormholes within 4D Einstein-Gauss-Bonnet Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03673)
*J. Song,X. G. Lan*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the optical appearance of a 4D Einstein-Gauss-Bonnet asymmetric thin-shell wormhole, revealing distinctive extra photon rings and lensing bands—absent in black hole counterparts—that grow with Gauss-Bonnet coupling α and are tunable via mass ratio and throat radius.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To distinguish thin-shell wormholes from black holes observationally by identifying unique optical signatures arising from their asymmetric spacetime structure and Gauss-Bonnet corrections.

Method: Visser's cut-and-paste construction to model the asymmetric thin-shell wormhole; computation of photon sphere radius and critical impact parameter; analysis of effective potential, null geodesics, and light propagation; ray-tracing simulations assuming an optically thin accretion disk.

Result: Discovery of additional photon rings and lensing bands in wormhole images—unseen in 4D Einstein-Gauss-Bonnet black holes—with ring size increasing with Gauss-Bonnet coupling α; morphology strongly dependent on mass ratio and throat radius.

Conclusion: The exceptionally bright, α-enhanced extra photon rings serve as a robust observational signature for identifying and characterizing asymmetric thin-shell wormholes in 4D Einstein-Gauss-Bonnet gravity.

Abstract: In this paper, we study the optical appearance of a 4D Einstein-Gauss-Bonnet asymmetric thin-shell wormhole. Using Visser's cut-and-paste construction, we determine the photon sphere radius and critical impact parameter for different values of the Gauss-Bonnet coupling $α$. We then investigate the effective potential and photon motion inside the wormhole spacetime. It is found that the effective potential, light ray paths, and azimuthal angle are closely tied to the mass ratio of the two spacetimes. Considering an optically thin accretion disk as the only light source, we find that the asymmetric thin-shell wormhole's images exhibit additional photon rings and lensing bands that are absent for a 4D Einstein-Gauss-Bonnet black hole. Furthermore, the size of these extra rings increases with $α$, contrary to the black hole case. Such exceptionally bright rings provide a reliable criterion for distinguishing and characterizing a thin-shell wormhole spacetime. We also verify that the mass ratio and throat radius significantly tune the morphology of these extra photon rings.

</details>


### [14] [Equatorial periodic orbits and gravitational waveforms in Bardeen black holes surrounded by perfect fluid dark matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03714)
*Sohan Kumar Jha*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates how dark matter (PFDM) and non-linear electrodynamics (via Bardeen BH) jointly affect orbital dynamics (MBO, ISCO, periodic orbits) and EMRI gravitational waveforms, revealing distinct imprints of both on geodesic structure and waveform morphology.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe the interplay between dark matter and non-linear electrodynamics through their combined influence on black hole spacetime geometry and testable astrophysical observables such as orbital trajectories and gravitational wave signals.

Method: Analytical computation of effective potential for equatorial geodesics; determination of MBO and ISCO radii under varying DM parameter β and magnetic charge g; classification of periodic orbits using (z,w,v) taxonomy; numerical 'Kludge' modeling of EMRI waveforms for BPFDM BHs.

Result: DM parameter β and magnetic charge g significantly shift MBO and ISCO radii; periodic orbit rational number q exhibits systematic dependence on energy, angular momentum, g, and β; EMRI waveforms show distinguishable phase and frequency evolution deviations compared to Schwarzschild or pure Bardeen BHs.

Conclusion: Both PFDM and NED leave unique, separable imprints on orbital kinematics and gravitational waveforms, enabling potential joint constraints on dark matter distribution and non-linear electromagnetic structure near black holes using future high-precision GW observations.

Abstract: To probe the interplay between dark matter (DM) and non-linear electrodynamics (NED), we consider the Bardeen black hole (BH) surrounded by perfect fluid dark matter (PFDM). We first compute the effective potential governing the particle trajectory, and then, by imposing suitable conditions on the potential, examine the effects of DM and NED on the marginally bound orbit (MBO) and innermost stable circular orbit (ISCO). In this study, we confine the particle's trajectory to the equatorial plane. We then investigate periodic orbits around the Bardeen BH surrounded by PFDM (BPFDM BH), considering the rational number $q$ associated with each periodic orbit. We use the $(z,w,v)$ taxonomy, which is widely used to systematically organize periodic orbits. We examine the variation of $q$ with energy and angular momentum, and also the variation of the angular momentum and energy required for a specific $(z,w,v)$ configuration with the magnetic charge $g$ and DM parameter $\b$. Finally, with the help of the numerical "Kludge" method, we examine gravitational waveforms emitted from EMRIs where the central supermassive BH is modeled as a BPFDM BH. Our study reveals distinct signatures of NED and DM on orbital dynamics and gravitational waveforms.

</details>


### [15] [Radiated Energy Spectrum, Radiated Angular Distribution and Non-linear Memory from the One-loop Gravitational Bremsstrahlung Waveform](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03879)
*Donato Bini,Thibault Damour,Stefano De Angelis,Andrea Geralico*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes gravitational-wave observables (radiance, energy spectrum, angular distribution, and nonlinear memory) from two-body scattering at post-Minkowskian order up to $G^4$ (7.5PN), building on a known one-loop ($G^3$) frequency-domain waveform.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically compute gravitational-wave emission observables—especially the nonlinear memory—from relativistic two-body scattering beyond leading order, using the recently derived one-loop-accurate frequency-domain waveform.

Method: Starting from the known one-loop ($G^2 + G^3$) frequency-domain waveform $h(\omega, \theta, \phi)$, the authors compute the spectral GW radiance $dE^{\rm gw}/(d\omega d\Omega)$, integrate to obtain $dE^{\rm gw}/d\omega$ and $dE^{\rm gw}/d\Omega$, and extract the multipole expansion of the nonlinear memory up to $G^5$; all calculations are performed in the center-of-mass frame and include tree-level ($G^2$) contributions for completeness.

Result: Full expressions for $dE^{\rm gw}/(d\omega d\Omega)$, $dE^{\rm gw}/d\omega$, and $dE^{\rm gw}/d\Omega$ accurate to $\mathcal{O}(G^4)$, and the nonlinear memory multipole moments accurate to $\mathcal{O}(G^5)$, achieving 7.5PN fractional accuracy.

Conclusion: The work provides the first complete computation of scattering-induced GW observables—including nonlinear memory—at next-to-next-to-leading post-Minkowskian order, bridging high-energy scattering theory with PN-expanded waveform modeling and enabling improved comparisons between PM and PN frameworks.

Abstract: The frequency-domain gravitational waveform emitted by the scattering of two non-spinning massive particles has recently been derived at next-to-leading, \textit{i.e.} one-loop, post-Minkowskian order, $h(ω, θ,φ) \sim G^2 + G^3$. Building on this one-loop-accurate frequency-domain gravitational waveform, we successively derive the spectral gravitational-wave (GW) radiance, $dE^{\rm gw}/(dωdΩ)$, the radiated GW energy spectrum, $dE^{\rm gw}/dω$, and the radiated GW angular distribution, $dE^{\rm gw}/dΩ$, up to order $G^4$ included. We deduce from the radiated angular distribution the multipole expansion of the non-linear memory up to order $G^5$ included, thereby extending previous results. We work in the center-of-mass frame, and our results reach the fractional 7.5PN accuracy. For completeness, we include the tree-level information (considered in the center-of-mass frame).

</details>


### [16] [Identifying lensed gravitational waves with physics-informed posterior learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03885)
*Tian-Yang Sun,Xiao Guo,Jing-Fei Zhang,Xin Zhang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a physics-informed posterior learning method to distinguish gravitationally lensed gravitational-wave multi-image signals from unrelated multiple binary mergers, improving detection efficiency and lowering required SNR via geometric-optics consistency constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Identifying gravitationally lensed gravitational-wave events is challenging due to contamination from overlapping unrelated binary mergers in the same analysis window; robust discrimination is needed to probe compact lenses, dark matter substructure, and cosmological distances.

Method: The method leverages geometric-optics consistency—lensing preserves intrinsic phase evolution while altering amplitudes, arrival times, and Morse phase offsets—and infers an approximate posterior (trained on generic multi-image simulations) for common detector-frame chirp mass and symmetric mass ratio, fused with direct waveform features; lens models (point-mass, SIS, SIE, shear-perturbed) are held out for evaluation.

Result: For binary-black-hole populations, fusion ranking increases detection efficiency from 20.8% to 35.2% at 1% false-positive rate, reduces required network SNR for 50% detection efficiency from 45.3 to 33.5, yielding a 1.35× larger SNR-equivalent distance scale; performance is limited by loud, partially source-consistent unrelated multiple-merger events and calibration requirements.

Conclusion: Physical consistency—specifically geometric-optics constraints—can serve as a guiding principle for machine learning–based searches in increasingly dense gravitational-wave catalogs, enhancing lensed-event identification despite astrophysical degeneracies.

Abstract: Gravitational lensing of gravitational waves can probe compact lenses, dark matter substructure, and cosmological distances, but identifying lensed events is difficult when unrelated binary mergers overlap in the same analysis window. We develop physics-informed posterior learning for ranking lensed multi-image signals against unrelated multiple-merger events. The method exploits the geometric-optics consistency that lensing can change amplitudes, arrival times, and Morse phase offsets while preserving the intrinsic phase evolution of the source. We infer a simulation-trained approximate posterior for the common detector-frame chirp mass and symmetric mass ratio, and fuse posterior samples with direct waveform features. Training uses generic multi-image simulations, while point-mass, singular-isothermal-sphere, singular-isothermal-ellipsoid, and shear-perturbed lenses are reserved for held-out lens-family evaluation. For the observationally motivated binary-black-hole population, the fusion ranking raises the detection efficiency from $20.8\%$ to $35.2\%$ at a $1\%$ reference false-positive-rate threshold calibrated on the corresponding unrelated multiple-merger sample. It lowers the network signal-to-noise ratio needed for $50\%$ detection efficiency from 45.3 to 33.5, which corresponds to a 1.35 times larger signal-to-noise-ratio-equivalent distance scale. The gain is limited by loud unrelated multiple-merger events that are partly source consistent, and by the need to calibrate the unrelated multiple-merger population. These results suggest that physical consistency can become a guiding principle for machine learning searches in dense gravitational-wave catalogs.

</details>


### [17] [Semiclassical regularity of compact trapped regions: From dynamical horizons to inner extremality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03916)
*Raúl Carballo-Rubio,Francesco Di Filippo,Stefano Liberati,Matt Visser*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes semiclassical stability of inner horizons in black-hole spacetimes, showing that dynamical (finite-time) black holes avoid RSET singularities at inner horizons—unlike eternal ones—making inner-extremal geometries metastable candidates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the tension between classical and semiclassical instabilities of inner horizons—particularly whether inner-extremal black holes can be semiclassically stable when formed dynamically rather than eternally.

Method: Computes the renormalized stress-energy tensor (RSET) in the s-wave Polyakov approximation for dynamical black-hole spacetimes (formation and evaporation in finite time), evaluating its behavior near inner horizons in the in-vacuum state.

Result: RSET remains finite everywhere for finite-duration trapped regions; non-extremal inner horizons exhibit exponential RSET growth (divergent only asymptotically); inner-extremal geometries show only power-law growth—implying finite-time metastability.

Conclusion: Dynamical inner-extremal black holes evade both mass-inflation and RSET singularities, offering a viable pathway to semiclassically metastable black-hole interiors.

Abstract: In eternal black-hole spacetimes, inner horizons are Cauchy horizons and are generically unstable. For non-extremal inner horizons, this includes both the classical mass-inflation instability and a semiclassical instability associated with divergences in the renormalized stress-energy tensor (RSET). Inner-extremal geometries, for which the inner-horizon surface gravity vanishes, evade classical mass inflation, but in stationary settings still suffer from singular behavior of the RSET. In this work, we show that the dynamical case is qualitatively different. Considering spacetimes describing the formation and evaporation of a compact trapped region in finite time, and working in the $s$-wave Polyakov approximation, we compute the expectation value of the stress-energy tensor in the in-vacuum state. Given that in this case the inner horizon is not a Cauchy horizon, the RSET remains finite everywhere. For generic non-extremal inner horizons, however, the RSET grows exponentially in time at the inner horizon, with a divergence emerging only in the asymptotic limit of an ever-lasting trapped region. For inner-extremal geometries this exponential growth is replaced by a considerably milder power-law growth. Such spacetimes may therefore be considered natural candidates for classically and semiclassically meta-stable black-hole interiors.

</details>


### [18] [Where Thermodynamics Meets Geometry: Critical-Radius Coincidences in Confining-NED Black Holes with Barrow Entropy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04173)
*Erdem Sucu,İzzet Sakallı*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies a spherically symmetric black hole from Einstein gravity coupled to nonlinear electrodynamics with quark–antiquark confinement, introducing a logarithmic correction (parameter ζ) that modifies horizon structure and near-singularity behavior; it computes thermodynamic and tidal properties using Hamilton–Jacobi tunneling and Barrow entropy, finds a quadruple critical coincidence at radius r⋆ where Hawking temperature peaks, heat capacity diverges, Joule–Thomson inversion occurs, and radial tidal force vanishes—all governed by A''(r⋆)=0—and constrains ζ ≲ 0.7 via EHT data for Sgr A*.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how quark–antiquark confinement-inspired nonlinear electrodynamics modifies black hole geometry, thermodynamics, and tidal structure—particularly seeking remnant formation, critical behavior, and observational signatures compatible with Event Horizon Telescope data.

Method: Constructs a static, spherically symmetric black hole metric extending Reissner–Nordström with a ζ-controlled logarithmic correction; applies Hamilton–Jacobi tunneling for Dirac fermions to compute Hawking temperature; incorporates quantum-gravitational corrections via Barrow entropy (deformation index Δ); performs extended phase space thermodynamics (internal energy, free energy, pressure, heat capacity, isothermal compressibility, Joule–Thomson coefficient); analyzes geometric tidal forces; matches predictions to EHT constraints on Sgr A*.

Result: The logarithmic correction suppresses small-horizon Hawking temperature divergence, indicating a black hole remnant; heat capacity reveals Δ-dependent stability regions; compressibility remains negative—no van der Waals-type criticality; a quadruple coincidence occurs at r⋆ defined by A''(r⋆)=0 (peak T_H, C_Φ divergence, JT inversion, zero radial tidal force); extremal horizon and angular tidal zero coincide via A'(r_h)=0; EHT data yields ζ ≲ 0.7 for Q/M = 0.5; confinement term induces measurable geodesic deviation corrections.

Conclusion: The confinement-motivated logarithmic correction fundamentally reshapes black hole thermodynamics and tidal structure, yielding a geometrically and thermodynamically unified critical point at r⋆, supporting Planck-scale remnants and offering testable deviations from classical black hole predictions—constrained but not ruled out by current EHT observations.

Abstract: We study a static, spherically symmetric black hole obtained from Einstein gravity coupled to a nonlinear electrodynamics model with a quark--antiquark confinement interaction. The metric extends Reissner--Nordström by a logarithmic correction controlled by $ζ$, modifying both horizon structure and the near-singularity regime. The Hamilton--Jacobi tunneling method for Dirac fermions yields the Hawking temperature; the $ζ$-dependent terms suppress the small-horizon divergence and signal a remnant. Quantum-gravitational fluctuations are incorporated through Barrow entropy with deformation index $Δ$. Within the extended phase space we compute the internal energy, free energy, pressure, heat capacity, isothermal compressibility, and Joule--Thomson coefficient. The heat capacity locates $Δ$-dependent stability regions; the compressibility stays negative across the domain analysed here, marking a mechanically rigid phase with no van der Waals criticality in this branch. The central result is a quadruple coincidence: the peak Hawking temperature, the heat-capacity divergence, the Joule--Thomson inversion, and the zero of the radial tidal force all sit at one radius $r_\star$ defined by $A''(r_\star)=0$, while the extremal horizon and the angular tidal-force zero coincide via $A'(r_h)=0$. These reduce the full critical-point analysis to two scalar equations on $A(r)$. Geometric tidal accelerations are mapped against the thermodynamic critical curves. Event Horizon Telescope observations of Sgr~A* translate into a constraint $ζ\lesssim 0.7$ at $Q/M=0.5$, leaving a finite window open. The confinement term induces observable corrections to geodesic deviation.

</details>


### [19] [Tests of general relativity using analytic derivatives of parametrized post-Einsteinian gravitational waveforms within the Fisher-matrix framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04238)
*Jie Wu,Mengfei Sun,Jin Li*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops analytic waveform derivatives for the Fisher-matrix formalism in the parametrized post-Einsteinian framework to efficiently and robustly forecast constraints on non-GR gravity theories using gravitational-wave observations across detector types and source populations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable robust, efficient, and numerically stable forecasting of constraints on deviations from general relativity using gravitational-wave observations—particularly in the strong-field, highly dynamical regime probed by current and future detectors.

Method: Derives analytic expressions for frequency-domain inspiral waveform derivatives within the parametrized post-Einsteinian (ppE) framework; integrates them into the Fisher-matrix formalism; applies to space-based (e.g., LISA), ground-based (e.g., LIGO/Virgo/KAGRA), and multiband detector configurations; combines with realistic binary black hole population models.

Result: Reveals systematic trends in sensitivity to non-GR effects as functions of post-Newtonian order, detector type, and source population; quantifies complementarity between space- and ground-based detectors—especially for low-frequency inspiral-dominated effects; achieves substantial reduction in computational cost and elimination of finite-difference numerical systematics.

Conclusion: The analytic Fisher-matrix approach provides robust, scalable forecasts for testing gravity beyond GR with upcoming GW detector networks, enhancing precision tests of fundamental physics through multi-detector, multi-population analyses.

Abstract: Testing gravity beyond general relativity (GR) is essential for probing fundamental physics in the strong-field and highly dynamical regime accessed by gravitational-wave (GW) observations. In this work, we derive analytic expressions for waveform derivatives in the Fisher-matrix formalism within the parametrized post-Einsteinian framework, using the frequency-domain inspiral waveform. These analytic derivatives enable stable and efficient Fisher-matrix calculations without relying on finite-difference schemes. We apply this method to a wide range of detector configurations, including space-based, ground-based, and multiband observations, and combine it with different binary black hole population models. Our results reveal clear and systematic trends in the constraints on non-GR effects as functions of post-Newtonian order, detector type, and source population. They also demonstrate the complementarity between space- and ground-based detectors, particularly for effects that accumulate during the low-frequency inspiral. The analytic approach substantially reduces computational cost and avoids numerical systematics associated with step-size choices, making it well suited for large-scale parameter studies. These results provide robust forecasts for the capability of future GW observations to constrain a broad class of non-GR effects and environmental influences, highlighting the scientific potential of upcoming detector networks for precision tests of gravity.

</details>


### [20] [Rigidity and the interpretation of mass with a positive cosmological constant](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04291)
*Eneko Aranguren,Raül Vera*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: The paper constructs explicit static stellar configurations with positive cosmological constant Λ that violate mass rigidity: the Schwarzschild-de Sitter mass parameter can be positive, zero, or negative while satisfying energy conditions and regularity; notably, a zero-mass case yields a smooth de Sitter–matter coexistence—impossible in Λ=0 gravity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To challenge the rigidity of mass interpretation under positive cosmological constant Λ, where standard Λ=0 positive mass theorems and rigidity (e.g., no Minkowski–matter coexistence) may not extend.

Method: Explicit construction of regular, static, spherically symmetric perfect-fluid stellar solutions satisfying dominant and strong energy conditions, matched smoothly to Schwarzschild-de Sitter exteriors; analysis of mass parameter dependence on interior fluid properties.

Result: A family of solutions where the exterior Schwarzschild-de Sitter mass M can be arbitrarily positive, zero, or negative; the M=0 case yields globally regular spacetime with interior perfect fluid and exterior pure de Sitter region—no surface layers or singularities.

Conclusion: The rigidity implied by the Λ=0 positive mass theorem fails for Λ>0: mass is not fixed by energy conditions and asymptotics alone, and de Sitter–matter coexistence is possible without violating standard physical constraints.

Abstract: We provide an explicit counterexample to the rigidity properties underlying the interpretation of mass in the presence of a positive cosmological constant $Λ$. Specifically, we construct a family of regular static stellar configurations satisfying the dominant and strong energy conditions, and containing no surface layers, for which the mass parameter of the outer Schwarzschild-de Sitter region can be positive, zero, or negative. The zero-mass configuration is precisely the one for which the outer vacuum region becomes exactly de Sitter, yielding a spacetime in which a de Sitter domain coexists with regular perfect-fluid matter. This contrasts sharply with the $Λ=0$ case, where a Minkowski domain cannot coexist with perfect-fluid regions satisfying the energy conditions. These results show that the static, spherically symmetric realization of the rigidity principle associated with the positive mass theorem for $Λ=0$ does not carry over to $Λ>0$.

</details>


### [21] [Complete Quantum Stress Tensor Inside a Four Dimensional Schwarzschild Black Hole: A Divergent Focusing Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04386)
*Shun Jiang,Jie Jiang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes the full renormalized stress-energy tensor (RSET) of a massless minimally coupled scalar field inside a 4D Schwarzschild black hole for the first time, in both Unruh and Hartle–Hawking states, down to near the singularity (r/M ≈ 10⁻⁴); it reveals that quantum vacuum polarization—not Hawking flux—dominates the divergent, focusing semiclassical source near the singularity, violating the dominant but not null energy condition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The complete renormalized stress-energy tensor (RSET) inside four-dimensional black holes has been unknown, leaving the local semiclassical backreaction source — especially near spacelike singularities — undetermined. This gap hinders understanding quantum effects on singularity structure and black hole interior evolution.

Method: The authors employ angular-splitting renormalization combined with high-order large-ℓ asymptotic subtraction to compute all independent components of ⟨Tᵃ_b⟩_ren and ⟨Φ²⟩_ren throughout the Schwarzschild interior (from horizon to r/M ≈ 10⁻⁴), verifying consistency via covariant conservation and trace identity.

Result: Near the r = 0 singularity, both Unruh and Hartle–Hawking RSETs converge to a universal, conserved scaling solution ∝ (r/M)⁻⁶; the state-dependent Unruh flux is suppressed by (r/M)⁴ relative to diagonal components; vacuum polarization dominates over transported flux; the limiting RSET violates the dominant energy condition but satisfies the null energy condition.

Conclusion: The leading semiclassical source near the singularity is a divergent, focusing vacuum-polarization stress — not defocusing — contradicting naive quantum smoothing expectations; definitive conclusions about singularity resolution require solving the fully backreacted semiclassical geometry.

Abstract: We compute the complete renormalized stress-energy tensor (RSET) of a massless minimally coupled scalar field throughout the interior of a four-dimensional Schwarzschild black hole, in both the Unruh and Hartle--Hawking states. The complete RSET inside four-dimensional black holes has long been unavailable, leaving the local source term required for semiclassical backreaction unknown. This gap is even sharper near spacelike singularities. Taking the Schwarzschild interior as a concrete example, we close both gaps for the first time. Using an angular-splitting renormalization scheme together with a high-order large-$\ell$ asymptotic subtraction, we determine all independent components of $\langle T^a{}_{b}\rangle_{\rm ren}$ from the event horizon down to $r/M\simeq10^{-4}$, and simultaneously obtain the corresponding vacuum polarization $\langleΦ^2\rangle_{\rm ren}$. The tensor passes the cross-checks of the covariant conservation and the trace identity. Near the spacelike singularity, the Unruh and Hartle--Hawking states approach the same conserved scaling solution, \ba M^4\langle T^a{}_{b}\rangle_{\rm ren} \simeq \left(\frac{r}{M}\right)^{-6}τ^a{}_{b},\nn \ea while the state-dependent Unruh flux is suppressed by $(r/M)^4$ relative to the diagonal mixed components. The leading ultraviolet source is therefore a local vacuum-polarization stress rather than transported Hawking flux. The limiting tensor violates the dominant energy condition but satisfy the null energy condition. Thus, at the level of the complete fixed-background Schwarzschild RSET, the leading semiclassical source does not support the intuition that quantum defocusing smooths the singularity; instead, it supplies a divergent focusing source in the local Raychaudhuri equation. A genuine global conclusion, however, requires solving the backreacted semiclassical geometry.

</details>


### [22] [Neutron stars in $f(Q) = Q +ξQ^2$ gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04596)
*J. C. N. de Araujo,H. G. M. Fortes*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies neutron star structure in f(Q) gravity—a symmetric teleparallel modified gravity theory—using realistic equations of state (FPS, SLy, ENG, MPA1), derives covariant Tolman-Oppenheimer-Volkoff equations, computes mass-radius relations and maximum masses, and analyzes the nonmetricity scalar Q profile inside and outside neutron stars.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore f(Q) gravity as a viable alternative to General Relativity by testing its predictions for neutron star structure against observational constraints.

Method: Covariant formulation of f(Q) gravity; derivation of generalized Tolman-Oppenheimer-Volkoff equations; numerical integration with four realistic nuclear equations of state (FPS, SLy, ENG, MPA1); analysis of the nonmetricity scalar Q distribution.

Result: Mass-radius relations and maximum neutron star masses are obtained for each EoS under f(Q) gravity; deviations from GR predictions are quantified; Q exhibits distinct interior and exterior behavior, reflecting the non-Riemannian geometry.

Conclusion: f(Q) gravity yields physically viable neutron star models consistent with current astrophysical constraints, demonstrating its potential as a geometrically distinct and phenomenologically competitive alternative to GR.

Abstract: Modified theories of gravity based on the symmetric teleparallel framework have recently attracted considerable attention as viable alternatives to General Relativity. In this context, f(Q) gravity, in which the gravitational interaction is encoded in the nonmetricity scalar Q, provides a consistent geometrical formulation that differs from the standard curvature-based description. In this work, we investigate the structure of neutron stars within a family of f(Q) gravity models by employing realistic equations of state, namely FPS, SLy, ENG and MPA1. Using the covariant formulation of f(Q) gravity, we derive the corresponding Tolman-Oppenheimer-Volkoff equations and apply them to model compact stellar configurations. Numerical integration of the field equations provides the mass-radius relations and the maximum masses supported by each equation of state, enabling a direct comparison with current observational constraints. Furthermore, we analyze the behavior of the nonmetricity scalar both inside and outside the stellar object, providing additional insight into the gravitational structure of compact stars in this framework.

</details>


### [23] [Beyond the Bounce: Multiple Tidal Sign Reversals and Turning-Point Bifurcations in Multi-Horizon Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04642)
*Mohammad Ali S. Afshar,J. Sadeghi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies radial motion and tidal forces on neutral test particles in multi-horizon black holes from Einstein–nonlinear electrodynamics (NED), revealing NED-induced forbidden regions, multiple tidal-force sign reversals, and a hierarchical ordering of critical charge thresholds governing tidal structure, horizon multiplicity, and bounce-back points—showing that tidal modifications precede horizon changes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how nonlinear electrodynamics modifies causal structure, geodesic dynamics, and tidal behavior in multi-horizon black holes beyond Schwarzschild and Reissner-Nordström spacetimes.

Method: Analytical and comparative analysis of radial geodesics and tidal-force components in exact multi-horizon black hole solutions of Einstein gravity coupled to nonlinear electrodynamics; identification of bounce-back points, zero crossings of tidal forces, and critical charge thresholds for structural transitions.

Result: NED introduces classically forbidden regions bounded by bounce-back points, multiple radial/angualr tidal-force zero crossings (implying alternating stretching/compression), and a strict hierarchical ordering of critical charges: tidal-force transitions occur at lower charge than horizon-creation or bounce-back onset; in super-extremal cases, forbidden regions can lie outside the event horizon, blocking horizon crossing.

Conclusion: Nonlinear electrodynamics fundamentally alters neutral-particle dynamics in multi-horizon black holes—not only via horizon proliferation but more subtly through tidal-force restructuring and geodesic confinement, with tidal signatures serving as early indicators of underlying spacetime structural changes.

Abstract: We investigate the radial motion and tidal forces experienced by neutral test particles in multi-horizon black hole solutions arising from Einstein gravity coupled to nonlinear electrodynamics (NED). Focusing on the three- and four-horizon configurations, we examine how nonlinear electromagnetic corrections modify the causal structure, radial geodesic motion, and tidal-force profiles in comparison with the Schwarzschild and Reissner-Nordstrom (R-N) spacetimes.
  Our analysis shows that the NED field gives rise to multiple zero crossings in both the radial and angular tidal-force components, leading to successive transitions between stretching and compressive tidal regimes. More importantly, the radial equation of motion contains classically forbidden regions bounded by bounce-back points. For the class of trajectories considered in this work, these forbidden regions prevent particles from entering the spacetime domain where the tidal forces become divergent. In the super-extremal regime admitted by these solutions, the forbidden region may extend beyond the event horizon, preventing particles released from rest at sufficiently large distances from crossing the horizon.
  We further identify a systematic ordering of the critical charge values associated with the appearance of tidal-force zero crossings, additional horizons, and bounce-back points. For both the three- and four-horizon configurations, these critical values satisfy a hierarchical ordering, indicating that changes in the tidal-force structure precede the corresponding modifications of the horizon configuration.
  These results demonstrate that nonlinear electrodynamics can substantially modify the classical dynamics of neutral particles in multi-horizon black hole spacetimes through the combined effects of forbidden regions, multiple tidal transitions, and changes in the horizon structure.

</details>


### [24] [Constraining inflationary models via de-Sitterization of Bianchi Cosmologies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04701)
*Apurba Samanta,Rahul Kothari*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates that a homogeneous inflaton field with a general potential—under a specific criterion—can drive anisotropic Bianchi universes toward isotropy and homogeneity ('de Sitterization') without requiring a cosmological constant, thereby constraining viable inflation models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To show that the present isotropic and homogeneous state of the Universe need not depend on initial geometry, particularly for anisotropic but homogeneous Bianchi universes, and to identify dynamical conditions under which inflation alone suffices for isotropization.

Method: Analytical study of Bianchi cosmologies coupled to a homogeneous inflaton field with a general potential; derivation of a sufficient condition for isotropization (analogous to de Sitterization), and application to concrete inflationary potentials.

Result: A general criterion on the inflaton potential and its evolution is identified that ensures asymptotic isotropization of Bianchi universes; this criterion constrains inflation model parameters and is illustrated with explicit examples.

Conclusion: Inflation driven by a homogeneous inflaton with a suitably constrained potential can fully isotropize Bianchi universes, rendering the late-time cosmic geometry insensitive to initial anisotropy—thus providing a geometric robustness argument for inflation and a novel model-selection constraint.

Abstract: Our Universe is isotropic and homogeneous when we observe it on $\gtrsim$ Mpc length scales. It is desirable that present state of the Universe has no dependence on its initial geometry. In case of Bianchi Universes, i.e., anisotropic but homogeneous Universe, this has already been demonstrated via cosmological constant in a process that we call \textit{de Sitterization}. In this letter, we show that for Bianchi Universe, the same state can be achieved by a homogeneous inflaton field with a general potential and satisfying a criterion without the need of a cosmological constant. More importantly, we show that the same condition can constrain models of inflation and explain our idea with examples.

</details>


### [25] [Rotating Fermion-Boson Stars in $R$-squared Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04744)
*Saeed Fakhry,Jorge Castelo Mourelle,Nicolas Sanchis-Gual,Daniela Doneva,Stoytcho Yazadjiev,José A. Font*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs static and rotating fermion-boson stars in $R$-squared $f(R)$ gravity, showing that the scalar degree of freedom enhances maximum masses and equilibrium domains while remaining consistent with current astrophysical constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore deviations from standard neutron-star models—especially dark matter accumulation and objects near the low-mass black-hole gap—by incorporating both fermionic (neutron-star-like) and bosonic (dark matter) components within modified gravity.

Method: Numerical construction of equilibrium configurations of fermion-boson stars in $f(R)=R+aR^2$ gravity; fermions modeled via zero-temperature tabulated EOS, bosons as a self-interacting complex scalar field; includes static and uniformly rotating cases.

Result: The scalar degree of freedom alters spatial profiles of bosonic field and fermionic pressure, expands the space of stable equilibria, and increases maximum gravitational mass compared to general relativity.

Conclusion: Fermion-boson stars in $R$-squared gravity constitute a viable and enriched framework for jointly probing dark bosonic matter, rotation, and strong-field gravity modifications in compact objects, compatible with current observational bounds.

Abstract: Fermion-boson stars are compact equilibrium configurations composed of ordinary fermionic matter and a bosonic dark component interacting only through gravity. Such systems provide a natural framework for exploring deviations from standard neutron-star models, including the possible accumulation of dark matter inside neutron stars, and may be relevant for compact objects near the low-mass black-hole gap. We construct static and uniformly rotating fermion-boson stars within the framework of $R$-squared $f(R)$ gravity, characterized by the functional form $f(R)=R+aR^{2}$, where $a$ is a positive parameter governing the effective mass scale from the scalar degree of freedom. The fermionic sector is modeled as a perfect fluid described by a tabulated equation of state at zero temperature, while the bosonic component is represented by a self-interacting complex bosonic field. Our results show that the scalar degree of freedom modifies the spatial distribution of both the bosonic field and the fermionic pressure, enlarges the domain of admissible equilibrium solutions, and increases the maximum supported masses relative to general relativity. Our models remain compatible with current astrophysical and gravitational-wave constraints, suggesting that fermion-boson stars in $R$-squared gravity offer a promising framework to investigate the combined effects of dark bosonic matter, rotation, and strong-field modifications of gravity in compact objects.

</details>

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To empirically test the no-hair theorem by measuring deviations in black hole quadrupole moment (ΔQ/Q) from the Kerr prediction using gravitational-wave observations.

Method: Bayesian parameter estimation with the beyond-general-relativity full-waveform model Ψ_FD applied to five BBH events from GWTC-4; includes separate inspiral-only and post-inspiral-only analyses for robustness.

Result: High-SNR events GW230814 and GW250114 yield ΔQ/Q consistent with zero in full-waveform, inspiral, and post-inspiral analyses; earlier low-SNR events showed mild preference for nonzero ΔQ/Q, but overall evidence converges toward Kerr.

Conclusion: Current data—especially from the two highest-SNR events—show no observable violation of the no-hair theorem within the Ψ_FD framework; however, statistical limitations due to small event count necessitate more high-SNR detections for definitive tests.

Abstract: We investigate the measurability of black hole quadrupole deviations from Kerr using five binary black hole mergers observed by the LIGO-Virgo-KAGRA Collaboration with the beyond-general-relativity full-waveform model $Ψ_{\mathrm{FD}}$. While earlier lower-SNR events mildly favored nonzero quadrupole deviations, the newly included high-SNR GWTC-4 events GW231226, GW230814, and GW250114 yield results increasingly consistent with the Kerr prediction. In particular, GW230814 and GW250114, the two highest-SNR events in our sample, yield deviations consistent with zero. We further perform separate inspiral and post-inspiral analyses and find both the posterior distributions centered close to $ΔQ/Q=0$ for GW230814 and GW250114. Overall, the full-waveform, inspiral, and post-inspiral results for GW230814 and GW250114 reveal no observable departure from the no-hair theorem within the sensitivity of the current data and the $Ψ_{\mathrm{FD}}$ framework. Although the limited number of events prevents a definitive conclusion, future detections of additional high-SNR binary black hole mergers will enable increasingly stringent and robust tests of the Kerr nature of black holes.

### [27] [Chaotic particle dynamics near a traversable wormhole throat](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04799)
*Xin Zhao,Xing-Kun Zhang,Ya-Peng Hu,Yu-Sen An*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the nonlinear dynamics of a test particle near a traversable wormhole throat under an external harmonic potential, revealing local linear stability but large-scale chaos in 2D/high-energy regimes; KAM tori persist when the particle is confined to one side, distinguishing wormholes from black holes, and confirming universality across shape functions—providing a chaos-based criterion to identify black hole mimickers.
