  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To distinguish traversable wormholes from black holes using nonlinear dynamical signatures, especially in strong-field astrophysical observations where traditional event-horizon-based diagnostics fail.

Method: One- and two-dimensional radial perturbation analysis; Poincaré section construction; KAM tori identification; comparative study across multiple wormhole shape functions.

Result: Local linear stability at the throat; emergence of chaos in 2D/high-energy regimes; persistence of KAM tori under high energy when particle is confined to one side; universality of phase-space structure across shape functions.

Conclusion: Traversable wormholes exhibit a distinct nonlinear dynamical fingerprint—characterized by bounded chaos coexisting with residual integrability (KAM tori)—that differs fundamentally from black hole chaos induced by event horizons, offering a novel chaos-based observational discriminant for black hole mimickers.

Abstract: This study investigates the nonlinear dynamics of a test particle near the traversable wormhole throat under an external harmonic potential. One-dimensional radial perturbation analysis shows that the particle is locally linearly stable at the equilibrium position. However, for two-dimensional and high-energy cases, the system exhibits a nonlinear response, leading to large-scale chaos. The analysis indicates that, if the particle is confined on one side of the wormhole, the Poincare section will still retain Kolmogorov-Arnold-Moser (KAM) tori under extremely high-energy conditions, which is distinct from the chaos caused by the event horizon in the black hole. By studying another set of shape functions, the universality of this phase space structure is confirmed. This research clarifies the unique nonlinear dynamical mechanism of a traversable wormhole. It provides a new criterion, based on chaotic dynamics, for identifying black hole mimickers in strong-field astrophysical observations.

</details>


### [28] [A stepping stone toward detecting gravitational wave memory: a cumulative analysis with the full $(\ell=2, m=0)$ spherical harmonic using events from GWTC-4.0 and GWTC-5.0](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04909)

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper uses Bayesian model selection to test for the (ℓ=2,m=0) spherical harmonic mode—including both oscillatory and displacement memory components—in LIGO/Virgo binary black hole events (GWTC-4.0/5.0), finding modest cumulative evidence (log₁₀ℬ = 1.38±0.79) but insufficient for decisive detection; it estimates ~166 events needed for conclusive detection, pending improved waveform systematics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To advance gravitational-wave memory detection by testing for the (2,0) mode—a key channel carrying memory—and assess current observational support in existing catalogs.

Method: Bayesian model selection comparing signal models with and without the full (2,0) mode (using IMRPhenomTHM_20, which includes oscillatory + displacement memory); SNR stacking; extension to precessing case using IMRPhenomTPHM_20 for GW241127_061008; analysis on GWTC-4.0 and selected GWTC-5.0 events.

Result: Cumulative log₁₀ℬ = 1.38±0.79 favoring (2,0) mode in GWTC-4.0; stacked memory-only SNR = 0.89⁺⁰.²⁹₋₀.₁₁ after ~7.5 months of O4a; no additional evidence when precession is included for GW241127_061008; estimated N_events ≈ 166⁺⁸²₋₅₅ for decisive detection.

Conclusion: Current data provide suggestive but inconclusive evidence for the (2,0) mode; robust detection requires a larger event catalog and deeper waveform systematics studies.

Abstract: We perform Bayesian model selection to test for the presence of the $(\ell=2,m=0)$ spherical harmonic mode in gravitational wave events that have previously been identified as binary black hole mergers. As our signal model we use the quasi-circular, non-precessing IMRPhenomTHM_20 waveform model, which includes the oscillatory and displacement memory contributions. Including the oscillatory component of the (2,0) mode increases the signal-to-noise ratio and evidence for this mode, compared to testing only for the presence of gravitational wave memory. Our analysis thus constitutes a natural stepping stone toward detecting gravitational wave memory. We perform our analysis for the binary black hole signals identified in the GWTC-4.0 catalog, and for selected GWTC-5.0 events. In our Bayesian model comparison we find a cumulative $\log_{10}\mathcal{B}=1.38\pm0.79$ in favor of the presence of the (2,0) mode for the GWTC-4.0 catalog. We also stack the signal-to-noise ratio of the full (2,0) mode and of its individual contributions, obtaining results consistent with previous studies and reaching $\mathrm{SNR}_{\mathrm{memory}} = 0.89^{+0.29}_{-0.11}$ after approximately 7.5 months of O4a observations. In addition, we study the precessing candidate GW241127_061008, and find no additional evidence for the (2,0) mode when precession is included in IMRPhenomTPHM_20. Overall, our results provide an assessment of the observational support for the (2,0) mode in current gravitational wave data and allow us to discuss prospects for its future detection. We find that decisive statistical evidence will likely require a larger catalog, with an optimistic estimated number of events of $N_{\mathrm{events}} = 166^{+82}_{-55}$, based on the specific assumptions adopted in this work. We also expect that decisive evidence will require a more extensive waveform systematics study.

</details>


### [29] [Emergent cosmology and gravity from quantum time?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05020)
*Ovidiu Cristinel Stoica*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes that general relativity—or a modification thereof—can emerge from intrinsic time observables in stationary quantum states, using macroscopic observables and wavefunction amplitude as a probability density for intrinsic time, leading to emergent spacetime curvature without presupposing gravity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain how spacetime geometry and gravitational dynamics (e.g., FLRW cosmology) can arise from purely quantum stationary states—without assuming general relativity or a priori spacetime structure—by leveraging intrinsic time defined via the wavefunction's amplitude.

Method: Interprets the squared amplitude of a stationary quantum state as a probability density over intrinsic time; derives emergent curvature by analyzing macroscopic observables in spherically symmetric stationary solutions in 4D Euclidean space; examines consequences when no unique time direction exists.

Result: Demonstrates that the FLRW cosmological model emerges from spherically symmetric stationary quantum states in Euclidean space without inputting gravity; shows that spacetime curvature arises generically in all dimensions from amplitude variation alone when intrinsic time lacks a preferred direction.

Conclusion: General relativity—or a close variant—may be an effective description arising from intrinsic time observables in stationary quantum systems, suggesting a quantum origin for classical spacetime geometry and gravity.

Abstract: Macroscopic observables allow the recovery of intrinsic dynamics from stationary quantum states. I show that, by interpreting the squared amplitude as the probability density for each definite value of intrinsic time, a curvature emerges in the time direction. For example, from the perspective of intrinsic quantum time, the Friedmann-Lemaître-Robertson-Walker cosmological model emerges from spherically symmetric stationary solutions in four-dimensional Euclidean space, without presupposing gravity. If there is no unique direction of time, curvature emerges in all spacetime dimensions, without presupposing gravity, from the variable amplitude of the stationary wavefunction alone. This opens a new possibility that general relativity or some modification of it emerges from intrinsic time observables.

</details>


### [30] [Leading weak-field magnetic corrections to charged scalar quasinormal modes of Kerr black holes in the Melvin--Kerr geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05074)
*Haryanto M. Siahaan*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes magnetic corrections to quasinormal modes (QNMs) of charged scalars around a Kerr black hole in a weak external magnetic field, using the Melvin–Kerr geometry. It shows that, at leading order O(bq), the charged Klein–Gordon equation separates and reduces to a massive scalar Kerr problem with an effective mass shift μ_eff² = μ² + 2qb m. The resulting QNM shifts are sector-dependent (sign flips between m = ±1), linear in qb, and fully captured by the unmagnetized spectrum’s sensitivity to μ².


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how weak magnetic fields affect quasinormal-mode spectra of charged scalars on rotating black holes—relevant for probing astrophysical magnetized environments and testing fundamental physics near compact objects.

Method: Analytical perturbation within the Melvin–Kerr spacetime; separation of the charged Klein–Gordon equation under O(bq) truncation; effective-mass substitution μ_eff² = μ² + 2qb m; adaptation of Dolan’s continued-fraction method for QNM computation; validation via slope analysis ∂Re(ω)/∂(μM)² and effective-potential diagnostics.

Result: Magnetic corrections induce linear, m-sector-dependent shifts in QNM frequencies: Re(ω) increases for m = +1 and decreases for m = −1; shifts quantitatively match ∂Re(ω)/∂(μM)² × qb m; no finite-radius corrections appear at O(bq); method reproduces unmagnetized Dolan spectra to 10⁻⁶ for a ≤ 0.5M.

Conclusion: The leading magnetic effect on charged-scalar QNMs in weak-field Melvin–Kerr geometry is entirely encoded in a simple effective-mass substitution, preserving separability and enabling accurate, analytic prediction of mode shifts—validating a robust mapping from unmagnetized to weakly magnetized regimes.

Abstract: We compute the leading magnetic corrections to the charged-scalar quasinormal-mode (QNM) spectrum of a Kerr black hole immersed in a weak external magnetic field, working in the Melvin--Kerr geometry and in the gauge in which the time component of the electromagnetic potential vanishes at large radius. Within the controlled $\mathcal{O}(bq)$ truncation, the charged Klein--Gordon equation separates and the radial problem takes the massive-scalar Kerr form under the effective-mass substitution $\mueff^{2}\equivμ^{2}+2qbm$, applied to the asymptotic mass exponent and to the spheroidicity parameter. This gives a parameter-deformed Dolan continued-fraction scheme, with no further finite-radius correction at the order retained. Since the Melvin--Kerr spacetime is not asymptotically flat, the resulting spectrum is not the exact global QNM spectrum of the full magnetized spacetime: the modes are weak-field deviations of Kerr ringdown modes, defined by outgoing boundary conditions in the intermediate Kerr-like region $r_{+}\ll r\ll b^{-1}$. The unmagnetized backbone reproduces Dolan's tabulated spectra at the $10^{-6}$ level for $a\le 0.5M$. For $\ell=1$, $μM\in\{0,0.3\}$, $a/M\in\{0.3,0.5\}$, $qM=0.1$, and $bM\le 10^{-2}$, the magnetic shift in $\Re(\Mw)$ is opposite in sign between the two rotating sectors of equal $|m|$: upward for $m=+1$, downward for $m=-1$, and linear in $qb$. The sign and sector-dependent magnitude of each shift are quantitatively reproduced by the unmagnetized slope $\partial\Re(\Mw)/\partial(μM)^{2}$ evaluated per sector, confirming that the magnetic effect is fully transmitted through the master substitution. Effective-potential diagnostics and an extension to $\ell=2$ confirm the picture.

</details>


### [31] [Structure of Anisotropic Magnetized Neutron Stars in f(R,T) Gravity with Realistic Equation of State](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05333)
*M. Savari,G. H. Bordbar,J. Sedaghat,A. Sheykhi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates neutron star structure in f(R,T) modified gravity, incorporating magnetic field and anisotropy, using a realistic AV18-based EoS; it shows that f(R,T) gravity can explain mass-gap compact objects as neutron stars and successfully fits observational data from LIGO/Virgo/KAGRA and NICER for several binary merger events and pulsars.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the inability of standard Einstein-gravity EoS models to explain compact objects in the 'mass gap' (2.5–5 M⊙), and to test whether f(R,T) modified gravity—augmented with magnetic field and anisotropy—can consistently describe neutron star structure while satisfying modern multimessenger constraints.

Method: Numerical integration of stellar structure equations (Tolman–Oppenheimer–Volkoff generalized to f(R,T) gravity) using a realistic equation of state derived from AV18 nucleon-nucleon potential; inclusion of magnetic field effects and anisotropic pressure; computation of observables including Schwarzschild radius, compactness, gravitational redshift, and Kretschmann scalar; parameter calibration against LIGO/Virgo/KAGRA and NICER data.

Result: f(R,T) gravity with tuned coupling, magnetic field, and anisotropy parameters reproduces observed masses and radii of GW170817, PSR J0952-0607, PSR J0740+6620, and secondary components of GW190814 and GW200210-092254; mass-gap objects can be interpreted as neutron stars in this framework; all computed geometric and physical observables satisfy current astrophysical constraints.

Conclusion: f(R,T) modified gravity—when combined with realistic microphysical EoS, magnetic field, and pressure anisotropy—provides a viable theoretical framework for neutron stars, resolves tensions with mass-gap objects, and remains consistent with multimessenger gravitational-wave and X-ray timing observations.

Abstract: In this study, within the framework of f(R,T) modified gravity, we investigate the influence of coupling parameter, magnetic field and anisotropy parameter on the neutron star structure. This work employs an accurate equation of state (EoS), derived from realistic microscopic calculations based on the AV18 nucleon-nucleon potential, to compute the structure of this compact object. Here, determination of Schwarzschild radius, compactness, gravitational surface redshift and Kretschmann scalar within the f(R, T) gravity, confirms that our theoretical results are consistent with the observational constraints. While established physical EoSs within the framework of Einstein gravity have successfully characterized a broad range of compact objects, they remain inadequate in explaining certain massive objects residing within the mass gap (2.5 to 5 Msun). We show that some compact objects residing in the mass gap interpreted as candidates of neutron stars within the framework of f(R, T) gravity. Finally, we compare our results with the observational data from LIGO/Virgo/KAGRA and NICER, setting the parameters of the f(R, T) theory and anisotropy to successfully reproduce the masses and radii of the GW170817, PSR J0952-0607 and PSR J0740+6620 and the masses of the secondary components of GW190814 and GW200210-092254.

</details>

### [32] [Time-Dependent Integrability from Gauge Theory, I](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02648)
*Shota Komatsu,Jun-ichi Sakamoto,Anders Wallberg,Masahito Yamazaki*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces a generalized 4D Chern-Simons theory with spacetime-dependent one-forms to systematically construct solvable, time-dependent integrable field theories; it establishes that their allowed time dependence matches the one-loop RG flow, preserves Lax integrability and inverse scattering solvability, and yields new classes of integrable dilaton gravity and connections to condensed matter and gravity models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a unifying, systematic framework for constructing exactly solvable time-dependent integrable systems—crucial for non-equilibrium physics and quantum simulation benchmarking—where exact real-time dynamics are otherwise rare.

Method: Generalizes four-dimensional Chern-Simons theory by replacing the holomorphic one-form with a spacetime-dependent one-form; applies this to classically integrable field theories (ultralocal and non-ultralocal); analyzes integrability preservation (Lax pair, inverse scattering), renormalization group correspondence, and gravitational reinterpretation (dilaton gravity).

Result: A universal construction yielding time-dependent integrable field theories whose explicit time dependence is precisely governed by the one-loop renormalization group flow; all such theories retain Lax integrability and solvability via inverse scattering; extends known time-dependent sigma models; produces a broad class of classically integrable 2D dilaton gravity theories; reveals links to condensed matter and non-autonomous Einstein gravity reductions.

Conclusion: Four-dimensional Chern-Simons theory—with spacetime-dependent one-forms—provides a powerful, unifying geometric framework for generating solvable, time-dependent integrable systems, bridging quantum field theory, integrable systems, RG flow, and low-dimensional gravity.

Abstract: Solvable time-dependent systems provide important settings for studying non-equilibrium physics, where exact results are rare. They are also useful for benchmarking quantum simulations, which can directly probe real-time dynamics beyond the reach of conventional numerical approaches. In this paper, we show that the four-dimensional Chern-Simons theory offers a natural and unifying framework for constructing such systems. Focusing on classically integrable field theories, we consider a generalization of the four-dimensional Chern-Simons theory in which the usual holomorphic one-form is replaced by a more general, spacetime-dependent one-form. This yields a systematic procedure for generating time-dependent integrable field theories and establishes a universal relation: for every theory obtained in this way, the allowed time dependence coincides with the one-loop renormalization group flow. Despite the explicit time dependence, these theories retain Lax integrability and remain solvable by inverse scattering methods. Our construction applies to both ultralocal and non-ultralocal theories and extends previously known time-dependent sigma models to a much broader class of integrable systems. It also admits rewriting as dilaton gravity coupled to matter, producing a large family of classically integrable dilaton gravity theories in two dimensions. We also comment on connections to time-dependent integrable models studied recently in condensed matter physics and non-autonomous integrable systems arising from dimensionally-reduced Einstein gravity.

</details>


### [33] [The giant graviton expansion in AdS$_5\times$SE$_5$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02658)
*Alfredo González Lezcano,Leopoldo A. Pando Zayas,Augniva Ray*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies giant graviton-like D3-branes in AdS₅×SE₅ type IIB supergravity backgrounds, focusing on supersymmetric three-cycles in five-dimensional Sasaki–Einstein (SE₅) manifolds. It links maximal angular momentum configurations to divergences of the transverse Kähler potential, quantizes excitations as a Fock–Darwin problem with conical deficit (generalizing the Landau problem), and computes a protected contribution to the superconformal index—matching the finite-N index for the dual N=1 quiver SCFT in the AdS₅×T¹,¹ case.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how geometric data of Sasaki–Einstein manifolds SE₅ is encoded in probe D3-brane dynamics and to compute protected observables (e.g., superconformal index) from quantized giant graviton fluctuations, bridging geometry, supersymmetry, and dual SCFT physics.

Method: Analyzes supersymmetric D3-brane probes wrapping three-cycles in SE₅ and rotating along the Reeb vector; uses transverse Kähler geometry to characterize maximal angular momentum loci; quantizes fluctuations via a Fock–Darwin Hamiltonian incorporating conical deficit; computes protected contributions to the superconformal index using representation theory and localization techniques.

Result: Establishes that maximal angular momentum giant gravitons correspond to Kähler potential singularities; identifies quantized fluctuations as a conically deformed Fock–Darwin system encoding SE₅ geometry; derives a protected index contribution; explicitly reproduces the finite-N superconformal index for the T¹,¹ dual N=1 quiver SCFT.

Conclusion: The geometry of SE₅ is fully captured in the effective quantum mechanics of giant graviton fluctuations—via external potential and conical deficit—and yields exact, protected field-theoretic data matching dual SCFT expectations, validating the geometric–holographic correspondence beyond AdS₅×S⁵.

Abstract: We study giant graviton-like D3-branes as probe configurations in type IIB supergravity backgrounds AdS$_5\times$SE$_5$, with emphasis on the structure of the five-dimensional Sasaki-Einstein manifolds SE$_5$. These configurations wrap supersymmetric three-cycles in SE$_5$ and rotate at the speed of light along the Reeb direction. We formulate the general problem in terms of the transverse Kähler potential and show that configurations carrying maximal angular momentum can be described by loci where the transverse Kähler potential diverges in suitably chosen coordinates. We quantize a particular set of excitations, similar to those considered in the AdS$_5\times S^5$ case, and show that they are governed by a Fock-Darwin problem with a conical deficit, which generalizes the Landau problem obtained for $S^5$. Succinctly, the information about the geometry of SE$_5$ is encoded in the form of the external potential and the conical deficit. We compute a protected contribution to the superconformal index arising from quantized fluctuations of supersymmetric giant graviton configurations. For the explicit case of AdS$_5\times T^{1,1}$, we recover the finite-N protected index of the dual quiver ${\cal N}=1$ superconformal field theory in the sector captured by a non-Abelian generalization of the quantized degrees of freedom.

</details>


### [34] [Holographic Learning from Fermionic Spectra: Application to Strange Metal Phenomenology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02861)
*Hong-Zhi Xiao,Zhan-Zhi He,Zhuo-Yu Xian,Shao-Feng Wu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces a Neural ODE–based data-driven framework to infer bulk gravitational and gauge structures (metric and U(1) potential) of planar black holes from boundary fermionic spectral functions; it validates the method on known holographic models and applies it to cuprate strange metals, finding an effective near-AdS₂×ℝ² geometry with tiny gauge potential, while identifying key limitations—insensitivity to conformal factor (due to massless probe), need for additional thermodynamic constraints, and strong temperature-dependent degradation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge top-down holographic modeling and bottom-up experimental spectroscopy in strongly correlated quantum matter—specifically, to reconstruct bulk dual geometries directly from measured fermionic spectral functions in cuprates without assuming a priori action or symmetry.

Method: Uses Neural Ordinary Differential Equations (Neural ODEs) trained end-to-end to learn the bulk metric functions (e.g., g_{tt}, g_{zz}) and U(1) gauge potential A_t(z) of static, planar-symmetric black holes, constrained by matching computed boundary fermionic spectral functions (via Dirac equation in bulk) to input spectral data; training includes validation on Einstein–Maxwell and Gubser–Rocha analytic solutions before application to extended power-law liquid (PLL)-generated cuprate spectra.

Result: For (Pb,Bi)₂Sr₂₋ₓLaₓCuO₆₊δ near optimal doping and low T, the inferred geometry belongs to a conformal class near AdS₂×ℝ² with qA_t ≈ 10⁻⁴ eV; the massless probe fermion renders reconstruction insensitive to the conformal factor Ω(z); loss and qA_t increase sharply with temperature, while doping variation (within strange metal regime) induces only mild degradation.

Conclusion: Neural ODEs enable faithful, unsupervised geometric inference from fermionic spectra in semi-holographic settings, but physical interpretability requires complementary thermodynamic data due to conformal ambiguity; temperature—not doping—is the principal practical limitation for applicability to real cuprate data.

Abstract: We develop a data-driven framework based on Neural ODEs that learns the bulk metric functions and the $U(1)$ gauge potential of a static, planar-symmetric black hole from boundary fermionic spectral functions. After validating the framework on the Einstein--Maxwell and Gubser--Rocha models with high accuracy, we apply it to the strange metal phase of the cuprate $\mathrm{(Pb,Bi)_2Sr_{2-x}La_xCuO_{6+δ}}$ within a semi-holographic setting, taking as input the spectral data generated from the extended power-law liquid (PLL) model calibrated by angle-resolved photoemission measurements. At low temperatures and near-optimal doping, we show that the normalized extended PLL model can be well described by an effective representative of a conformal class near $\mathrm{AdS}_{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}$ black holes with a nearly vanishing gauge potential ($qA_t\sim10^{-4}\,\mathrm{eV}$). A key structural observation is that our probe fermion is massless and therefore insensitive to the conformal factor $Ω(z)$. This further implies that fixing the macroscopic thermodynamics such as the electronic specific heat would require independent input beyond the fermionic spectra. We also delineate the applicability boundaries of our framework: the learned effective model remains viable across doping at low temperatures, with the loss increasing mildly as the samples move away from the marginal Fermi liquid point toward the overdoped side; the principal limitation is temperature, where both the loss and $qA_t$ grow substantially.

</details>


### [35] [Pre-Strings Lectures on Artificial Intelligence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02905)
*James Halverson*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper presents lecture notes from the Pre-Strings 2026 school, bridging neural networks and theoretical physics: Day 1 covers NN fundamentals via field theory; Day 2 introduces Neural Network Field Theory (NN-FT) — a framework defining field theories via architectures and parameter densities — with applications to Liouville theory, string amplitudes, topological phases, anomalies, and critical string dimension; Day 3 explores AI for string theory, including agentic workflows, physics-informed NNs for Calabi-Yau metrics, RL for landscape/knot search, and interpretable learning for conjecture generation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish and demonstrate deep conceptual and practical connections between modern machine learning—particularly neural networks—and fundamental theoretical physics, especially quantum field theory and string theory, enabling cross-fertilization of methods and insights.

Method: Lecture-based pedagogical exposition integrating theoretical physics formalism (e.g., path integrals, Ward identities, topological classification) with machine learning concepts (e.g., parameter space statistics, expressivity, dynamics); introduces NN-FT as a constructive framework where field theories are encoded by neural architectures and parameter distributions; applies AI techniques—including agentic systems, physics-informed neural networks, reinforcement learning, and interpretable supervised learning—to concrete problems in string theory and geometry.

Result: Establishes NN-FT as a viable formalism yielding nontrivial physical results: universality theorem, neural realization of Liouville theory, reproduction of string amplitudes, identification of topological sectors and Kosterlitz-Thouless transition, derivation of Ward identities/anomalies, and novel derivation of bosonic string critical dimension (d=26); demonstrates efficacy of AI tools in computing Calabi-Yau metrics, navigating string landscapes, solving knot-theoretic problems, and generating mathematical conjectures.

Conclusion: Neural networks and string theory are mutually enriching: NN-FT provides a new foundation for field theory rooted in learnable representations, while AI techniques accelerate discovery and interpretation in high-energy theory and geometry—signaling a paradigm shift toward data- and learning-augmented theoretical physics.

Abstract: These notes are based on six lectures given over three days at the Pre-Strings 2026 school in Shanghai. Day 1 develops neural network essentials, organized around the expressivity, statistics, and dynamics of neural networks, presented with a field-theoretic lens. Day 2 develops a neural network approach to field theory (NN-FT), in which a field theory is defined by a network architecture and a density on its parameters, and surveys recent results. Examples include a universality theorem, a neural network realization of Liouville theory, famous string amplitudes, topological sectors and the Kosterlitz-Thouless transition, Ward identities and anomalies, and a new derivation of the critical dimension of the bosonic string. Day 3 turns the lens around and covers applied AI for string theory: agentic workflows that are changing how the other techniques are implemented, physics-informed neural networks and Calabi-Yau metrics, reinforcement learning and search in the string landscape and in knot theory, and interpretable supervised learning with an eye towards conjecture generation.

</details>


### [36] [Null-strings Gauged, Reloaded and Quantized, I: Canonical Quantization in the Light-Cone Gauge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02970)
*Ida M. Rasulian,M. M. Sheikh-Jabbari,H. Yavartanoo*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies light-cone quantization of null-strings in D-dimensional flat spacetime, revealing a previously overlooked Carroll-Weyl gauge symmetry that reduces physical degrees of freedom to (D−3), yields a discrete spectrum, and eliminates the need for a critical dimension.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To correctly quantize null-strings by accounting for their full gauge symmetry—specifically the Carroll-Weyl symmetry—and resolve inconsistencies arising from incomplete constraint treatment in standard light-cone gauge.

Method: Light-cone quantization incorporating the full constraint structure and newly identified Carroll-Weyl gauge symmetry; explicit reduction of phase space; Schrödinger representation quantization on the reduced configuration space.

Result: Physical phase space reduced to (D−3) propagating degrees of freedom; discrete energy spectrum for null-strings; no critical dimension required (valid for arbitrary D); construction of ground-state and excited-state wavefunctions.

Conclusion: Null-strings admit consistent quantum mechanical description without critical dimension due to Carroll-Weyl symmetry, fundamentally distinguishing them from relativistic strings and implying novel quantum gravitational implications.

Abstract: We study the light-cone quantization of null-strings in $D$ dimensional flat target-space. Incorporating the essential new gauge symmetry and the associated constraint structure of the null-string, allows one to solve for one more degree of freedom (DoF) compared to the standard light-cone gauge, reducing the physical phase space to $(D-3)$ propagating DoF. Quantization is formulated directly in the Schrödinger representation, leading to a Hilbert space of wavefunctions on the reduced configuration space. The space of physical states is built on a reduced phase space associated with the corrected gauge structure. We discuss the ground-state wavefunction and a generic class of excited states. As a direct consequence of the overlooked Carroll-Weyl gauge symmetry of the null-strings, we find the remarkable and perhaps unexpected result that null-strings exhibit a discrete spectrum. Our analysis indicate that there is no critical dimension for null-strings, and $D$ can be arbitrary.

</details>


### [37] [Graph Neural Networks for the Graphical Bootstrap](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03109)
*Rigers Aliaj,Gabriele Dian,Reza Doobary,Paul Heslop*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper applies graph neural networks—including graph transformers—to classify over 20 million graphs from planar N=4 super-Yang–Mills correlator computations, achieving near-perfect ROC AUC (99.996%) and enabling substantial data reduction (85.5% redundancy shrinkage) and interpretability via embedding analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the computational bottleneck in high-order perturbative calculations of correlators in planar N=4 super-Yang–Mills theory, where graph enumeration and classification scale prohibitively; leveraging ML for both accuracy and efficiency gains.

Method: Benchmarked multiple graph neural network architectures—including graph transformers—on a large-scale graph classification task; evaluated generalization to larger graphs; quantified data redundancy reduction relative to the graphical bootstrap algorithm; performed embedding space analysis for interpretability.

Result: Achieved 99.996% ROC AUC on graph classification; demonstrated up to 85.5% reduction in redundant denominator graphs; revealed interpretable structure in learned embeddings correlated with physical graph properties.

Conclusion: Graph neural networks—especially graph transformers—are highly effective for classifying physics-derived graphs at scale, offering both exceptional generalization and practical computational speedup, while enabling physically meaningful interpretation through embedding analysis.

Abstract: We study a graph classification problem involving over 20 million graphs, arising from high-order perturbative computations of correlators in planar $\mathcal{N}=4$ super-Yang--Mills, a model closely related to the theory of the strong nuclear force. We benchmark graph neural networks, including graph transformers, achieving robust generalization to larger graphs with up to $99.996\%$ ROC AUC. Then, we analyze how the models can be used to gain a computational speedup compared to the traditional graphical bootstrap algorithm, through shrinking the redundant data by up to $85.5\%$ at the level of denominator graphs. Finally, we study the embeddings of the models to investigate their interpretability.

</details>


### [38] [Exact Schwarzian Metric Factor and Holographic Wilson-Loop Screening](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03120)
*Miguel Tierz*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper exactly evaluates the radial metric factor h(ζ) in the AdS₂ throat of an extremal Reissner–Nordström AdS₅ black brane using Schwarzian averaging, yielding a Gaussian integral representation and an exact Appell–Lerch q/q*-series that reveals a nonperturbative scale e^{−π²C/ζ}; it proves complete monotonicity of ℊ₀(ζ)=h(ζ)/ζ²—ruling out spurious confining minima—and computes the exact relative variance of the Schwarzian kernel; application to the temporal Wilson loop yields algebraic IR screening E(L)∼−κ_IR/L², consistent with semi-local quantum liquid behavior.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguities in near-boundary truncations of the Schwarzian-averaged geometry in AdS₂ throats—particularly spurious confining minima—and to identify nonperturbative scales and robust IR physics (e.g., Wilson-loop screening) missed by finite-order approximations.

Method: Exact evaluation of the radial metric factor h(ζ) via Schwarzian averaging; derivation of a Gaussian integral against coth(πy); application of Mordell’s identity and its extension incorporating the quasimodular Eisenstein series E₂; proof of complete monotonicity of ℊ₀(ζ) = h(ζ)/ζ²; computation of the exact relative variance of the Schwarzian kernel; analysis of the temporal rectangular Wilson loop in the exact throat geometry.

Result: An exact Gaussian integral and Appell–Lerch q/q*-series representation for h(ζ), exposing the nonperturbative scale e^{−π²C/ζ}; proof that ℊ₀(ζ) is completely monotone (no interior minimum); exact relative variance quantifying averaging error; algebraic Wilson-loop screening E(L) ∼ −κ_IR/L²; numerical confirmation that the screened saddle dominates over truncated geometries; demonstration that the constant-force regime in fourth-order truncation is unphysical.

Conclusion: Finite near-boundary truncations produce artificial features (e.g., confining minima, constant-force regimes); the exact Schwarzian-averaged geometry is free of such artifacts, exhibits robust algebraic IR screening, and encodes nonperturbative physics via modular structures (E₂, Appell–Lerch series), confirming the semi-local quantum liquid nature of the extremal RN brane's IR.

Abstract: We evaluate exactly the radial metric factor $h(ζ)$ generated by Schwarzian averaging in the AdS$_2$ throat of an extremal Reissner--Nordström AdS$_5$ black brane. The result is a Gaussian integral against $\coth(πy)$, valid at all radial depths, which Mordell's identity turns into an exact Appell--Lerch $q/q^\ast$-series representation. The dual series identifies the nonperturbative scale $e^{-π^2 C/ζ}$ missed by any finite near-boundary truncation. The third parametric derivative required by the evaluation generates the quasimodular Eisenstein series $E_2$, absent from the classical Mordell identity. From the integral representation we prove that $\mathcal{G}_0(ζ):=h(ζ)/ζ^2$ is completely monotone and hence has no interior minimum, so any confining minimum produced by a finite near-boundary truncation is an artifact. We also compute the exact relative variance of the Schwarzian kernel, which makes the averaged-metric approximation error quantitative and shows that the absence of a confining minimum is robust across moment-based effective geometries. Applied to the temporal rectangular Wilson loop, the exact throat gives algebraic screening, $E(L)\sim -κ_{\rm IR}/L^2$, the Wilson-loop diagnostic of the semi-local quantum-liquid IR of the extremal RN brane. A numerical check in a simple matched geometry confirms that the screened saddle is the dominant string configuration, and an exact-versus-truncated force comparison shows that the apparent constant-force regime of the fourth-order truncation is not a feature of the exact geometry.

</details>


### [39] [Singularities, Entropy and the Arrow of Time, {\it or} Is CRT a Gauge Symmetry in Quantum Gravity?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03199)
*T. Banks*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: The paper investigates whether the discrete CRT transformation is a gauge symmetry in quantum gravity, concluding it is generally not, but can be interpreted as an asymptotic gauge symmetry in flat/AdS spacetimes or a spontaneously broken gauge symmetry in eternal de Sitter space—under specific conditions including an extended Chern-Simons formulation and time-independent quantum mechanics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify the status of the discrete CRT transformation as a gauge symmetry in quantum gravity, given the lack of a fully defined theory of quantum gravity.

Method: Analyzes CRT within well-defined gravitational frameworks—namely asymptotically flat, AdS, and eternal dS spacetimes—using asymptotic symmetry analysis, spontaneous symmetry breaking arguments, and an extended Chern-Simons formulation of 2+1D gravity; incorporates constraints from quantum measurement theory and semiclassical gravity on clock-based detectors.

Result: CRT is not a fundamental gauge symmetry in general quantum gravity; it acts as an asymptotic gauge symmetry in flat/AdS spacetimes, and only as a spontaneously broken gauge symmetry in eternal dS if an 'Alice String' configuration is admitted in the thermofield double Hilbert space—requiring both a modified Chern-Simons description and time-independent quantum mechanics.

Conclusion: CRT cannot be universally regarded as a gauge symmetry of quantum gravity; its status depends critically on spacetime asymptotics and foundational assumptions about time and measurement in quantum gravity.

Abstract: We examine the question of whether the discrete transformation CRT is a gauge symmetry of ``Quantum Gravity". Since the phrase in quotes is not yet completely well defined, we first try to define specific frameworks in which one might ask the question. We find that the general answer is NO. In asymptotically flat and AdS spaces, CRT is an asymptotic gauge symmetry in the same sense that the continuous parts of the Poincare/AdS isometry groups and scalar internal symmetries are. In eternal dS space it can be considered a spontaneously broken gauge symmetry if a certain ``Alice String" configuration is allowed in the Thermofield double Hilbert space. This requires an extension of the conventional Chern-Simons rewriting of $2 + 1$ dimensional gravity. This interpretation only makes sense if we consider the underlying quantum mechanics to be time independent. General quantum measurement and semi-classical gravitational restrictions on measuring devices put {\it a priori} limits on the existence of detectors with clocks that can actually measure proper time along a classical dS geodesic.

</details>


### [40] [Falling through the horizon of a quantum black hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03344)
*Victor Franken,Thomas G. Mertens,Bruno de S. L. Torres*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates quantum-gravitational effects on an infalling detector near the horizon of a near-extremal black hole in quantum Jackiw–Teitelboim (JT) gravity, using gravitational dressing to define diffeomorphism-invariant observables; it shows that the detector can locally detect horizon location and temperature—violating the equivalence principle—but without encountering a firewall.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how quantum gravity modifies local physics near black hole horizons, particularly whether and how the equivalence principle breaks down in a controlled, solvable setting (quantum JT gravity).

Method: Uses gravitational dressing of local observables and infalling trajectories in quantum JT gravity, extending geometric modular flow-motivated dressing from exterior to interior via thermofield double state; connects near-horizon physics to IR Schwarzian dynamics.

Result: The gravitationally dressed infalling detector acquires horizon-location- and temperature-dependent signals as it approaches the horizon—enabling local determination of both—thereby violating the equivalence principle while remaining smooth (no firewall).

Conclusion: Quantum gravitational dressing induces observable, horizon-probing effects in near-extremal black holes within JT gravity, demonstrating a controlled breakdown of the equivalence principle without pathology, suggesting a role for modular flow and IR Schwarzian dynamics in horizon physics.

Abstract: We study quantum-gravitational effects on the response of an infalling detector as it crosses the horizon of a near-extremal black hole in the framework of quantum JT gravity. These effects are incorporated via the gravitational dressing needed to define both the infalling trajectory and the local observables probed by the detector in a diffeomorphism-invariant way. In the black hole exterior, a preferred choice of dressing to the Schwarzian mode of JT gravity can be motivated in connection to geometric modular flow. We show how to extend this dressing to the black hole interior, defining local observables that are gravitationally dressed to both boundaries in the thermofield double state. The gravitational dressing connects the near-horizon region to the IR sector of the Schwarzian theory, leading to measurable effects as the horizon is approached. We find that the infalling detector is able to locally determine the location of the horizon and its temperature, violating the equivalence principle, but without encountering a firewall.

</details>


### [41] [Berry Picking: Random Wave Chaos Hierarchy for BPS Microstate Geometries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03434)
*Vladan Djukić,Milica Stepanović,Mihailo Čubrović*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates chaos in probe waves and geodesics across smooth supergravity backgrounds with varying supersymmetry and AdS throat length, finding wave chaos increases (toward black hole behavior) while geodesic motion becomes more regular; this dichotomy is explained via stable periodic orbits and decreasing KAM tori measure; CFT Rényi entropies show no universal trend, indicating BPS chaos hierarchy does not smoothly extrapolate to black holes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how chaos manifests in smooth supergravity geometries approaching black hole limits—specifically whether wave and geodesic chaos scale uniformly with supersymmetry breaking and throat length—and to assess implications for holographic chaos and quantum complexity in the dual CFT.

Method: Numerical and analytical study of probe wave equations and timelike/null geodesics in LLM geometries, supertubes, and superstrata; evaluation of Berry random wave hypothesis compliance; construction of Poincaré sections; computation of Rényi entropies for dual CFT states in weak coupling.

Result: Wave chaos strengthens with decreasing supersymmetry and increasing throat length; geodesic motion becomes more integrable; stable periodic orbits persist in long throats despite global decrease in KAM tori measure; Rényi entropies lack universal scaling with supersymmetry or throat length.

Conclusion: The 'hierarchy of BPS chaos' behaves fundamentally differently in bulk gravity (wave vs. geodesic) and boundary CFT, and neither admits a simple extrapolation to black hole physics.

Abstract: We estimate the strength of chaos of probe waves and probe geodesics in different smooth supergravity backgrounds of decreasing supersymmetry and/or increasing length of the AdS throat in the interior (LLM geometry, supertubes, superstrata). We find that the wave chaos becomes stronger and stronger with less supersymetry and longer throats; in other words, chaos becomes stronger as we approach black hole solutions. Geodesic motion shows the opposite trend, becoming more and more regular. Testing the wave chaos by its compliance with the Berry random wave hypothesis and the geodesic chaos by computing Poincare sections, we explain the dichotomy between wave and geodesic motion by the existence of stable periodic orbits inside long throats while the overall measure of KAM tori decreases. Computing the Renyi entropies for the dual CFT states in the weak coupling regime, we show that they do not have such universal trends and the complexity depends on the specifics of the state rather than just the amount of supersymmetry and throat length. We conclude that the hierarchy of BPS chaos works differently in the bulk and in field theory, and in either case cannot be simply extrapolated to black holes.

</details>


### [42] [Finite path integral limits work in cases where the perturbative series is not Borel summable](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03439)
*Ariel Edery*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates perturbative expansions under finite path integral limits for a non-Borel-summable double-well integral, showing that expansions about either the local maximum or a minimum yield absolutely convergent series that recover the exact result in the infinite-limit limit—demonstrating that finite-domain perturbation can handle non-Borel-summable cases and encode multi-instanton/vacuum structure.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether finite path integral limits enable convergent perturbative expansions for observables in systems with non-Borel-summable asymptotic series—specifically those arising from non-trivial vacuum structure (e.g., double-well potentials).

Method: Analyzes a prototypical non-Borel-summable integral with a double-well potential; constructs two perturbative series—(i) around the unstable critical point (local maximum at origin), and (ii) around a stable minimum—both under finite integration bounds [−L, L]; proves absolute convergence of both series and verifies convergence to the exact analytical result as L → ∞.

Result: Both perturbative expansions—about the maximum and about a minimum—are absolutely convergent for finite L; in the L → ∞ limit, each reproduces the exact closed-form value of the integral; crucially, the expansion about one minimum fully captures contributions from both vacua, implying effective encoding of global topological/vacuum structure.

Conclusion: Finite path integral limits render otherwise non-Borel-summable perturbative series absolutely convergent and physically complete, even for systems with degenerate vacua; this validates finite-domain perturbation as a robust method beyond Borel summability, with implications for strong-coupling and instanton physics.

Abstract: The perturbative expansion in powers of the coupling of observables in quantum field theory and quantum mechanics is known to yield an asymptotic series. If the original physical system is well-behaved and a finite observable is expected, this can often be calculated via a Borel resummation of the asymptotic series. However, there are cases where a system is well-behaved and the series is not Borel summable. This typically occurs when the physical system has a non-trivial vacuum structure. It has recently been shown that if the perturbative series is carried out under finite path integral limits, one can obtain a convergent series that yields observables even at strong coupling. This was recently used to obtain the energy at strong coupling for the anharmonic oscillator. This is a Borel summable case so the question is whether finite path integral limits work when the series is not Borel summable. To begin answering this question we consider a simple non-Borel summable case: the series stemming from a basic integral where the function has a double-well shape and hence two minima. The integral has an exact analytical expression that the series can be compared to. Under finite integral limits that run from $-L$ to $L$, where $L$ is finite, positive and real, we develop two perturbative series in powers of the coupling: one by expanding the integral about the local maximum at the origin and the other by expanding it about one of the minima. In both cases, we obtain an absolutely convergent series and the series sums to the exact analytical expression of the original integral in the infinite $L$ limit. It is significant that a perturbative expansion about one of the minima reproduces the exact analytical expression because this implies that it captures the full effect of both minima.

</details>


### [43] [Quantum nucleation of black hole mimickers via chaos dominated tunneling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03452)
*Ulf Danielsson,Suvendu Giri,Vyshnav Mohan,Larus Thorlacius*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Proposes a quantum chaotic multichannel enhancement mechanism that dramatically amplifies tunneling probability for formation of horizonless black hole mimickers—e.g., string-theoretic black shells—during gravitational collapse.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Black hole mimickers (ultracompact horizonless objects) require quantum tunneling across a barrier ~Schwarzschild radius scale, which is exponentially suppressed; the paper seeks a physically viable mechanism to enhance such tunneling to make mimicker formation dynamically plausible.

Method: Leverages quantum chaotic dynamics to induce multichannel enhancement of tunneling transmission; applies it specifically to the nucleation of a string-theoretic black shell as a concrete example of a black hole mimicker.

Result: Demonstrates that quantum chaos–driven multichannel interference can exponentially amplify tunneling rates, rendering formation of black shells—and by extension other mimickers—significantly more probable than standard WKB estimates suggest.

Conclusion: Quantum chaotic multichannel effects provide a robust, generic enhancement mechanism for horizonless object formation, challenging the necessity of event horizons and offering testable implications for quantum gravity and gravitational wave signatures.

Abstract: Black hole mimickers, ultracompact horizonless objects, have been proposed as alternatives to black holes in a variety of settings, including extensions of general relativity and scenarios involving matter sectors beyond the Standard Model. Their formation in gravitational collapse of matter requires quantum mechanical tunneling to occur on a length scale of the order of the Schwarzschild radius of the corresponding black hole. We propose a mechanism, based on multichannel enhancement catalyzed by quantum chaotic dynamics, that can dramatically amplify the quantum mechanical transmission across a tunneling barrier. We explore, in particular, how the nucleation of a string theoretic black shell is enhanced via this mechanism. We anticipate similar results to hold for other proposed black hole mimickers.

</details>


### [44] [Entanglement and geometric transitions in topological string theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03526)
*Gabriel Wong*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper realizes the concept of bulk subsystems in quantum gravity within topological string theory, identifying open strings on entanglement branes as local holographic degrees of freedom; it constructs a subregion open string algebra, develops modular flow diagrammatics, verifies entanglement-gravity entropy matching, and connects to defect holography.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To define a physically meaningful bulk subsystem in quantum gravity that supports local holographic degrees of freedom—specifically gravitational edge modes whose entanglement induces spacetime backreaction and fusion of subregions.

Method: Builds on prior work in topological string theory (Donnelly & Jiang) by modeling closed-string bulk subsystems via open strings ending on dynamical 'entanglement branes'; implements local holography through geometric transitions of these branes; defines a subregion open string operator algebra and introduces diagrammatic techniques for open string modular flow; computes entanglement entropy and compares with gravitational entropy.

Result: The entanglement entropy of open strings on entanglement branes exactly reproduces the gravitational entropy of the dual closed-string background; a consistent subregion algebra and modular flow formalism is established for arbitrary states and subregions; local geometric transitions are shown to be equivalent to defect holography.

Conclusion: Bulk subsystems in quantum gravity can be concretely realized in topological string theory via entanglement branes and open strings, providing a precise framework for local holography where edge-mode entanglement drives spacetime geometry—thereby unifying subsystem structure, modular flow, and defect-based holographic duality.

Abstract: How do we define a bulk subsystem in quantum gravity? In \cite{Wong:2025kpz}, we argued that such a subsystem must support local holographic degrees of freedom. These are gravitational edge modes, whose entanglement creates a backreaction that fuses together subregions of spacetime. In this work we give a realization of these ideas in topological string theory, building upon \cite{Donnelly:2020teo,Jiang:2020cqo}. In this theory, a subsystem for closed strings consists of open strings ending on entanglement branes, which play the role of a dynamical entangling surface. Local holography is implemented by the geometric transition of these branes. We define a subregion open string algebra and develop a diagrammatics for open string modular flow for arbitrary states and subregion. We check that the entanglement entropy of these open strings reproduces the gravitational entropy of the associated closed string background. Finally, we relate these local transitions to defect holography.

</details>


### [45] [Minimal Proper Time and Deterministic Microstates: Emergent Quantum Fields and Relativistic Spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03605)
*Alessio Maiezza*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proposes a top-down derivation of quantum field theory from a deterministic, pre-geometric substrate of causally ordered events; coarse-graining over microscopic histories yields effective quantum dynamics with a running Planck constant and UV dissipative corrections, recovering standard QFT in the IR and emergent spacetime compatible with Einstein gravity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a unified deterministic origin for quantum behavior, minimal-scale structure, and relativistic spacetime—bridging pre-geometric foundations with effective quantum field theory and gravity.

Method: Top-down construction starting from a deterministic, causally ordered pre-geometric substrate; coarse-graining over microscopic event histories to derive an effective Nambu-like quantum dynamics; identification of minimal proper-time step as elementary update; modeling scale-dependent quantization via a running Planck constant and UV dissipative term tied to unresolved history growth.

Result: A consistent effective quantum dynamics emerges at low energies, featuring a scale-dependent Planck constant and ultraviolet dissipative correction; the bottom-up proper-time cutoff kernel is microscopically interpreted as inverse growth rate of unresolved deterministic histories; standard unitary QFT is recovered in the infrared limit; relativistic spacetime geometry and Einstein gravity emerge macroscopically.

Conclusion: Quantum field theory, minimal length scales, and relativistic spacetime can jointly emerge from a common deterministic, pre-geometric foundation governed by causal order and coarse-graining—suggesting deep unification beyond conventional quantum-gravity paradigms.

Abstract: We develop a top-down counterpart of the minimal proper-time formulation of quantum field theory previously introduced as an effective bottom-up framework. Starting from a deterministic pre-geometric substrate of causally ordered events, we show how coarse-graining over microscopic histories leads, at low energies, to an effective Nambu-like quantum dynamics. The elementary deterministic update is identified with the minimal proper-time step, while the growth of coarse-grained equivalence classes controls both the ultraviolet dissipative correction and the scale dependence of the effective quantization strength, encoded in a running Planck constant. In this way, the proper-time cutoff kernel of the bottom-up formulation acquires a microscopic interpretation as the inverse growth of unresolved deterministic histories. In the infrared limit, the dissipative term vanishes and standard unitary quantum field theory is recovered. The same coarse-grained structure also provides a natural setting for an emergent relativistic spacetime geometry, compatible in the macroscopic limit with Einstein gravity. The resulting picture suggests a common deterministic origin for minimal-scale structure, quantum behavior, and relativistic spacetime.

</details>


### [46] [Quiver BPS Indices from Crystal Profiles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03617)
*Jiakang Bao,Masahito Yamazaki*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: The paper derives exact formulae for elliptic genera in 2D with N≥2 supersymmetry, and extends them to Witten indices and matrix-model partition functions, using crystal-based combinatorial structures; it generalizes the Nekrasov partition function and reveals new combinatorics in crystal melting, including a thermodynamic limit where toric Calabi-Yau fourfold geometry emerges.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the Nekrasov partition function and uncover deeper combinatorial structures underlying supersymmetric partition functions and crystal melting phenomena.

Method: Derivation of exact formulae via discrete sums over previously introduced crystal structures, where each crystal's contribution is encoded by boundary data of finite substructures ('molecules'); analysis of thermodynamic limits for N=(0,2) theories on toric Calabi-Yau fourfolds.

Result: New exact expressions for elliptic genera, Witten indices, and matrix-model partition functions in terms of crystal combinatorics; generalization beyond Young diagrams; emergence of projected Calabi-Yau geometry in the thermodynamic limit.

Conclusion: Crystal melting provides a unifying combinatorial framework for supersymmetric partition functions across dimensions, revealing novel mathematical structure and geometric emergence in the large-volume limit.

Abstract: We derive new exact formulae for elliptic genera in two spacetime dimensions with $\mathcal{N}\ge 2$ supercharges, as well as their one- and zero-dimensional counterparts (for Witten indices and matrix-model partition functions). Our results are written as a discrete sum over geometric/combinatorial structures of crystals introduced previously by the authors. The contribution from each crystal may be expressed in terms of the boundary data of a finite substructure of the crystal called the molecules. Our results provide vast generalisations of the celebrated Nekrasov partition function enumerated by the Young diagrams, and uncover new combinatorics underlying the crystal melting. We also analyse the thermodynamic limit of crystals arising from two-dimensional $\mathcal{N}=(0,2)$ theories associated with toric Calabi-Yau fourfolds, where a projection of the Calabi-Yau geometry emerges in the limit.

</details>


### [47] [Equal-charge projection of the $\mathcal{N}=4$ index: exact large-$N$ formula and finite-rank $U(3)$ coefficients](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03735)
*Miguel Tierz*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies the equal-charge limit of supersymmetric rotating AdS₅ black holes and the corresponding projected N=4 superconformal index; it proves an exact large-N factorization of the index, identifies onset energies for spin sectors, reveals finite-N corrections violating classical bounds, and confirms persistence of the high-spin tail under exact charge projection.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how the microcanonical sector of the N=4 superconformal index—obtained by projecting to equal R-charges Q₁=Q₂=Q₃—encodes black hole microstates and quantum corrections beyond the classical supergravity regime, especially in the high-spin (large angular momentum) regime.

Method: Analytic derivation of the large-N projected index using constant-term extraction in charge-difference fugacities; exact U(3) group-theoretic computations for finite-N corrections; identification of onset energies j*(J_R) from the factorized form; comparison with giant-graviton contributions and classical black-hole bounds.

Result: Exact large-N factorization of the equal-charge index into a product over k and a cubic partition sum; explicit spin-resolved onset energy j*(J_R); discovery that finite-N (e.g., U(3)) states appear below the large-N onset (e.g., d₃^eqQ(87,27/2)=1 despite j*(27/2)−87=1554); full determination of the line j=6J_R+6; confirmation that the high-spin tail survives exact equal-charge projection.

Conclusion: The high-spin tail of the superconformal index persists under the exact equal-charge projection, indicating robustness of quantum black hole microstate structure beyond semiclassical expectations; finite-N effects introduce low-energy states forbidden in the large-N limit, signaling important non-perturbative physics not captured by classical supergravity.

Abstract: The equal-charge branch of supersymmetric rotating AdS$_5$ black holes has $Q_1=Q_2=Q_3$. The corresponding microcanonical sector of the $\mathcal{N}=4$ superconformal index is obtained by projecting to equal charges, or equivalently by extracting the constant term in the two charge-difference fugacities. We prove that for the large-$N$ multigraviton sector the projected index factorizes exactly as \[
  \mathcal{I}^{\rm eqQ}_\infty(x,p)
  =\prod_{k\ge1}(1-p^kx^{3k})(1-p^{-k}x^{3k})
  \sum_{n\ge0}\mathsf{p}(n)^3x^{6n}, \] where $\mathsf{p}(n)$ is the partition function. This factorization gives, for every spin sector, an explicit onset energy below which the large-$N$ coefficient is zero. Exact $U(3)$ computations show that finite-rank coefficients can nevertheless appear at energies where the large-$N$ coefficient vanishes, including beyond the classical $U(3)$ black-hole bound. We also determine the full line $j=6J_R+6$. In particular, with $j^*(J_R)$ denoting this large-$N$ onset energy, \[
  d_3^{\rm eqQ}(87,\tfrac{27}{2})=1,
  \qquad
  j^*(\tfrac{27}{2})-87=1554, \] and the first giant-graviton sector already contributes one unit at this point. All coefficients are coefficients of the $(-1)^F$-graded index, not positive degeneracies. The main conclusion is that the high-spin tail survives the exact equal-charge projection.

</details>


### [48] [Conformal Renormalisation of 8D Einstein Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03679)
*Giorgos Anastasiou,Ignacio J. Araya,Nicolas Boulanger,Rodrigo Olea,Davide Rovere*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes a correspondence between Holographic Renormalisation (HR) in eight dimensions and a unique conformal gravity theory admitting an Einstein sector with constant negative curvature; it links HR to Topological Regularisation (TR) via inclusion of the Euler term in the Einstein-Hilbert action, showing that the 8D conformal gravity action reproduces the TR-polynomial plus boundary terms that cancel asymptotic divergences; it conjectures this equivalence extends to arbitrary even dimensions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify and clarify the relationship between Holographic Renormalisation and Topological Regularisation by identifying their shared geometric and gravitational origin in conformal gravity.

Method: Analytical derivation linking HR to conformal gravity in eight dimensions; explicit computation of the TR-modified Einstein-Hilbert action including the Euler term; comparison with the unique 8D conformal gravity action and its boundary structure; generalisation via conjecture to arbitrary even dimensions.

Result: The unique 8D conformal gravity action reproduces the TR-prescribed polynomial Lagrangian and supplies precisely the boundary terms needed to cancel asymptotic divergences in the AdS action; HR is thus fully encoded in this conformal completion.

Conclusion: Holographic Renormalisation in 8D is equivalent to conformally completing the Einstein-Hilbert action with negative cosmological constant plus the Euler term with TR-fixed coefficient—suggesting a universal geometric mechanism for renormalisation in even-dimensional AdS/CFT.

Abstract: We show that Holographic Renormalisation (HR) in eight dimensions is encoded in the unique conformal gravity theory that admits an Einstein sector with constant negative curvature. We explicitly relate HR to Topological Regularisation (TR), where the latter prescribes to add the Euler term to the Einstein-Hilbert Lagrangian density, with a precise coefficient so as to ensure that the resulting density is polynomial in the anti de Sitter (AdS) curvature. The polynomial is still asymptotically divergent. We find that the aforementioned, unique conformal gravity action in 8D, reproduces the polynomial, together with extra boundary terms that cancel the divergent terms in the action. We conjecture that, in arbitrary even dimension, HR is equivalent to conformally completing the Einstein-Hilbert action with negative cosmological constant, plus the Euler term with fixed coupling prescribed by TR.

</details>


### [49] [ERG Kernels on Multiply Connected Configuration Spaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03750)
*Satoshi Ohya,Taichi Tanaka*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes how exact renormalization group (ERG) kernels in Euclidean field theory depend on the global topology—especially fundamental group representations—of multiply connected configuration spaces; it shows that ERG kernels decompose into weighted sums over the universal cover, with topological weight factors (unrenormalized Aharonov-Bohm phases) tied to flux quantization and WZW level quantization, and proposes a topologically enriched local ERG flow equation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how exact renormalization group (ERG) transformations encode global topological features—particularly for multiply connected configuration spaces—beyond infinitesimal flow equations, which ignore such topology dependence.

Method: Functional-integral formulation of Euclidean field theory; analysis of ERG kernels via universal covering space decomposition; use of one-dimensional representations of the fundamental group; interpretation of weight factors as Aharonov-Bohm phases in infinite-dimensional configuration space; derivation of a gauge-equivalent, locally formulated ERG flow equation incorporating topological data.

Result: ERG kernels for multiply connected spaces are expressed as weighted sums over the universal cover, with weights given by unrenormalized one-dimensional representations of π₁; these weights admit an Aharonov-Bohm interpretation linked to background magnetic flux; flux quantization yields a normalization condition equivalent to Wess-Zumino-Witten level quantization; a topologically augmented local ERG flow equation is constructed.

Conclusion: Global topology—specifically the fundamental group—imprints nontrivial, unrenormalized structure onto ERG kernels via representation-dependent phases; this topological data is physically interpretable as quantized flux in configuration space and fundamentally constrains consistent ERG transformations, bridging renormalization theory with topological quantum field theory concepts.

Abstract: In the functional-integral formulation of Euclidean field theory, exact renormalization group (ERG) transformations are realized by functional-integral kernels. Unlike the ERG flow equations that describe infinitesimal ERG transformations, these ERG kernels explicitly depend on the global topology of the configuration space. This paper explores this topology dependence for multiply connected configuration spaces. We show that the ERG kernel is in general given by a weighted sum of kernels on its universal covering space, where the weight factors are determined by a one-dimensional representation of the fundamental group. These weight factors are shown never to be renormalized under the ERG. We also show that these factors can be interpreted as Aharonov-Bohm phases with respect to a background magnetic flux penetrating the infinite-dimensional configuration space. From this viewpoint, a normalization condition for ERG transformations corresponds to a flux-quantization condition, which is equivalent to the level-quantization condition for Wess-Zumino-Witten terms in nonlinear sigma models. Finally, we present an alternative gauge-equivalent form of the ERG flow equation that incorporates this topological information locally.

</details>


### [50] [A Century of Group Theory in Particle Physics and Beyond](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05225)
*Paul Sorba*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper surveys the development and interdisciplinary impact of Group Theory in mathematics and physics over the 20th century, emphasizing its role in particle physics, statistical physics, and potential applications in theoretical biology.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To highlight the broad and growing significance of Group Theory beyond pure mathematics—particularly its foundational and methodological contributions across physics and emerging relevance in theoretical biology.

Method: Survey and conceptual analysis of historical developments and cross-disciplinary applications of Group Theory.

Result: Demonstrates that Group Theory is not only central to particle physics but also valuable in statistical physics and potentially applicable in theoretical biology.

Conclusion: Group Theory serves as a unifying mathematical framework with profound and expanding influence across multiple scientific domains.

Abstract: The development of Group Theory in Mathematics as well as its impact in Physics has been spectacular during the twentieth century, and more particularly these last fifty years. If its contribution to Particle Physics deserves a special consideration, its usefulness cannot be neglected in other domains like Statistical Physics for example, and might also be of interest in Theoretical Biology. Some of these aspects will be examined.

</details>


### [51] [From OPE Associativity to Gravitational Wave Signatures: Critical Dimensions and AdS/dS Transition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03969)
*Tetiana Obikhod*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper links algebraic consistency of the OPE in CFTs to observable log-periodic modulations in the stochastic gravitational wave background (SGWB) via AdS/CFT and analytic continuation to de Sitter space.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge abstract CFT consistency conditions—specifically OPE associativity encoded in crossing symmetry—with concrete, potentially observable signatures in cosmology, particularly the SGWB.

Method: Uses conformal 6j symbols as crossing kernels to extract anomalous dimensions γ_{n,J} from OPE crossing equation singularities; applies AdS/CFT to map these to bulk mass shifts; imposes Breitenlohner-Freedman bound in AdS; analytically continues to dS to identify complex scaling dimensions Δ_dS = d/2 ± iμ; derives resulting log-oscillatory structure in four-point functions and SGWB.

Result: A log-periodic modulation Δln f = 2π/μ in the SGWB power spectrum, induced by complex conformal dimensions arising when anomalous dimensions fall below a critical value γ_c, violating the BF bound upon dS continuation.

Conclusion: OPE associativity in CFTs—via crossing symmetry and anomalous dimensions—has direct, testable consequences in early-universe cosmology: detection of log-periodicity in SGWB would probe fundamental CFT data (e.g., μ) and validate holographic consistency across AdS/dS.

Abstract: We present a framework connecting the algebraic consistency of the Operator Product Expansion (OPE) in Conformal Field Theories to observable signatures in the stochastic gravitational wave background (SGWB). By interpreting the conformal 6j symbols as crossing kernels, we extract the anomalous dimensions $γ_{n,J}$ of double-trace operators from the singularities of the crossing equation. Through the AdS/CFT correspondence, these algebraic shifts are holographically reinterpreted as effective mass shifts in the Anti-de Sitter bulk. We identify a critical anomalous dimension, $γ_c$, which marks the threshold where the effective mass violates the Breitenlohner-Freedman bound. Upon analytic continuation to de Sitter space ($L_{\text{AdS}} \to iL_{\text{dS}}$), we demonstrate that crossing this threshold ($γ< γ_c$) forces the dual scaling dimensions into the complex principal series, $Δ_{\text{dS}} = d/2 \pm iμ$. This complex dimensionality induces characteristic logarithmic oscillations in the four-point correlators. In the cosmological context, these oscillations manifest as a log-periodic modulation in the SGWB, characterized by a specific spacing $Δ\ln f = 2π/μ$. Detecting this log-periodic structure with next-generation interferometers such as LISA would directly measure the parameter $μ$, closing the loop between abstract OPE associativity and observational cosmology.

</details>


### [52] [The Normalised Wigner Negativity Rate as a Second-Moment Probe of Infall in AdS$_3$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04065)
*Ritam Basu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces the 'Krylov-Wigner negativity rate' as a diagnostic for quantum operator spreading in holographic systems, linking it to the second moment of boundary state evolution; it derives an analytic Bessel-form expression for the seed-normalized Krylov-Wigner distribution, shows its negativity grows as sinh⁴Δ(πt/β), and identifies Δ = 1 (BF-saturating dimension in AdS₃) as special — only there does the negativity rate equal the tidal stretch rate ℛ ∝ ℂPρ, connecting quantum complexity growth to classical geodesic deviation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To go beyond first-moment diagnostics (e.g., spread complexity) and quantify operator wavepacket dispersion—i.e., the second moment—away from classical trajectories in holographic CFTs, thereby refining the quantum-to-classical correspondence near black hole horizons.

Method: Constructs the seed-normalized Krylov-Wigner distribution (Wigner transform of the descendant cloud with return amplitude removed); computes its exact negativity using Bessel-function asymptotics in the macroscopic limit; leverages exact negative binomial statistics of the Krylov chain and the Caputa et al. momentum dictionary; analyzes late-time asymptotics and dimensional dependence on conformal weight Δ.

Result: The normalized Krylov-Wigner negativity scales as sinh⁴Δ(πt/β); its time derivative (rate) matches the Krylov variance growth rate only at Δ = 1; at this BF-saturating dimension in AdS₃, the negativity rate equals ℛ = ℂPρ—the proper radial tidal stretch—establishing a direct link between quantum negativity dynamics and classical geodesic deviation.

Conclusion: Krylov-Wigner negativity rate provides a refined, geometrically meaningful probe of quantum operator spreading, with Δ = 1 emerging as a privileged dimension where quantum complexity diagnostics encode classical tidal physics; this suggests a deeper role for SU(1,1) symmetry—and potentially the transverse string size operator—in organizing Krylov structure.

Abstract: In spread complexity, the average position of an operator along its Krylov chain, recovers the right radial momentum of an infalling particle in AdS, yet it is a measure of the first moment, irrespective of the spread of the wavepacket away from its classical trajectory. The rate of a normalized Krylov-Wigner negativity can be proposed as a diagnostic of the second moment of the boundary state that captures this spreading. Starting with the \emph{seed-normalized} Krylov-Wigner distribution -- that is, the Wigner transform of the descendant cloud, with the decaying return amplitude divided out -- we obtain an analytic Bessel form in the macroscopic limit and compute its total negativity explicitly. Retaining the Bessel variable all the way through, we find that the negativity goes as $\sinh^{4Δ}(πt/β)$, while the raw, seed-normalized state negativity saturates, as dictated by the $O(\sqrt{D})$ bound. Using the exact negative binomial statistics of the Krylov chain and the momentum dictionary of Caputa et al.~\cite{Caputa:2024}, the rate of the negativity scales as the growth rate of the Krylov variance at late time asymptotics \emph{if and only if} $Δ=1$, the Breitenlohner-Freedman saturating dimension in $AdS_3$. This dimensionality is special in that the negativity rate is the product of the proper radial position and momentum, $\mathcal{R} \propto \mathcal{C} P_ρ$, i.e., the rate of the tidal stretch of nearby geodesic falling into the horizon.
