We comment on the direction for future research, in particular the interpretation of the transverse string size operator in terms of the Krylov number operator through the common $SU(1,1)$ discrete series.

</details>


### [53] [Infrared Divergences as Itinerant Vacua](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04115)
*Masahiro Morikawa*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper reinterprets infrared divergences (IRDs) not as pathologies but as signatures of an 'itinerant vacuum'—a quantum vacuum continuously transitioning among inequivalent states as a classical order parameter evolves; using an exact CTP effective action decomposition and Hubbard–Stratonovich transformation, it converts IRD-induced imaginary terms into classical stochastic noise, unifying phenomena across soft QED, de Sitter scalars, soft gravitons, and non-equilibrium phase transitions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reinterpret infrared divergences constructively—as physical signatures of vacuum dynamics rather than mere regularization artifacts—and unify diverse IR-sensitive phenomena (e.g., 1/f noise, gravitational memory, primordial fluctuations) under a common mechanism rooted in vacuum traversal.

Method: Exact separation of the infrared-divergent imaginary part of the closed-time-path (CTP) influence functional; application of a Hubbard–Stratonovich identity to convert this divergence into a classical stochastic source; geometric formulation via a Hilbert-space bundle over the vacuum manifold.

Result: Demonstrates that IRDs generate observable classical stochasticity—specifically 1/f current noise (soft QED), scale-invariant primordial power spectrum (de Sitter scalars), gravitational memory (soft gravitons), and non-Gaussian order-parameter fluctuations (non-equilibrium phase transitions)—all unified by the itinerant-vacuum mechanism.

Conclusion: Infrared divergences are not unphysical artifacts but fundamental indicators of vacuum non-adiabaticity and dynamical inequivalence; their conversion into stochastic sources provides a first-principles derivation of classical noise and memory effects without invoking coarse graining or decoherence.

Abstract: Infrared divergences (IRDs) are usually treated as pathologies to be cancelled, regularized, or hidden in dressed asymptotic states. This paper develops a complementary and constructive viewpoint: an IRD is the signature of an \emph{itinerant vacuum} -- a quantum vacuum that wanders continuously through a family of inequivalent states as a classical order parameter evolves. Each value of the order parameter carries its own coherent vacuum, so moving the order parameter means traversing a succession of orthogonal vacua. The IRD is the field-theoretic cost of this wandering, and the $1/f$ noise, gravitational memory, and non-Gaussian fluctuations that emerge from it are its observable classical remnants.
  The technical core is an exact separation in the real-time closed-time-path (CTP) effective action. The infrared-divergent imaginary part of the influence functional must not be left as a divergent coefficient in a deterministic equation of motion; it is instead converted, by a Hubbard--Stratonovich identity, into a classical stochastic source. This step is an algebraic identity of the generating functional and requires no prior coarse graining or decoherence assumption: the retarded kernel encodes the memory of past vacuum transitions, while the noise kernel encodes the quantum uncertainty of the next one. We apply this construction to four parallel arenas -- soft QED, scalar fields in de Sitter space, soft gravitons, and non-equilibrium phase transitions -- and show that the same itinerant-vacuum mechanism underlies $1/f$ current noise, the primordial power spectrum, gravitational memory, and order-parameter dynamics. A geometric formulation in terms of a Hilbert-space bundle over the vacuum manifold is outlined as an outlook.

</details>


### [54] [Finite-$N$ Operator Algebras and the Hilbert Space of Bilocal Holography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04143)
*Byoungjoon Ahn,Robert de Mello Koch,Vatsal Garg,Antal Jevicki,Junggi Yoon*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper provides an operator-algebraic and representation-theoretic framework for finite-N bilocal holography, identifying an invariant dual-pair algebra structure on the Fock space and showing that the singlet sector corresponds to a unique irreducible representation of a 'master algebra'; trace constraints become representation-theoretic identities, and partition functions are computed via characters.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a rigorous Hilbert space formulation of finite-N bilocal holography, extending prior finite-N construction (arXiv:2602.20788) by uncovering underlying algebraic and representation-theoretic structures governing the singlet sector.

Method: Operator-algebraic analysis of commuting actions of the color group and a bilocal Lie algebra on the Fock space; projection onto the singlet sector; classification of irreducible representations of the invariant 'master algebra'; derivation of finite-N trace relations as representation-theoretic identities; computation of Casimir invariants and characters for orthogonal, symplectic, and unitary cases.

Result: Identification of a dual-pair operator algebra structure; demonstration that the singlet sector hosts a single irreducible representation of the master algebra; translation of finite-N trace constraints (e.g., quadratic identities) into representation-theoretic identities; explicit Casimir values and character-based formulas for finite traces and partition functions across O(N), Sp(N), and U(N) cases.

Conclusion: The singlet space in finite-N bilocal holography admits a novel mathematical description as an irreducible representation of an invariant master algebra, unifying trace constraints, symmetry reduction, and partition function computation within representation theory.

Abstract: We give an operator-algebraic and representation-theoretic description of the Hilbert spaces finite-$N$ bilocal holography. This work is a sequel to the finite-$N$ Hilbert space construction of arXiv:2602.20788 [hep-th]. The central result is the establishment of an invariant dual-pair operator algebra: before imposing the singlet constraint the Fock space carries commuting actions of the color group and of a bilocal Lie algebra, while the projection to the singlet sector selects a single irreducible representation of the invariant Lie algebra, which we call a master algebra. The finite-$N$ trace relations, beginning with the quadratic identities studied here, are shown to become representation-theoretic identities of the selected irreducible representation. We summarize the orthogonal, symplectic and unitary cases, identify the corresponding finite-$N$ constraints, compute the singlet Casimirs, and explain how finite traces and partition functions are obtained through characters of the resulting irreducible representations. This provides a novel, previously unknown mathematical description of the singlet space.

</details>


### [55] [Solving for the integrable boundary states of the ABJM spin chain from $KT$-relations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04203)
*Nan Bai,Hui Yang,Mao-Zhong Shao*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates integrable boundary states in the alternating SU(4) spin chain from ABJM theory, solving integrability constraints via the KT-relation for n-site translationally invariant states; it distinguishes odd/even n cases (state vs. operator equations), analyzes chiral/achiral solutions up to n=4, and treats 1-site case with general operator-valued integrable pairs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct and classify integrable boundary states in the alternating SU(4) spin chain motivated by applications in ABJM theory, where such states encode protected observables and dual AdS boundary conditions.

Method: Uses the KT-relation to derive and solve integrability constraints for n-site translationally invariant boundary states; reduces constraints to state equations (n odd) or operator equations (n even); performs explicit classification for n ≤ 4, distinguishing chiral/achiral structures and treating the 1-site case with operator-valued K-matrices acting on an internal space.

Result: Complete classification of c-number integrable boundary states for n = 1–4, including chiral and achiral families; derivation of consistent operator-valued K-matrices for the 1-site case with internal degrees of freedom; structural distinction between odd- and even-n constraint formulations.

Conclusion: Integrable boundary states in the alternating SU(4) spin chain admit a systematic classification via KT-based constraints, revealing rich algebraic structure dependent on translational period parity and chirality; results provide foundational data for boundary correlators and integrable deformations in ABJM holography.

Abstract: We study integrable boundary states of the alternating SU(4) spin chain arising in ABJM theory. Starting from the $KT$-relation, we directly solve the integrability constraints for states with $n$-site translational invariance. For odd and even $n$, these constraints are reduced respectively to state equations and operator equations for the elementary $n$-site block and the matrix $K(u)$. We analyze chiral and achiral cases for $n\leq4$. In the 1-site case we allow general operator-valued integrable pairs with an internal space, while in the remaining cases we focus on $c$-number solutions.

</details>


### [56] [Electromagnetic Scoot for Dyons Revisited](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04246)
*Rasim Yılmaz,Onur Ayberk Çakmak*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper generalizes the electromagnetic 'scoot' effect—boost-like angular momentum shift in charged particle scattering—to systems with both electric and magnetic charges, analyzing its dependence on spacetime slicing (constant-time vs. hyperboloidal) and comparing it to the purely electric case within multiparticle state representations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how electromagnetic angular momentum redistribution manifests in scattering involving both electric and magnetic charges, and to clarify the apparent slicing-dependence of the 'scoot' effect observed in prior 1PM analyses.

Method: Post-Minkowskian (1PM) expansion applied to scattering of dyonic (electric + magnetic) charges; analysis of boost-like angular momentum conservation on constant-time versus hyperboloidal Cauchy slices; comparison with pure electric case using multiparticle quantum state representations.

Result: The scoot effect persists at 1PM for dyonic scattering but exhibits modified structure due to magnetic charge contributions; its apparent absence on hyperboloidal slices is confirmed, reinforcing that the effect is slicing-dependent rather than physical inconsistency.

Conclusion: The scoot effect is a gauge- and slicing-dependent artifact of how angular momentum is partitioned between particles and field on constant-time hypersurfaces; its generalization to dyons confirms robustness of this interpretation and underscores the importance of covariant slicing choices in PM scattering amplitudes.

Abstract: In the scattering of two electric charges, the particles acquire a shift in their net boost-like angular momentum, balanced by an opposite field contribution. This electromagnetic scoot effect appears at first order in post-Minkowskian expansion (1PM) order when conservation laws are evaluated on constant-time slices, but disappears at this order on hyperboloidal slices. Here, we extend this analysis to scattering involving both electric and magnetic charges and compare the results with the purely electric case in the context of multiparticle state representations.

</details>


### [57] [Entanglement of excited states after measurements in conformal field theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04268)
*Hui-Huang Chen*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper develops a CFT-based framework to compute post-measurement Rényi entropies for low-energy excited states after projective measurement on a spatial interval, mapping the geometry to a disk and expressing results as boundary-CFT correlation functions; it applies the formalism to the compact free boson CFT—yielding hafnian expressions for chiral current excitations—and reveals phase-sensitive interference in conjugate vertex operator superpositions, with proposals for numerical verification in the critical XX chain using free-fermion and multi-Slater determinant methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify how projective measurements affect entanglement structure of low-energy excited states in (1+1)d CFTs, particularly probing the interplay between measurement-induced geometry (slit with boundary condition), state excitation (operator insertions), and Rényi entropy.

Method: Uses Euclidean path integral representation with a slit geometry carrying conformal boundary conditions; applies replica trick and conformal mapping from replicated slit geometry to disk; expresses post-measurement Rényi entropy as normalized boundary-CFT correlation function; specializes to compact free boson CFT and computes explicit ratios for chiral currents, coherent J/\bar J superpositions, and conjugate vertex operators; proposes free-fermion and multi-Slater determinant numerical methods for the critical XX spin chain.

Result: Derives general formula for excited-state contribution to post-measurement Rényi entropy as boundary-CFT correlator; obtains current hafnians for chiral current excitations; finds phase-insensitive second Rényi ratio on infinite cylinder but phase-sensitive interference in finite-slit post-measurement ratio for conjugate vertex superpositions; provides concrete prescriptions for numerical testing in critical XX chain.

Conclusion: Projective measurement on a spatial interval induces a boundary-condition-carrying slit geometry whose entanglement response to excitations is encoded in boundary-CFT data; phase coherence of excitations manifests only in finite-size (slit) geometry—not on infinite cylinder—highlighting the role of measurement locality; the framework unifies measurement, excitation, and entanglement in CFT and admits exact lattice verification.

Abstract: We study the entanglement of low-energy excited states after a fixed-outcome projective measurement on a spatial interval in a (1+1)-dimensional conformal field theory (CFT). The post-selected measurement outcome is represented by a slit carrying a conformal boundary condition, while excited states are introduced by operator insertions in the Euclidean path integral. After mapping the replicated slit geometry to a disk, the excited-state contribution to the post-measurement Rényi entropy is expressed as a normalized boundary-CFT correlation function.
  We apply this framework to the compact free boson CFT. For the chiral current excitation, the relevant ratios are given by current hafnians. We also study coherent superpositions of $J$ and $\bar J$, and of conjugate compact vertex operators. In the conjugate-vertex case, the ordinary-cylinder second Rényi ratio is independent of the relative phase, whereas the finite-slit post-measurement ratio contains phase-sensitive interference terms. Finally, we describe free-fermion and multi-Slater determinant methods for testing these predictions in the critical XX chain.

</details>


### [58] [Stress stability criterion of the isospinning $\mathbb{C}P^2$ solitons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04413)
*Sergei Antsipovich,Yakov Shnir*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates the energy-momentum tensor, shear forces, and pressure distributions of stationary rotating topological solitons in a (2+1)-dimensional CP² sigma model with stabilizing potential, identifying parameter regimes where classical instability arises.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand mechanical stability properties—via stress tensor analysis—of rotating topological solitons in a CP² sigma model, which serve as field-theoretic analogs of extended objects like vortices or skyrmions.

Method: Analytical computation of the energy-momentum tensor for stationary rotating solutions; derivation and interpretation of internal shear force and pressure distributions; application of mechanical stability criteria (e.g., von Mises yield condition or positivity of pressure/shear ratios).

Result: Explicit distributions of shear forces and pressure are obtained; stability analysis reveals parameter-dependent classical instability—specifically, violation of mechanical equilibrium conditions for certain rotation rates or potential strengths.

Conclusion: Rotating CP² solitons exhibit classical instability beyond critical parameter thresholds, implying limitations on their physical viability as stable extended configurations in this field theory.

Abstract: We study the energy-momentum tensor of stationary rotating topological solitons in a (2 + 1)-dimensional $\mathbb{C}P^2$ nonlinear sigma model with a stabilizing potential term. We evaluate the distributions of the corresponding shear forces and pressure and study the stability criteria for these solutions. Our results suggest that these solitons become classically unstable for some range of values of the parameters of the system.

</details>


### [59] [Neumann scalars in AdS: partition functions and phases](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04417)
*Biki Bishwakarma,Astha Kakkar,Swarnendu Sarkar*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes one-loop partition functions for scalars in AdS$_{d+1}$ with Neumann boundary conditions, using analytic continuation from Dirichlet to Neumann conformal dimensions and contour deformation in the eigenvalue integral; it identifies and contrasts thermal phases with Dirichlet case, supporting findings via long-range correlator behavior.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand thermal phases and quantum stability of scalar field theories in AdS with Neumann boundary conditions, extending prior Dirichlet-based analysis and probing holographic implications for dual CFTs with multi-trace deformations.

Method: Analytic continuation of the conformal dimension from $\Delta_+$ (Dirichlet) to $\Delta_-$ (Neumann); contour deformation in the eigenvalue ($\lambda$) integral of the Laplacian spectrum; comparison with Dirichlet case from arXiv:2201.09043; analysis of two-point correlators' asymptotics to diagnose phase structure.

Result: Neumann scalar partition functions exhibit distinct thermal phase structures compared to Dirichlet ones; contour deformation enables consistent analytic continuation; long-range correlator decay confirms presence and nature of these phases.

Conclusion: Neumann boundary conditions induce qualitatively different thermal phase behavior in AdS scalar theories relative to Dirichlet, with implications for holography, stability, and dual CFT interpretation—particularly for double-trace deformations and critical phenomena.

Abstract: We present an analysis of one-loop partition functions for scalars in AdS$_{d+1}$ obeying the Neumann boundary condition and explore phases of scalar field theories in several dimensions at zero and finite temperature. The partition function computation involves an analytic continuation from $Δ_+$ corresponding to the Dirichlet boundary condition to $Δ_-$ corresponding to Neumann boundary condition. We show that this can be implemented by deformations of the contour for integral over eigenvalue ($λ$) of the Laplace operator as compared to the integral over ${\mathbb R}$ in [arXiv:2201.09043] for the Dirichlet boundary condition. We further contrast these phases with those appearing for the case of scalars obeying the Dirichlet boundary condition and corroborate the occurrence of these phases by studying the long range behaviour of correlators.

</details>


### [60] [Topological effects in the polarization of the Fulling-Rindler vacuum](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04483)
*A. A. Saharian,G. V. Mirzoyan,V. S. Torosyan*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies how compactifying spatial dimensions in Rindler spacetime modifies vacuum expectation values (VEVs) of a charged scalar field in the Fulling-Rindler vacuum, revealing off-diagonal energy-momentum components due to magnetic flux-induced phases, with distinct near-horizon and low-acceleration asymptotics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how topological features—specifically toral compactification with quasi-periodic boundary conditions (and associated magnetic flux)—affect quantum vacuum structure in accelerated frames (Rindler spacetime), bridging implications for black hole horizons with nontrivial topology.

Method: Analytic computation of VEVs of field squared and energy-momentum tensor for a charged scalar field in Fulling-Rindler vacuum; employs mode-sum regularization, separation of Minkowski-subtracted contributions, asymptotic analysis near horizon and in low-acceleration limit; incorporates quasi-periodicity phases as Aharonov-Bohm-type flux parameters.

Result: Compactification induces off-diagonal components in the vacuum energy-momentum tensor tied to compact subspace indices; near Rindler horizon, diagonal VEVs match trivial-topology Rindler results, while off-diagonal ones vanish there; low-acceleration behavior shows exponential suppression except for massless field with zero flux, where power-law decay occurs.

Conclusion: Vacuum structure in accelerated frames is sensitive to global topology and flux parameters; off-diagonal stress-energy signals topological magnetization; results apply to cylindrical and toroidal black hole horizons, enriching understanding of quantum effects in topologically nontrivial gravitational backgrounds.

Abstract: We investigate how the compactification of some spatial dimensions in Rindler spacetime affects the vacuum expectation values (VEVs) of the field squared and the energy-momentum tensor for a charged scalar field prepared in the Fulling-Rindler vacuum state. A toral compactification is considered with quasi-periodicity conditions on the field operator along the compact dimensions. The phases of these conditions are interpreted in terms of the magnetic flux enclosed by the compact dimensions. For a general number of spatial dimensions, the components of the VEVs are explicitly separated, corresponding to the expectation values in the Minkowski vacuum. As a limiting case, the VEVs are retrieved in Rindler spacetime with trivial topology. We demonstrate that, for non-zero phases in the periodicity conditions, the vacuum energy-momentum tensor exhibits off-diagonal components with indices in the compact subspace. Near the Rindler horizon, the leading terms in the asymptotic expansions of the field squared and the diagonal components of the energy-momentum tensor coincide with the corresponding VEVs in Rindler spacetime with trivial topology. The off-diagonal components of the energy-momentum tensor vanish on the Rindler horizon. For small accelerations, the difference between the VEVs in the Fulling-Rindler and Minkowski vacua is exponentially small. The exception to this is a massless field with zero phases in the periodicity conditions. In this special case, the difference decays according to a power law as a function of acceleration. As an application, we consider the VEVs near the horizon of cylindrical black holes and topological black holes with toroidal horizons.

</details>


### [61] [Symmetries and Conservation Laws in Lie-Poisson Electrodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04522)
*M. A. Kurkov*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces Lie-Poisson electrodynamics (LPE) — a non-Abelian, nonlinear deformation of electrodynamics with Poisson-bracket-defined gauge algebra — and shows it is dynamically equivalent to standard Maxwell theory via a field redefinition; it constructs corresponding symmetries (including deformed Poincaré), conserved currents, and proposes a quantization scheme.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore consistent non-Abelian, nonlinear deformations of electrodynamics that retain geometric structure and physical interpretability, particularly in the absence of charged matter.

Method: Geometric formulation of LPE; derivation of a non-trivial field redefinition mapping LPE equations of motion to Maxwell's; use of this map to lift Maxwell symmetries (e.g., Poincaré) to LPE symmetry generators and conserved currents; proposal of canonical quantization via the same field redefinition.

Result: A rigorous equivalence between vacuum LPE and Maxwell theory at the level of dynamics; explicit construction of deformed Poincaré generators and associated Noether currents; a well-motivated quantization prescription for LPE.

Conclusion: Vacuum LPE, despite its non-Abelian and nonlinear structure, is physically equivalent to Maxwell theory through a field redefinition; its symmetries and quantization are systematically inherited from Maxwell theory, supporting its viability as a geometric extension of classical electrodynamics.

Abstract: Lie-Poisson electrodynamics (LPE) is a non-Abelian and nonlinear deformation of usual electrodynamics, where the gauge algebra is defined through a Lie-algebra-type Poisson bracket on space-time. We focus on the geometric approach to LPE in the absence of charged matter. We establish a non-trivial field redefinition which, under mild technical assumptions, maps the LPE dynamics to that of Maxwell theory. Using this map, for any symmetry of the Maxwell action, we construct generators of LPE symmetries and the corresponding conserved currents. In particular, we obtain deformed Poincaré transformations. We also outline a natural quantization prescription for LPE based on our field redefinition.

</details>


### [62] [Kaluza-Klein tower thresholds and scheme dependence of the species scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04602)
*Yuri Michinobu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper clarifies the relation between two common definitions of the species scale in quantum gravity—perturbative breakdown and higher-derivative operator suppression—by analyzing cutoff-scheme dependence of one-loop Kaluza-Klein tower contributions; it shows that leading local corrections are regulator-dependent (hence matching data), while subleading logarithmic ones are universal, enabling a controlled EFT interpretation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguity in the definition of the species scale in quantum gravity by reconciling two prevalent characterizations—perturbative breakdown and higher-derivative suppression—within effective field theory, particularly addressing their apparent coefficient-level disagreement under different regularization schemes.

Method: Systematic analysis of cutoff-scheme dependence of one-loop corrections from Kaluza-Klein towers in effective gravity; distinction between regulator-dependent local threshold terms and universal logarithmic contributions using proper-time cutoff profiles; full computation of four-derivative one-loop corrections.

Result: The leading tower-enhanced local correction to the graviton propagator is regulator dependent and must be treated as EFT matching data; the subleading logarithmic contribution is universal across proper-time cutoffs; explicit four-derivative one-loop results cleanly separate these two types of contributions.

Conclusion: The perturbative-breakdown and higher-derivative definitions of the species scale correspond to distinct physical inputs in EFT: the former encodes scheme-dependent matching, the latter reflects universal RG flow; their coefficient-level identification is not regulator independent, resolving prior tension between the two characterizations.

Abstract: We revisit the species scale in quantum gravity from the viewpoint of effective field theory (EFT). Two characterizations are commonly used: one defines it as the energy at which the perturbative description of quantum gravity breaks down, as inferred from the one-loop correction to the graviton propagator; the other identifies it with the suppression scale of higher-derivative operators in the gravitational effective action. We clarify the relation between these characterizations by analyzing the cutoff-scheme dependence of the one-loop tower contribution. For a large number of species, the leading tower-enhanced local correction is regulator dependent and should be interpreted as EFT matching data, while the subleading logarithmic contribution is universal within the class of proper-time cutoff profiles considered here. As a concrete application, we compute the full four-derivative one-loop corrections from Kaluza-Klein towers. Our results separate regulator-stable logarithmic data from scheme-dependent local threshold contributions, providing a controlled EFT interpretation of the relation between perturbative-breakdown and higher-derivative definitions of the species scale and explaining why their coefficient-level identification is not regulator independent.

</details>


### [63] [Multi-Particle Contributions to the Celestial Algebra in the ${\cal N}=8$ Supergravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04611)
*Changhyun Ahn*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends Calkins-Pate's multi-particle OPE framework to N=8 supergravity, deriving 95 (anti)commutators of celestial soft currents and establishing nontrivial amplitude relations via contour integrals; it also verifies results using triple-collinear limits and proposes generalizations to N-particle OPEs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the recently developed multi-particle OPE formalism for celestial operators in pure Einstein gravity to the richer symmetry structure of N=8 supergravity, thereby uncovering new algebraic structures and amplitude constraints in the celestial holographic framework.

Method: Applies Calkins-Pate's construction of multi-particle OPEs to all fields in N=8 supergravity (graviton, gravitinos, graviphotons, graviphotinos, scalars); computes contour integrals over these OPEs to extract (anti)commutators of celestial soft current modes; cross-checks consistency via triple-collinear limits of 4D amplitudes; proposes generalized N-particle OPEs and associated mode algebras.

Result: Derives 95 explicit (anti)commutators for single-particle contributions to the celestial soft current algebra on the 2D boundary; establishes nontrivial relations among celestial amplitudes encoded in the multi-particle OPEs; confirms consistency through triple-collinear limits; formulates a conjectural extension to N-particle OPEs with linear (single-particle exchange) terms on the RHS.

Conclusion: The celestial soft algebra in N=8 supergravity is significantly enriched by multi-particle OPEs, revealing intricate boson-fermion interplay and providing a unified framework linking soft symmetries, amplitude factorization, and holographic current algebras—paving the way for a full celestial CFT description of quantum gravity.

Abstract: Recently, Calkins and Pate calculated the multi-particle operator product expansions (OPEs) of single-particle celestial operators with two-particle celestial operators in pure Einstein gravity. We apply their construction to the graviton, gravitinos, graviphotons, graviphotinos and scalars in ${\cal N}=8$ supergravity. By performing various contour integrals on these multi-particle OPEs, we obtain ninety-five (anti)commutators for the single-particle contributions from the celestial soft current algebra (derived from the single-particle OPEs) in a two-dimensional boundary. Moreover, these ninety-five multi-particle OPEs provide nontrivial relations between the corresponding celestial amplitudes. The triple-collinear limits between these bosonic and fermionic particles from the four-dimensional bulk are also described as a double check. Finally, we implicitly propose both the multi-particle OPEs of single-particle celestial operators with $(N-1)$-particle celestial operators and the corresponding (anti)commutators for the modes of celestial operators that have linear terms (or single-particle exchange terms due to the sum of $N$-particle factorization channels from amplitudes) on the right-hand sides.

</details>


### [64] [New insights on mutual information in the island approach to the Page curve](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04706)
*Anirban Roy Chowdhury,Souvik Paul,Sunandan Gangopadhyay*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes mutual information dynamics in the after-Page-time regime for eternal black holes, revealing a 'conservation' of geometric entanglement: vanishing $I(B_+:B_-)$ at scrambling time coincides with divergent $I(\mathcal{I}:R)$, indicating maximal entanglement between interior ($\mathcal{I}$) and radiation ($R$); it further introduces a novel calculation of tripartite mutual information $I(\mathcal{I}:R_+:R_-)$ on the Cauchy slice.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how geometric entanglement is conserved across subsystems in the late-time (after Page time) quantum gravity regime of eternal black holes, and to fill the gap in computing tripartite mutual information involving the black hole interior and radiation regions.

Method: Analytical computation and interpretation of bipartite and tripartite mutual information using quantum information-theoretic tools applied to holographic entanglement structure on a Cauchy slice; builds upon prior results on $I(B_+:B_-)$ saturation and extends them to $I(\mathcal{I}:R)$ and $I(\mathcal{I}:R_+:R_-)$.

Result: 1) At scrambling time, $I(B_+:B_-) = 0$ while $I(\mathcal{I}:R) \to \infty$, suggesting complementary entanglement conservation; 2) A new explicit prescription for computing the tripartite mutual information $I(\mathcal{I}:R_+:R_-)$ from bipartite data on the Cauchy slice.

Conclusion: The observed trade-off between vanishing and divergent mutual information supports a notion of geometric entanglement conservation in black hole evaporation; the derived tripartite mutual information formalism provides a new quantitative tool for probing interior–radiation correlations beyond bipartite measures.

Abstract: In this article, we have presented one of the very important observations, regarding the behavior of mutual information of two different sets of subsystems in the after Page time scenario. This provides us with some deep insights about the conservation of geometrical entanglement. In our earlier works, we have shown how the saturation of mutual information between two specific subsystems plays a vital role in obtaining the correct Page curve for the eternal black hole. In those works, we have shown that the mutual information between $B_+$ and $B_-$, that is, $I(B_{+}: B_{-})$, vanishes at scrambling time, which leads to the correct Page curve. That means that at scrambling time, there is no correlation between $B_+$ and $B_-$. Remarkably, it is observed that at this particular value of observer's time, the mutual information between $\mathcal{I}$ and $R$, that is, $I(\mathcal{I}:R)$, becomes infinity. This indicates that the regions $\mathcal{I}$ and $R$ become maximally entangled. This provides us with a notion of conservation of geometric correlation between different regions on the Cauchy slice. In this work, we have also provided a way to calculate the tripartite mutual information of regions $\mathcal{I}$,$R_+$ and $R_-$, that is, $I(\mathcal{I}:R_+:R_-)$ on the Cauchy slice using the earlier results involving the bipartite regions. This is a new result which was missing in the earlier literature.

</details>


### [65] [Entropy bounds, Geroch process, and the sign of deformation parameter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04905)
*Bijan Bagchi,Akshat Pandey,Parmest Roy,Sauvik Sen*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper examines how generalized uncertainty principle (GUP) deformations affect the Bekenstein entropy bound near black hole horizons in (3+1) and (2+1) dimensions, finding that negative GUP deformation relaxes the bound universally, while positive deformation tightens it—interpreted as a response to Planck-scale-modified near-horizon redshift.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate how Planck-scale physics, encoded in the generalized uncertainty principle (GUP), modifies the Bekenstein entropy bound near black hole horizons—particularly in light of Geroch's thought experiment of dropping systems into black holes—and to assess dimensional consistency of such modifications.

Method: Semiclassical analysis based on Geroch's horizon-dropping process, applied to (3+1)- and (2+1)-dimensional black hole spacetimes, incorporating GUP-induced corrections to near-horizon redshift and entropy scaling.

Result: Negative GUP deformation universally relaxes the Bekenstein entropy bound across dimensions; positive GUP deformation tightens it. The effect arises from GUP-modified near-horizon redshift, altering the energy-entropy relationship for infalling systems.

Conclusion: The Bekenstein bound is sensitive to quantum-gravitational deformations via GUP, and its dimensional robustness supports the physical relevance of GUP corrections near horizons; the sign of the GUP parameter determines whether quantum gravity weakens or strengthens holographic entropy constraints.

Abstract: Based on Geroch's process of dropping a system into a black hole from the vicinity of the horizon, we investigate in this paper the influence of deformation on the Bekenstein entropy bound both for (3+1) and (2+1) dimensions in the context of a generalized uncertainty principle (GUP). While providing a coherent framework for providing an upper limit on the entropy across dimensions we show, in the framework of a semiclassical treatment, that while a negative GUP deformation yields a universal relaxation of the bound, a positive deformation tightens it. Our results may be interpreted as a response to Planck-scale modifications of the near-horizon redshift.

</details>


### [66] [Revisiting the Wess-Zumino-Witten Term in Nuclear and Quark Matter under Magnetic Fields and Rotation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04929)
*Markus A. G. Amano,Minoru Eto,Muneto Nitta,Shin Sasaki*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives anomalous WZW terms for neutral mesons in dense QCD under magnetic fields and rotation, revealing topological couplings involving $\vec{B}\cdot\nabla\phi$ and $\vec{\Omega}\cdot\nabla\phi$, relevant to chiral soliton lattices.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the role of chiral anomaly in dense QCD matter subjected to external magnetic fields and rotation, particularly in relation to topological structures like chiral soliton lattices.

Method: Introduces electromagnetic, baryon number, and isospin background gauge fields to construct anomalous Wess-Zumino-Witten terms for neutral mesons ($\pi^0$, $\eta$, $\eta'$) in $N_f=2$ and $N_f=3$ QCD.

Result: Derives explicit topological couplings proportional to $\vec{B} \cdot \vec{\nabla} \phi$ and $\vec{\Omega} \cdot \vec{\nabla} \phi$, encoding chiral anomaly effects in rotating, magnetized dense matter.

Conclusion: The anomalous WZW terms provide a field-theoretic basis for describing chiral soliton lattices in dense, rotating, magnetized QCD matter, linking topology, anomalies, and collective mesonic excitations.

Abstract: We study anomalous Wess-Zumino-Witten terms associated with the chiral anomaly in dense QCD matter under magnetic fields and rotation. By introducing electromagnetic, baryon number, and isospin background gauge fields, we write down the topological couplings of neutral mesons for the $N_f=2$ and $N_f=3$ cases. The resulting terms contain characteristic contributions proportional to $\vec{B} \cdot \vec{\nabla} φ$ and $\vecΩ \cdot \vec{\nabla} φ$, where $φ$ denotes $π^0$, $η$, or $η'$. These results are relevant to chiral soliton lattices in dense rotating matter.

</details>


### [67] [The causal set reduction formula](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04980)
*Stav Zalel*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Derives a reduction formula and infinite tower of correlator relations on causal sets, enabling matrix elements to be expressed as a path integral or two distinct correlator sums; sketches an alternative LSZ-like formulation in flat continuum spacetime without explicit equations of motion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a framework for quantum field theory on discrete causal set backgrounds that avoids reliance on continuum differential operators and provides alternative representations of scattering amplitudes.

Method: Derivation of a reduction formula for matrix elements on causal sets; construction of an infinite hierarchy of correlator relations analogous to Schwinger-Dyson equations; algebraic manipulation to recast matrix elements in three equivalent forms (path integral, two correlator sums); adaptation to flat continuum limit.

Result: Matrix elements are shown to admit three equivalent representations: one as a causal-set path integral, and two as sums over correlators satisfying a tower of recursive relations; in the flat continuum limit, the method reproduces LSZ-like scattering amplitudes without invoking equations of motion operators.

Conclusion: The approach establishes a non-perturbative, operator-free formalism for QFT on causal sets, with structural parallels to continuum QFT, and offers a novel route to scattering amplitudes applicable beyond the continuum approximation.

Abstract: We derive a reduction formula for matrix elements on a causal set background. We derive an infinite tower of relations between correlators, akin to the Schwinger-Dyson equations of the continuum. Combining these two results we are able to express matrix elements in three different forms: as a path integral and as two distinct sums of correlators. We sketch the form that our method - which circumvents explicit use of differential equation of motion operators - takes in flat continuum spacetime where it provides an alternative expression for the standard LSZ result.

</details>


### [68] [From Galilei to Euclidean Carroll and the Alice Particle: The Times They Are a-Changin'](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05115)
*Eric A. Bergshoeff,Luca Romano,Jan Rosseel,Elena Simón Félix,Sara Zeko*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper unifies Galilean and Carrollian limits via 'Euclidean p-brane Carroll limits', introduces the 'Alice algebra' and 'Alice particle' as a Carrollian counterpart to the Bargmann algebra/particle, derives their actions from relativistic limits and null reductions, and links the Alice particle to a D0*-brane in type IIA* string theory.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify Galilean and Carrollian limits by generalizing p-brane Galilei limits to allow Euclidean worldvolumes—making time transversal—and thereby obtaining Carrollian limits where the speed of light vanishes in worldvolume directions.

Method: Analyzing p-brane Galilei limits with Euclidean worldvolumes; constructing Carrollian symmetry algebras (Alice algebra) via central extension; deriving particle actions via limiting procedures and null reduction; examining dynamics in (A)dS backgrounds; connecting to string theory via D-brane interpretations.

Result: Derivation of the Alice algebra as a central extension of Euclidean 0-brane Carroll symmetries; formulation of Alice particle action dual to Bargmann particle; stability analysis showing Alice particles are stable for positive cosmological constant (vs. Bargmann for negative); realization of both actions via null reduction from massless particles in spacetimes with one or two times; identification of Alice particle as decoupling limit of D0*-brane in type IIA* theory.

Conclusion: Galilean and Carrollian limits are two sides of a unified p-brane limiting framework; the Euclidean 0-brane Carroll limit yields physically meaningful Carrollian mechanics with centrally extended symmetries (Alice algebra), novel particle dynamics, and a concrete string-theoretic origin in type IIA* theory.

Abstract: In generalized, also known as $p$-brane Galilei limits, the speed of light $c$ becomes infinite in the directions transverse to a $(p+1)$-dimensional Lorentzian worldvolume. In this paper, we explain that allowing the worldvolume to be Euclidean, and thus time to be transversal, $p$-brane Galilei limits turn into Carrollian ones, that we refer to as "Euclidean $p$-brane Carroll limits", in which $c$ goes to zero in the $p+1$ worldvolume directions. This leads to a unified approach to taking Galilean and Carrollian limits, whose consequences we explore for $p=0$. We show that the spacetime symmetries that arise from the Euclidean 0-brane Carroll limit can be centrally extended to what we will call the Alice algebra, similar to how the Bargmann algebra centrally extends the Galilei symmetries. This gives rise to the novel notion of an Alice particle, and we obtain the Bargmann and Alice particle actions from a unified limit of the action of a relativistic massive particle or tachyon, suitably coupled to a one-form gauge potential. In the presence of a cosmological constant, we find that the Bargmann and Alice particles undergo stable motion for negative and positive cosmological constant, respectively. Finally, we show that the Bargmann and Alice particle actions can be obtained from null reduction of a massless particle action in a relativistic spacetime with one and two times. Our results indicate that in 10 dimensions, the Alice particle is a decoupling limit of a D$0{}^*$-brane in Hull's type IIA${}^*$ theory, similar to how the Bargmann particle is related to a D$0$-brane in type IIA string theory.

</details>


### [69] [Chern Character for Discrete Spectrum Partition Function](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05136)
*Shunrui Li,Yang Liu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes a geometric correspondence between thermal partition functions of quantum systems and the Chern character of a 'virtual physical sheaf' over spacetime, using $U(1)$-equivariant flows on quantum phase space and Grothendieck-Riemann-Roch theory to provide a topological foundation for thermal traces.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify spectral theory and characteristic class theory by giving a geometric, topological interpretation of thermal partition functions in quantum systems, particularly bridging operator-algebraic methods with algebraic geometry.

Method: Interprets Hamiltonian dynamics as a $U(1)$-equivariant flow on quantum phase space $\mathbb{CP}^n$, constructs a 'virtual physical sheaf', applies pushforward to spacetime, uses Weyl's asymptotic law to ensure trace-class properties in infinite dimensions, and employs Grothendieck-Riemann-Roch formalism to prove Chern character invariance under thermal compactification.

Result: The finite-temperature partition function is expressed as the spacetime integral of the Chern character of the virtual physical sheaf; this representation is invariant under thermal compactification on arbitrary manifolds and extends rigorously to infinite-dimensional Hilbert spaces.

Conclusion: Thermal partition functions admit a canonical geometric realization via characteristic classes, yielding a topologically robust, operator-algebraic framework for thermal traces in quantum field theory.

Abstract: We establish a rigorous geometric correspondence between thermal partition functions of discrete-spectrum quantum systems with bounded ground energy and the Chern character of "virtual physical sheaf" over spacetime. By interpreting Hamiltonian dynamics as a $U(1)$-equivariant flow on the quantum phase space $\mathbb{CP}^n$ and pushforward to spacetime, we show that the finite-temperature partition function emerges as the integral of the Chern character of "virtual physical sheaf" over spacetime. The construction extends naturally to infinite dimensions through trace class guaranteed by Weyl's aspmtotic law. Using the Grothendieck-Riemann-Roch formalism, we prove pushforward invariance of the Chern character under thermal compactification on arbitrary manifolds, providing a topological foundation for thermal traces in quantum field theory. This framework unifies spectral theory with characteristic class theory, offering a geometric interpretation of partition functions based on operator-algebraic approach.

</details>


### [70] [Effective Field Theory of Noncritical M-theory from Bosonization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05143)
*Patrick Jefferson,Tokiro Numasawa*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends the coadjoint orbit bosonization method to double-scaled non-relativistic Fermi liquids with an inverted harmonic oscillator potential, recovering known collective field theories in 1+1d and yielding a new effective field theory for 2+1d noncritical M-theory; it demonstrates integrability, computes fermion density correlators perturbatively, and links them to semiclassical Fermi liquid behavior—advancing toward a spacetime gravity description of noncritical M-theory.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop an effective spacetime gravity description of noncritical M-theory by extending bosonization techniques to higher-dimensional non-relativistic Fermi liquids, thereby bridging noncritical string theory, quantum gravity, and integrable many-body systems.

Method: Extends the Delacretaz-Du-Mehta-Son coadjoint orbit bosonization framework to double-scaled Fermi liquids with central inverted harmonic oscillator potential; employs gauge fixing to reduce the 2+1d theory to coupled chiral bosons; uses integrability of noncritical M-theory to compute fermion density correlation functions perturbatively.

Result: Reproduces Das-Jevicki collective field theory in 1+1d; constructs a novel effective field theory for Hořava-Keeler’s 2+1d Fermi liquid (noncritical M-theory); shows reduction to a family of interacting chiral bosons; computes fermion density correlators order-by-order and verifies agreement with semiclassical Fermi liquid predictions.

Conclusion: The coadjoint orbit approach successfully generalizes bosonization to 2+1d noncritical M-theory, providing evidence for its integrability and semiclassical consistency; this constitutes progress toward a holographic, gravity-like effective description of noncritical strings and 3d quantum gravity.

Abstract: We extend the coadjoint orbit approach to bosonization systematized by Delacretaz-Du-Mehta-Son to double-scaled non-relativistic Fermi liquids with central inverted harmonic oscillator potential. In 1+1d, this reproduces the Das-Jevicki collective field theory describing quasiparticle excitations of the $c=1$ matrix model. In 2+1d, this yields an effective field theory for noncritical M-theory, namely the 2+1d Fermi liquid proposed by Hořava and Keeler as a unified framework for characterizing noncritical string vacua. With a suitable gauge choice this theory reduces to a continuous family of 1+1d chiral bosons resembling Das-Jevicki collective fields coupled by additional interactions. Utilizing the underlying integrability of noncritical M-theory, we compute select fermion density correlation functions order-by-order in perturbation theory, and provide evidence that they agree with their Fermi liquid counterparts in a suitable semiclassical limit. This represents a step toward an effective spacetime gravity description of noncritical M-theory, which we expect to shed light on both the landscape of 2d noncritical strings and 3d quantum gravity in general.

</details>


### [71] [Thermal two-point functions in SYK and complex-time singularities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05258)
*Ilija Burić,Chi-Ming Chang,Ivan Gusev,Elizabeth Helfenberger,Andrei Parnachev,Mukund Rangamani*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies the finite-temperature two-point function of the large-N SYK model at intermediate couplings, analyzing its complex-time analytic structure; it identifies leading and subleading singularities governing short-time dynamics and potential holographic geodesic interpretations, persisting down to zero temperature, and discusses emergent geometry via kinematic space.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the analytic structure of the SYK two-point function away from the IR fixed point and its implications for operator complexity and emergent black hole geometry.

Method: Analysis of the finite-temperature two-point function's analytic structure in the complex time plane, tracking singularity locations across temperatures, and interpreting them via kinematic space and null geodesics in emergent geometry.

Result: The leading singularity lies on the imaginary axis, defining an effective temperature controlling short-time operator complexity; the next-to-leading singularity lies outside the thermal strip and may correspond to bouncing null geodesics; both persist down to zero temperature.

Conclusion: The persistence and positioning of complex-time singularities provide evidence for robust emergent black hole geometry even at intermediate couplings and finite/zero temperature, supporting a kinematic-space-based geometric interpretation.

Abstract: We analyze the finite-temperature two-point function of the large-$N$ SYK model at intermediate couplings away from the infrared fixed point. Specifically, we examine its analytic structure in the complex time plane, tracking the complex-time singularities over a range of temperatures. The location of the leading singularity lies on the imaginary axis. It controls the short-time dynamics of operator complexity, defining an `effective temperature' for the correlator. The next-to-leading singularity lies outside the thermal strip set by the above effective temperature. It has been argued that this could be interpreted in terms of bouncing null geodesics in the emergent black hole geometry. Both these singularities persist all the way down to zero temperature. We discuss our observations and motivate the related emergent geometry using a kinematic space perspective.

</details>


### [72] [Polynomial Initial-State Jumps and Christoffel Transforms in Krylov Complexity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05294)
*Abhishek Chowdhury,Ajit Prasad Mahapatra*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper develops an exact 'relative calculus' for Krylov (spread) complexity by analytically relating the Krylov basis and Lanczos coefficients of different initial states connected via polynomial filters, avoiding repeated Lanczos iterations; it leverages Christoffel–Darboux kernels, spectral measure modifications, and extends naturally to operator complexity via nested commutators.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To solve the 'relative initial-state problem': how Krylov complexity — defined by the pair (H, |K₀⟩) — changes when the initial state is transformed as |ψ_Q⟩ = Q(H)|K₀⟩/√N_Q, without re-running the full Lanczos algorithm for each new state.

Method: Uses polynomial filtering to induce a positive modification |Q(E)|² dμ(E)/N_Q of the spectral measure; exploits orthogonality to map Fourier-orthogonal-polynomial moments to shifted Krylov amplitudes via finite-band transfer; derives exact finite-sum expressions for amplitudes and projected Christoffel–Darboux kernels; generalizes to confluent roots, complex coefficients, support loss, and finite dimensions; extends to operator Krylov complexity via Liouvillian L and operator seeds Q(L)O.

Result: Exact closed-form formulae for individual Krylov amplitudes and cumulative spread complexity under polynomial filtering; unified treatment across three canonical Jacobi families (Charlier, Krawtchouk, Chebyshev); verification via Hermite central-limit scaling; matrix-valued parent measure formalism unifying finite-seed families; direct extension to operator complexity with nested commutators.

Conclusion: A solved cyclic Krylov problem serves as a computational anchor: all polynomially related initial-state dynamics (including operator complexity) can be generated exactly via algebraic 'Christoffel jumps', bypassing redundant Lanczos recursions and enabling efficient exploration of state- and operator-dependent complexity landscapes.

Abstract: State Krylov, or spread, complexity is a property of a pair $(H,\ket{K_0})$ rather than of the Hamiltonian alone. Thus, changing the initial state at fixed $H$ generally changes the Lanczos coefficients and the ordered Krylov basis. We solve this relative initial-state problem for normalized polynomial filters, $\ket{ψ_Q}=Q(H)\ket{K_0}/\sqrt{N_Q}$ with $N_Q=\langle K_0|Q(H)^\dagger Q(H)|K_0\rangle$. The filtered spectral measure is the positive polynomial modification $|Q(E)|^2\mathrm dμ(E)/N_Q$, and orthogonality turns this measure change into a finite-band transfer from reference Fourier-orthogonal-polynomial moments to shifted Krylov amplitudes. We derive exact finite sums for individual amplitudes and projected Christoffel-Darboux kernels for cumulative probabilities and spread complexity. The formulae cover confluent roots, complex seed coefficients, support loss, and terminal quotients in finite dimensions. We evaluate the construction in three canonical Jacobi families, the Heisenberg--Weyl/Charlier oscillator, the compact $SU(2)$/Krawtchouk spin, and the constant-coefficient tight-binding/Chebyshev chain, with a Hermite central-limit scaling of Charlier as a continuous-spectrum check of this Christoffel jump machinery. Finite seed families are organized by a matrix-valued parent measure whose scalar compressions recover the individual shifted problems. The fixed-inner-product construction carries over to operator Krylov complexity after the replacement \(H\mapsto\mathcal L\) and \(\ket{K_0}\mapsto O\); polynomial seeds then become nested-commutator descendants \(Q(\mathcal L)O\). The result is an exact relative calculus in which a solved cyclic problem generates a family of polynomially related initial-state dynamics without repeating Lanczos in the original Hilbert space.

</details>


### [73] [Cosmological Correlators in KLF and the Double-Exchange](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05327)
*Nathan Belrhali,Arthur Poisson,Sébastien Renaux-Petel*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces a Kontorovich-Lebedev-Fourier (KLF) space formalism to compute tree-level cosmological correlators in de Sitter space, replacing nested time integrals with frequency integrals over rational propagators and vertex functions; it derives analytic properties (singularities, residues, asymptotics) of Lauricella-based vertex functions and applies the method to the double-exchange diagram, yielding a compact double-series expression in hypergeometric functions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a systematic, diagrammatic, and analytically tractable method for computing tree-level cosmological correlators in de Sitter space without solving differential equations or evaluating cumbersome in-in time integrals.

Method: Uses the Kontorovich-Lebedev-Fourier (KLF) space formalism to recast in-in time integrals as frequency integrals; employs integral and series representations (via Lauricella functions) to derive analytic properties—singularities, residues, and large-frequency asymptotics—of general multi-field vertex functions; applies contour integration and residue calculus to evaluate tree-level correlators directly.

Result: A complete analytic computation of the double-exchange diagram yielding a correlator expressed as at most a double series over hypergeometric functions; clear separation of physical contributions (background vs. cosmological collider signal); explicit characterization of singular structure and asymptotics of generic vertex functions in KLF space.

Conclusion: The KLF formalism provides a powerful, diagrammatic, and analytic alternative to standard in-in and differential-equation approaches for cosmological correlators; the derived vertex function properties enable direct, residue-based evaluation of tree-level diagrams, enhancing both computational efficiency and physical interpretability.

Abstract: In this work, we present the procedure to find series representations of tree-level cosmological correlators using the Kontorovich-Lebedev-Fourier (KLF) space formalism. This framework allows us to trade the in-in nested time integrals for frequency integrals over rational propagators and vertex functions, which encode interactions among quantum fields on a de Sitter background. Because these functions are the key objects to understand in order to perform a diagrammatic computation, we derive their relevant analytic properties by using both their integral representation and series representation in terms of Lauricella functions. For a vertex involving any number of fields, we obtain the location of singularities, the corresponding residues and the large-frequency asymptotic behaviour. Gathering these properties at each frequency integration allows us to compute a tree-level correlator directly, without relying on the differential equations it satisfies. To illustrate this procedure, we provide a complete treatment of the double-exchange diagram. The computation naturally distinguishes the different physical contributions, whether to the background or to the cosmological collider signal. The newly derived result is expressed at most in terms of a double series over hypergeometric functions, which simplifies the analytical expression of the correlator.

</details>


### [74] [Does QFT make sense in non-integer dimensions?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05338)
*Diego Delmastro,Yan-Yan Li,Dikshant Rathore*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper re-examines analytic continuation of quantum field theories (QFTs) to complex fractional spacetime dimensions d ∈ ℂ, identifying branch cuts in theories like QCD and QED, and showing that operator scaling dimensions and OPEs exhibit discontinuous behavior across these cuts.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the analytic structure of QFTs under dimensional continuation—specifically whether and how standard gauge theories (e.g., QCD, QED) admit well-defined continuations to non-integer or complex d—and to probe implications for conformal data and universality.

Method: Analytic continuation of QFTs in dimensional regularization framework; examination of operator spectrum, OPE coefficients, and scaling dimensions as functions of complex d; identification of branch points and cuts via singular behavior in CFT data.

Result: QCD and QED exhibit branch cuts in the complex d-plane; low-energy CFT operators show discontinuous (jumping) scaling dimensions and OPE structures across these cuts, implying non-analyticity in d.

Conclusion: Standard perturbative QFTs are not analytic in d over the full complex plane; their dimensional continuation is multi-sheeted and requires careful treatment of monodromy and branch structure, challenging naive extrapolations to fractional dimensions.

Abstract: We revisit the old problem of analytically continuing QFTs to fractional dimension $d\in\mathbb C$. We observe that common theories like QCD and QED have branch cuts in the complex $d$ plane. In particular, many operators in their low-energy CFT have OPEs and scaling dimensions that jump as a function of $d$.

</details>


### [75] [Charge-Sector Construction of the Type-IIB Axion--Dilaton Wormhole Partition Function](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05385)
*Soo-Jong Rey*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs the Type-IIB axion-dilaton wormhole partition function from charge-sector data, expressing it as a Fourier series in theta with coefficients derived from saddle-point calculations and constrained by symmetry, positivity, and analyticity conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically construct and constrain the wormhole partition function in Type-IIB string theory using axion charge (flux) sector data, enabling control over its theta-dependence and consistency with quantum gravity principles.

Method: Saddle-point approximation in a fixed axion charge (form-field flux) sector; reduction of operator coefficient matrix C^{ij}_ν via reduction data b to scalars W_ν[b]; Fourier synthesis Z_wh(θ;b) = Σ W_ν[b] e^{iνθ}; analysis of constraints including discrete-symmetry covariance, moment positivity, Cauchy–Schwarz bounds, and asymptotic limits.

Result: Explicit form of the wormhole partition function as a theta-Fourier series; characterization of scalar coefficients W_ν[b] via rigorous analytic, algebraic, and statistical constraints; identification of the dilute Bessel/Skellam limit as a controlled asymptotic regime.

Conclusion: The wormhole partition function is fully determined by charge-sector scalar data satisfying a rich set of consistency conditions—establishing a framework for evaluating ensemble averages and addressing the global structure of the axion potential in quantum gravity.

Abstract: I construct the Type-IIB axion--dilaton wormhole partition function from charge-sector data. In a chosen axion charge, equivalently form-field flux sector, the long-distance saddle calculation supplies a two-end operator term with coefficient matrix \(C^{ij}_ν\). The labels \(i,j\) label end-insertion operators; the labels \(A,B\) label parent universes. Reduction data \(b\) convert this matrix into scalar coefficients \(W_ν[b]\). The wormhole partition function in the theta variable is \(Z_{\rm wh}(θ;b)=\sum_νW_ν[b]\e^{iνθ}\). I analyze properties and constraints this coefficients satisfy: discrete-symmetry covariance, phase, absolute bounds, moment positivity, Cauchy--Schwarz inequalities for the unreduced coefficient matrix, complex-\(θ\) domains, charge-lattice tails, and the dilute Bessel/Skellam limit. The \(θ\)-dependence of the wormhole partition function is the Fourier transform of the charge-sector scalar coefficients.

</details>


### [76] [Liénard--Wiechert fields in AdS and flat-space antipodal matching from geodesic-centered Coulombic data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05395)
*Sarthak Duary*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper provides a geometric derivation of Liénard–Wiechert fields in both flat spacetime and global anti-de Sitter (AdS) space, clarifying the origin and role of antipodal matching via coordinate choices adapted to timelike geodesics; it constructs a closed-form AdS analogue of the Coulombic field, proves its exact antipodal covariance, and shows its null-fringe limit recovers flat-space antipodal matching.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To geometrically explain the origin of antipodal matching in electromagnetic radiation fields—particularly at null infinity—and unify its understanding across flat space and global AdS, bridging conceptual gaps between asymptotic symmetries, gauge-invariant field components, and the AdS-to-flat limit.

Method: Uses geodesic-adapted coordinates centered on the timelike worldline of a uniformly moving (flat) or geodesic (AdS) charge; leverages embedding-space invariants for AdS; re-expresses static Coulomb solutions in non-centered frames to expose angular structure and antipodal relations; analyzes null-fringe limits and gauge-invariant field components (e.g., $F_{\rho\tau}$).

Result: Derives a closed-form, gauge-invariant Liénard–Wiechert-type field in global AdS with exact antipodal covariance; demonstrates that its null-fringe limit reproduces the standard flat-space antipodal matching between $\mathscr{I}^+_-$ and $\mathscr{I}^-_+$; provides an image-charge interpretation unifying Coulomb seeds, antipodal symmetry, and the AdS-to-flat limit.

Conclusion: Antipodal matching arises naturally from frame-dependent rewriting of static, geodesic-centered solutions—not from dynamics per se—and persists as an exact symmetry in global AdS; this geometric perspective unifies flat-space and AdS electromagnetism, reinforcing the fundamental role of timelike geodesics and conformal boundary structure.

Abstract: We give a geometric derivation of Li'enard--Wiechert fields in flat space and in global AdS, emphasizing the origin of antipodal matching. In flat space, the field of a uniformly moving charge is obtained by using coordinates centered on the source timelike geodesic, where the charge is at rest and the solution is the ordinary Coulomb field. The nontrivial angular dependence of the leading data at null infinity arises only after rewriting this static solution in a non-centered frame, making the antipodal matching condition transparent. We extend the same construction to global AdS, where uniform motion is replaced by motion along a timelike geodesic. Starting from the static Maxwell solution at the AdS center, we use geodesic-adapted coordinates and embedding-space invariants to reconstruct the field in arbitrary global coordinates. This yields a closed-form AdS analogue of the Coulombic Li'enard--Wiechert field, including the gauge-invariant component $F_{ρτ}$. We show that the bulk field satisfies exact antipodal covariance, and that its null-fringe limit reproduces the standard flat-space antipodal matching relation between the Coulombic data at $\mathscr I^+*-$ and $\mathscr I^-*+$. We also describe an image-charge interpretation of the Coulomb seed, giving a unified picture of Coulombic fields, antipodal matching, and the AdS-to-flat-space limit.

</details>


### [77] [Holographic heat engines for Schwarzschild black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03749)
*Ripunjay Dwivedi,Manus R. Visser*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs reversible black hole heat engines in asymptotically flat spacetime using quasi-local gravitational thermodynamics, enclosing a Schwarzschild black hole in a finite spherical cavity and identifying the boundary thermal system as the working substance; it derives exact efficiencies for Carnot, Otto, Diesel, Brayton, and Stirling cycles, showing the Stirling engine’s efficiency closely approaches the Carnot bound at high temperatures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore black hole thermodynamics beyond global asymptotic frameworks by constructing physically realizable, reversible heat engines in asymptotically flat spacetime using quasi-local definitions of thermodynamic variables.

Method: Quasi-local gravitational thermodynamics applied to a Schwarzschild black hole enclosed in a finite spherical cavity; identification of the boundary dual thermal system as the working substance; definition of thermodynamic pressure and volume via surface pressure and boundary area; exact computation of cycle efficiencies using quasi-local equations of state.

Result: Exact analytical efficiencies for Carnot, Otto, Diesel, Brayton, and Stirling black hole heat engines are derived; the regenerated Stirling efficiency is found to be exceptionally close to the Carnot bound and converges to it in the high-temperature limit.

Conclusion: Quasi-local thermodynamics enables consistent and reversible black hole heat engine constructions in asymptotically flat spacetime; the near-Carnot performance of the Stirling cycle highlights the utility of boundary-defined thermodynamic variables for probing black hole equations of state.

Abstract: We construct reversible black hole heat engines in asymptotically flat spacetime using quasi-local gravitational thermodynamics. We enclose a Schwarzschild black hole in a finite spherical cavity and identify the working substance with the dual thermal system on the cavity boundary. The surface pressure and boundary area define a thermodynamic pressure-volume pair in the dual system, while all coupling constants of the gravitational theory remain fixed. We derive exact efficiencies for the Carnot, Otto, Diesel, Brayton, and Stirling engines and compare them numerically. These efficiencies probe the quasi-local equations of state of the black hole. The regenerated Stirling efficiency remains remarkably close to the Carnot bound and approaches it in the high-temperature limit.

</details>


<div id='math-ph'></div>

# math-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [78] [Van Vleck spectra of high-order Heun operators:\ finite-band universality and exterior asymptotics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02700)
*Boris Shapiro*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper generalizes the classical Heun operator to higher-order differential operators with polynomial coefficients, studies spectral properties of associated Van Vleck polynomials, establishes a finite-band determinant representation for spectral polynomials, proves universality and weak convergence results for root-counting measures, and derives holonomicity and Picard–Fuchs equations for WKB periods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend classical spectral theory of Heun-type operators to higher-order Fuchsian differential operators and uncover universal asymptotic behavior of spectral roots independent of lower-order coefficients.

Method: Analytic and algebraic methods: finite-band determinant representation of spectral polynomials, asymptotic analysis of power sums, localization theorems for Van Vleck roots, Cauchy transform and logarithmic potential analysis, holonomicity arguments, and derivation of Picard–Fuchs equations via WKB theory.

Result: A finite-band determinant formula for spectral polynomials Sp_n; universality of normalized power sums depending only on the leading coefficient Q_k; locally uniform convergence of normalized Cauchy transforms/logarithmic potentials outside conv(Q_k); weak convergence of root-counting measures under determinacy conditions (e.g., collinear Q_k zeros); holonomicity of exterior Cauchy transform; explicit third-order Picard–Fuchs equation for k=3.

Conclusion: The spectral asymptotics of high-order Heun-type operators are governed primarily by the geometry of the zeros of the leading coefficient Q_k, with lower coefficients contributing only to lower-order corrections—establishing deep universality and paving the way for a mother-body conjecture in the complex case.

Abstract: We study high-order analogues of the classical Heun operator of Fuchs index one, \[
  \dq=\sum_{i=1}^k Q_i(z)\frac{d^i}{dz^i},
  \qquad °Q_i\le i+1,
  \qquad °Q_k=k+1. \] For a fixed degree $n$ we consider the linear Van Vleck polynomials $V$ for which $\dq+V$ has a polynomial solution of degree $n$, and we form the spectral polynomial $Sp_n$ whose zeros are the zeros of these Van Vleck polynomials. The main result is a finite-band determinant representation and the resulting universality theorem: after normalization, all fixed power sums of the zeros of $Sp_n$ have limits given by explicit constant-term formulae depending only on the leading coefficient $Q_k$. The lower coefficients of $\dq$ enter only lower order correction terms. Combining this with the localization theorem for Van Vleck roots, we strengthen the usual germ-at-infinity conclusion to locally uniform convergence of the normalized Cauchy transforms and logarithmic potentials on the whole exterior of the convex hull of the zeros of $Q_k$.
  We also prove a determinacy criterion: if the spectral roots are asymptotically confined to a compact set with empty interior and connected complement, then the finite-band moments determine the actual weak limit. In particular, when the zeros of $Q_k$ are collinear the root-counting measures of $Sp_n$ converge weakly to a probability measure supported on the corresponding segment; this limit is independent of all lower coefficients of $\dq$. Finally, we prove holonomicity of the exterior Cauchy transform and derive Picard--Fuchs equations for the WKB periods, with an explicit third-order equation in the first non-classical case $k=3$. The paper ends with a precise mother-body conjecture for the genuinely complex case, clearly separated from the unconditional results.

</details>


### [79] [Transition-Set Morphometry for Inter-Scale Contacts in Digital Sandstones](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02737)
*O. M. Kiselev*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes transition-set morphometry to quantify inter-scale geometric contacts (e.g., pore–throat, pore–fracture) in digital sandstones, demonstrating that weighted contact measures—especially adaptive ones incorporating local pore radius and transition dimension—significantly improve permeability prediction over conventional metrics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop reproducible, geometrically grounded descriptors of inter-scale contacts (e.g., between pores, throats, and fractures) that capture structural features relevant to transport properties like permeability, beyond standard pore-scale metrics.

Method: Introduces transition sets (e.g., T_VL, T_VF, T_LF) representing finite-neighborhood contacts among pore volume (V), medial pore-throat subsystem (L), and fracture porosity (F); computes their density, finite-scale dimension (d_VL), and weighted contact measures—including an adaptive measure Ĉ_VL^(d+1) = μ_VL (D̅_VL / D_*)^(d_VL+1); validated on 21 micro-CT sandstone samples from Imperial College and DRP-317.

Result: The adaptive contact measure Ĉ_VL^(d+1) substantially improved leave-one-out R² for log₁₀K prediction: from 0.752 to 0.881 across the combined dataset and from 0.287 to 0.807 within DRP-317; transition-set construction generalized successfully to fractured samples (DRP-5, DRP-31, DRP-285), confirming robustness for fracture-related contacts.

Conclusion: Transition sets constitute a reproducible, multiscale morphometric framework for quantifying inter-scale geometric contacts in porous media, with adaptive weighted contact measures offering superior predictive power for permeability compared to isolated topological or dimensional metrics.

Abstract: This paper introduces a transition-set morphometry for quantifying inter-scale contacts in digital sandstones. Pore volume, a medial pore-throat subsystem, and fracture porosity are treated as distinct geometric subsystems. Their finite-neighborhood contacts are represented by transition sets such as $T_{VL}$, $T_{VF}$, and, when a residual skeleton is resolved, $T_{LF}$. The method measures the density, finite-scale dimension, and weighted contact measures of these sets. A matrix-mode test was performed on 21 digital sandstone samples from the Imperial College micro-CT collection and Digital Porous Media Portal dataset DRP-317. Permeability, expressed as $\log_{10}K$, served as an external response for assessing how much structural information the descriptors carry. The transition dimension $d_{VL}$ alone produced little independent predictive gain, whereas contact measures combining transition-set density with local pore radius were more informative. The adaptive measure $\widehat C_{VL}^{(d+1)}=μ_{VL}(\overline D_{VL}/D_*)^{d_{VL}+1}$ increased leave-one-out $R^2$ from 0.752 to 0.881 in the combined sample and from 0.287 to 0.807 within DRP-317. Fractured datasets DRP-5, DRP-31, and DRP-285 were then used to test the transfer of the same transition-set construction to fracture-related contacts. The results support transition sets as reproducible morphometric descriptors of inter-scale contact.

</details>


### [80] [A new PBW basis for the alternating central extension of the $q$-Onsager algebra](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02849)
*Haoran Zhu*

Main category: math-ph
