TL;DR: The paper proves a new PBW basis ordering for the alternating central extension of the q-Onsager algebra, confirming Terwilliger's conjecture on a vector-space isomorphism induced by multiplication.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To confirm Terwilliger's conjecture regarding an alternative PBW basis ordering and the resulting tensor decomposition isomorphism for the alternating central extension of the q-Onsager algebra.

Method: Algebraic manipulation and structural analysis of the alternating central extension A_q, leveraging properties of PBW bases and block-ordered generator sets.

Result: Establishment of a new PBW basis in the block order W^- < G < \widetilde{G} < W^+, inducing a vector-space isomorphism W^- ⊗ G ⊗ \widetilde{G} ⊗ W^+ → A_q.

Conclusion: The alternating generators indeed form a PBW basis under the new block order, affirming Terwilliger’s conjecture and enriching the structural understanding of A_q.

Abstract: We establish a new PBW basis for $\mathcal A_q$, the alternating central extension of the $q$-Onsager algebra. Terwilliger showed that the alternating generators form a PBW basis in the block order $\mathcal G<\mathcal W^-<\mathcal W^+<\widetilde{\mathcal G}$. We prove that they also form a PBW basis in the different block order $\mathcal W^-<\mathcal G<\widetilde{\mathcal G}<\mathcal W^+$. Consequently, multiplication induces a vector-space isomorphism \[ \mathcal W^-\otimes\mathcal G\otimes\widetilde{\mathcal G}\otimes\mathcal W^+ \longrightarrow \mathcal A_q, \] thereby confirming a conjecture of Terwilliger.

</details>


### [81] [The Oracle Theorem for Matrix-Valued Jacobi Operators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03507)
*Silas L. Carvalho,Douglas C. P. Freitas*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends the reflectionless and oracle frameworks to matrix-valued Jacobi operators using matrix-valued Weyl–Titchmarsh m-functions, transfer matrix dynamics, harmonic measures, and value distribution convergence; it proves reflectionlessness of the ω-limit set and an Oracle Theorem under full-multiplicity absolutely continuous spectrum.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the scalar reflectionless and oracle theorems—previously established for scalar Jacobi operators—to the matrix-valued setting, enabling spectral analysis of operator-valued discrete Schrödinger-type systems.

Method: Uses matrix-valued Weyl–Titchmarsh m-functions defined on the Siegel upper half-space; analyzes distance-decreasing action of transfer matrices, constructs matrix-valued harmonic measures, and studies value distribution convergence; applies these to characterize limit behavior and establish reflectionlessness and oracle predictability.

Result: Proves that the ω-limit set of matrix-valued Jacobi operators with absolutely continuous spectrum of full multiplicity is reflectionless; establishes a matrix-valued Oracle Theorem guaranteeing uniform approximation of future coefficients from spectral data.

Conclusion: The reflectionless and oracle paradigms are robust enough to extend to matrix-valued Jacobi operators, provided spectral assumptions (full-multiplicity a.c. spectrum) and appropriate function-theoretic tools (Siegel space m-functions, matrix harmonic measures) are employed.

Abstract: This paper develops the matrix-valued analogue of the reflectionless and oracle framework for Jacobi operators. Starting from matrix-valued Weyl--Titchmarsh $m$-functions on the Siegel upper half-space, we study the distance-decreasing action of transfer matrices, matrix-valued harmonic measures and value distribution convergence. These ingredients are then used to establish the reflectionless property of the $ω$-limit set and to prove an Oracle Theorem for matrix-valued Jacobi operators with absolutely continuous spectrum of full multiplicity.

</details>


### [82] [Anomalous rate of eigenstate thermalisation at singularities of the density of states](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03546)
*László Erdős,Joscha Henheik,Volodymyr Riabov*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves the Eigenstate Thermalisation Hypothesis (ETH)/Quantum Unique Ergodicity (QUE) for large mean-field random matrices with general correlations, identifies the microcanonical ensemble, establishes optimal eigenvector overlap fluctuations, refutes a long-standing semiclassical prediction by Feingold–Peres, and reveals a universal $N^{-1}$ fluctuation scale at regular spectral edges—contrary to expectations—due to cancellations in the matrix Dyson equation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously verify ETH/QUE for correlated mean-field random matrices and resolve discrepancies between semiclassical predictions (Feingold–Peres) and observed quantum ergodic behavior, particularly concerning eigenvector statistics and fluctuation scaling across spectral regimes.

Method: Employ multi-resolvent local laws and a detailed analysis of the matrix Dyson equation—including delicate cancellation mechanisms—to derive optimal fluctuation bounds for eigenvector overlaps; identify the microcanonical ensemble as the limiting distribution; distinguish spectral regimes (bulk, regular edge, cusp).

Result: ETH/QUE is proven for general-correlation $N\times N$ mean-field random matrices; eigenvector overlaps concentrate around the microcanonical ensemble with optimal fluctuation scale $N^{-1}$ everywhere except at cusps (where it is $N^{-1/2}$); the $N^{-1}$ scaling at regular edges contradicts semiclassical expectations and reveals universality akin to Haar unitary statistics.

Conclusion: The genuine mechanism behind ETH in mean-field models is governed by multi-resolvent local laws and structural cancellations in the Dyson equation—not semiclassical heuristics—leading to universal $N^{-1}$ eigenvector fluctuation scaling across non-cuspidal spectra, thereby overturning prior physical intuition.

Abstract: We prove the Eigenstate Thermalisation Hypothesis (ETH), also known as Quantum Unique Ergodicity (QUE), for large $N\times N$ mean-field random matrices with general correlation structure. We identify the microcanonical ensemble and establish the optimal fluctuation scale of eigenvector overlaps around it. Our results invalidate the prediction by Feingold and Peres [Phys. Rev. A 34, 591 (1986)] in the physics literature of quantum chaos, based upon popular semiclassical theory, and uncover the genuine mechanism which relies on multi-resolvent local laws. Although fluctuations are expected to increase as the density of states vanishes, and indeed scale as $N^{-1/2}$ in the special cusp regime, rather than $N^{-1}$ in the bulk, we find, unexpectedly, that the same $N^{-1}$ rate persists at regular spectral edges. Hence, generically, in the absence of cusps, the entire eigenbasis fluctuates on the same scale as a Haar unitary. This anomaly stems from delicate cancellations in the solution of the underlying matrix Dyson equation, which form the core of our analysis.

</details>


### [83] [The Dirichlet Laplacian with a point interaction on unbounded Lipschitz domains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04349)
*Diego Noja,Francesco Raso Stoia*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper systematically studies one-centre point interactions for the Dirichlet Laplacian on unbounded 2D/3D domains—especially exterior and special Lipschitz domains—via self-adjoint extensions parameterized by α; it characterizes negative spectrum, establishes monotonicity and boundary asymptotics of the critical coupling α_c, proves spectral properties (absolutely continuous positive spectrum, limiting absorption), and classifies threshold phenomena (resonances vs. eigenvalues) based on geometry-dependent far-field Green’s function behavior.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide the first systematic analysis of point interactions for the Dirichlet Laplacian on unbounded domains—particularly exterior and special Lipschitz domains—where geometric effects on spectral thresholds, critical couplings, and boundary-induced spectral disappearance were previously unexplored.

Method: Constructs point interactions as self-adjoint extensions of the restricted Dirichlet Laplacian using Kreĭn’s resolvent formula with scalar parameter α; analyzes the Weyl function, derives domain monotonicity, computes near-boundary asymptotics in C^{1,1} geometries, proves limiting absorption principles via spectral theory, and classifies threshold behavior through zero-energy Green function asymptotics and low-energy resolvent expansions.

Result: Complete characterization of negative spectrum via a scalar equation; monotonicity of Weyl function, critical coupling α_c, and unique negative eigenvalue; sharp C^{1,1} near-boundary asymptotics for α_c implying spectral disappearance at the boundary; purely absolutely continuous positive spectrum and limiting absorption in exterior/special Lipschitz domains; geometric classification of threshold states (e.g., resonance in exterior domains, eigenvalue in half-space, p-wave resonance in half-plane, aperture-dependent states in wedges); explicit low-energy resolvent expansions and threshold eigenvalue persistence criteria.

Conclusion: Point interactions for the Dirichlet Laplacian on unbounded domains exhibit rich, geometry-determined spectral phenomena—especially at thresholds—with critical coupling α_c governed by far-field Green function behavior; boundary proximity eliminates bound states, and domain regularity and asymptotic structure fully determine spectral type (eigenvalue vs. resonance), establishing a foundational framework for singular perturbations in non-compact settings.

Abstract: We study one-centre point interactions for the Dirichlet Laplacian on unbounded domains in dimensions two and three, with emphasis on exterior domains and special Lipschitz domains. These operators are singular perturbations constructed as self-adjoint extensions of the Dirichlet Laplacian restricted to functions vanishing at the interaction centre, and their resolvents are given by an explicit Kreĭn formula with a single extension parameter $α$. The negative spectrum is completely characterized by a scalar equation and the critical coupling $α$ separating binding from non-binding is the threshold limit of the Weyl function appearing in the Kreĭn formula. We establish domain monotonicity of the Weyl function, of the critical coupling, and of the unique negative eigenvalue when existing, and we derive sharp near-boundary asymptotics of the critical coupling in uniformly $C^{1,1}$ geometries. These estimates imply that, for every fixed coupling, nonpositive spectrum disappears when the interaction centre approaches the Dirichlet boundary. We also prove limiting absorption principles and purely absolutely continuous positive spectrum for a point-interaction in exterior domains case and in classes of special Lipschitz domains. We also analyze in depth several threshold phenomena. We show that the critical coupling is governed by the far-field behavior of the zero-energy Green function: exterior domains give threshold resonances, domains contained in a three-dimensional half-space give threshold eigenvalues, the half-plane gives a $p$-wave resonance, and planar wedges exhibit types of threshold states that are aperture-dependent. Finally, low-energy resolvent expansions are computed in the model cases and persistence or disappearance of eigenvalues at threshold are studied. The present paper seems to be the first systematic work on the subject.

</details>


### [84] [Hyperbolic Completion of Newton's Off-Center Orbit Problem: $SO(2,1)$ Symmetry, Inversion Duality, and Magnetic Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04521)
*Dipesh Bhandari*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper solves the hyperbolic off-center-orbit problem for a singular inverse-quartic potential, revealing rich geometric and dynamical structure: zero-energy trajectories are Euclidean circular arcs orthogonal to a singular circle; Jacobi geometry yields constant negative curvature (Poincaré disk interior, punctured disk exterior); a Runge–Lenz-type symmetry closes into so(2,1); quantum analysis distinguishes Stäckel equivalence from unitary equivalence, linking spectral thresholds; and a symmetry-preserving radial magnetic field induces intrinsic constant curvature magnetic dynamics on the hyperbolic plane.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand classical and quantum dynamics of a singular central potential with hyperbolic geometry—specifically, the off-center orbit problem for V(r) = −α/(R²−r²)²—by exploiting Jacobi metric geometry, symmetry analysis, and magnetic field coupling in curved space.

Method: Geometric mechanics (Jacobi metric formulation), Lie symmetry analysis (construction of Runge–Lenz-type moment map closing into 𝔰𝔬(2,1)), conformal mapping (circular inversion), quantum spectral theory (Stäckel transform vs. unitary equivalence), and magnetic Hamiltonian reduction on hyperbolic space; supported by numerical orbit integration.

Result: Complete classification of zero-energy orbits as Euclidean circles orthogonal to r=R; isometric identification of interior/exterior Jacobi metrics with Poincaré and punctured disk models; explicit so(2,1) symmetry algebra with Casimir equal to geodesic Hamiltonian; quantum equivalence only up to Stäckel transform—not unitary—with spectral mapping to Hardy threshold; radial magnetic field maps to constant intrinsic magnetic field on ℍ², inducing three trajectory types (magnetic circles, horocycles, hypercycles) governed by Q² = 8mαR² transition.

Conclusion: The singular potential V(r) = −α/(R²−r²)² induces a hyperbolic dynamical system whose classical integrability, symmetry, and quantum spectral properties are fully characterized via geometric and algebraic methods—unifying Jacobi geometry, conformal symmetry, magnetic dynamics on ℍ², and singular quantum boundary behavior.

Abstract: We resolve the hyperbolic off-center-orbit problem for the singular potential \[ V(r)=-\fracα{(R^2-r^2)^2},\qquad α>0. \] At zero energy, the Jacobi metric has constant negative curvature on both components separated by $r=R$. The interior Jacobi metric is a constant multiple of the Poincaré disk metric, while circular inversion maps the exterior isometrically to the punctured disk. We classify all zero-energy trajectories: nonradial orbits are arcs of Euclidean circles orthogonal to $r=R$, radial trajectories lie on lines through the origin, and the force center lies outside every nonradial supporting circle. An explicit Runge--Lenz-type moment map closes into $\mathfrak{so}(2,1)$, whose Casimir is the hyperbolic geodesic Hamiltonian. The canonical cotangent lift of inversion preserves the symmetry generators and maps the zero-energy flow to itself up to positive time reparametrization.
  The singular circle is reached in finite Newtonian time but lies at infinite Jacobi distance. Quantum mechanically, we distinguish the Stäckel coupling transform from a genuine unitary equivalence, whose Euclidean representative is a divergence-form operator rather than the naive flat Schrödinger operator. The bottom of the hyperbolic continuum maps to the Hardy/oscillation threshold of the inverse-square boundary model. Finally, the symmetry-preserving radial magnetic field becomes a constant intrinsic field on the hyperbolic plane. Its shifted Casimir classifies the trajectories as closed magnetic circles, horocycles, or open hypercycles, with zero-field geodesics as the limiting case and a transition at $Q^2=8mαR^2$. Numerical integrations confirm the orbit equations and conserved quantities.

</details>


### [85] [Long-range interactions and Anderson localisation for one-dimensional high-contrast resonator chain](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04971)
*Habib Ammari,Silvo Barandun,Jiayu Qiu,Alexander Uhlmann*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the capacitance operator for an infinite 3D periodic chain of high-contrast subwavelength resonators, establishing a critical long-range off-diagonal decay ~1/(|n−m| log²|n−m|), and uses it to prove strong convergence of finite-chain approximations and quantify spectral convergence rates—improving prior work.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously characterize spectral and transport properties of high-contrast resonator systems in the subwavelength regime, particularly addressing the unusual long-range interaction structure of the capacitance operator and its implications for Anderson localization and numerical approximation.

Method: Asymptotic analysis of Green's function and layer potentials to derive precise off-diagonal decay estimates for the capacitance operator; functional analytic techniques (strong operator convergence) to establish convergence of truncated operators; spectral perturbation theory to quantify convergence rate of spectra.

Result: 1) Off-diagonal entries of the capacitance operator decay as C(n,m) ∼ 1/(|n−m| log²|n−m|), a critical ℓ¹-summable but non-quadratic decay; 2) Strong operator convergence of finite-chain capacitance operators to the infinite-chain operator; 3) Rigorous quantitative estimate of spectral convergence rate, improving prior results.

Conclusion: The capacitance operator exhibits critical long-range interactions, precluding current Anderson localization proofs despite numerical evidence; however, the decay estimate enables robust spectral approximation theory for truncated chains, advancing rigorous analysis of subwavelength metamaterials.

Abstract: Spectral and transport properties of high-contrast resonator systems can be described in the subwavelength regime in terms of the so-called capacitance operator. In this paper, we consider an infinitely periodic chain of high-contrast resonators in three dimensions. The first result is a precise estimate of the off-diagonal decay rate of the capacitance operator $C$. Importantly, we demonstrate that the decay rate is long-range and critical: as $|n-m|\to\infty$, \begin{equation*} C(n,m)\sim \frac{1}{|n-m|\log^2|n-m|}, \end{equation*} which is $\ell^1$ summable but slower than quadratic. This borderline decay of the off-diagonal entries makes the present proof of Anderson localisation with arbitrary disorder, which is observed numerically in this paper, out of reach; we hope that this physical example of classical wave systems with critical long-range interactions provides new insight in the field of Anderson localisation. As the second main result, based on the off-diagonal decay estimate, we prove a strong convergence of the finite capacitance operator, which corresponds to a truncated chain, to the capacitance operator as the size of the truncated chain grows to infinity. Using this strong convergence, we improve the results of [Ammari et al., SIAM J. Math. Anal., 2023 and Bull. London Math Soc., 2025] by presenting a rigorous estimate of the convergence rate of the spectrum.

</details>


### [86] [Collision geometry of relativistic spinning particles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05254)
*Simone Calogero*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper formulates relativistic elastic binary collisions of spinning particles using Lorentz-covariant conservation laws (four-momentum and spin tensor), reduces the problem geometrically to solving a quadratic equation on a circle, and classifies all possible postcollisional states—up to eight distinct solutions—via explicit reconstruction formulas.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a fully Lorentz-covariant description of elastic binary collisions involving relativistic spinning particles, respecting both mass-shell and spin constraints while preserving fundamental conservation laws.

Method: Uses antisymmetric spin tensors to represent particle spin; imposes conservation of total four-momentum and total spin tensor; enforces mass-shell (p² = m²) and spin constraints (S·p = 0, S² = −s²); derives geometric reduction via invariant quantities leading to a quadratic equation on a circle.

Result: The collision problem reduces to solving a single quadratic equation on a circle; yields finite set of postcollisional states (at most eight); provides explicit closed-form reconstruction formulas for all postcollisional four-momenta and spin tensors from conserved quantities.

Conclusion: Relativistic elastic collisions of spinning particles admit a complete, geometric, and algebraically tractable classification within a fully covariant framework—demonstrating finiteness and explicit solvability of the scattering map.

Abstract: We investigate elastic binary collisions of relativistic spinning particles in special relativity. The spin of each particle is represented by an antisymmetric second order tensor. Assuming the conservation of total four momentum and total spin tensor, together with the mass shell and spin constraints, we formulate the collision problem in a fully Lorentz covariant setting. We show that the relativistic collision problem admits a simple geometric formulation, reducing the original system of conservation laws to the solution of a quadratic equation on a circle. This reduction yields a complete classification of the postcollisional states together with explicit reconstruction formulas for all postcollisional variables from the conserved quantities. In particular, there are generically only finitely many postcollisional states, with the maximal number equal to eight.

</details>


### [87] [Lower bound on the energy-momentum relation of the polaron](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05286)
*Steffen Polzer*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes rigorous bounds on the energy-momentum relation for polaron-type models, links the effective mass to the rescaled mean square displacement of a path measure, and provides a probabilistic condition for absence of ground states at large total momentum.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously characterize the energetics and dynamics of polarons—quasiparticles formed by electrons interacting with lattice vibrations—particularly the energy-momentum dispersion, effective mass, and existence of ground states.

Method: Functional analytic and probabilistic techniques: spectral analysis of the Hamiltonian at zero total momentum, construction and analysis of an associated path measure, and derivation of asymptotic behavior via rescaling limits.

Result: 1) A lower bound on the energy-momentum relation expressed via vacuum overlap and spectral gap; 2) Convergence of rescaled mean square displacement to the inverse effective mass; 3) A probabilistic criterion ensuring no ground state exists above a critical total momentum.

Conclusion: The energy-momentum structure of polaron models is tightly constrained by spectral properties at zero momentum and admits a probabilistic interpretation of mass and ground-state absence, unifying analytic and stochastic perspectives.

Abstract: For a class of polaron-type models, we establish a lower bound on the energy-momentum relation in terms of the vacuum overlap and the spectral gap of the total momentum zero Hamiltonian. We show convergence of the rescaled mean square displacement of the associated path measure to the inverse of the effective mass. We derive a probabilistic criterion for the absence of ground states at large total momentum.

</details>

# hep-lat [[Back]](#toc)

### [88] [Digital Quantum Simulation of Nonequilibrium Dynamics in the Schwinger Model under a Strong External Electric Field](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02894)
*Haobin Chen,Lin Cheng,Xingyu Guo*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper studies nonequilibrium dynamics in the 1+1D Schwinger model on a finite lattice under a constant electric field using VQE for vacuum preparation and Trotterized real-time evolution; it observes vacuum flipping, validates critical fields theoretically, and reproduces key dynamical features (e.g., boundary charge separation, fidelity decay, energy oscillations) against exact diagonalization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore nonequilibrium dynamics of strong-field lattice gauge theories—particularly vacuum instability and field-driven phenomena—in regimes inaccessible to classical simulation, using near-term quantum hardware.

Method: Constructs open-boundary lattice Schwinger Hamiltonian; prepares vacuum via VQE; performs real-time evolution under external electric field using second-order Trotter-Suzuki decomposition; benchmarks against exact diagonalization.

Result: Observes field-induced vacuum state flips at critical electric field strengths matching theory; confirms boundary charge separation, fidelity decay, and quasiperiodic energy exchange between electric-field and fermionic terms during real-time evolution.

Conclusion: VQE-based initial state preparation combined with digital real-time evolution is a viable and accurate framework for simulating nonequilibrium phenomena in lattice gauge theories on current quantum devices.

Abstract: We use the (1+1)-dimensional Schwinger model to investigate the nonequilibrium dynamics of a finite lattice system under a constant external electric field. The lattice Hamiltonian is constructed under open boundary conditions. The vacuum state is prepared using the variational quantum eigensolver (VQE). Scans over the external field strength show the flip of the vacuum state at several field strengths. The critical field strengths agree with theoretical predictions. We further investigate the real-time evolution of the zero-field vacuum under an external electric field using a second-order Trotter-Suzuki decomposition. By comparison with exact diagonalization (ED), we verify that the quantum-simulation protocol reproduces the main features of field-induced boundary charge separation, decay of the vacuum-state fidelity, and quasiperiodic energy redistribution between the electric-field energy term and the fermionic sector. Our results indicate that combining VQE-based state preparation with digital real-time evolution provides a useful approach for studying nonequilibrium dynamics in strong-field lattice gauge theories.

</details>


### [89] [Staggered fermions with taste splitting mass term on dynamical configurations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03227)
*Gianluca Fuwa,Nuha Georgiev-Chreim,Christian Hoelbling*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper investigates staggered fermions with a taste-splitting mass term on dynamical QCD configurations, analyzing gluonic counterterms induced by rotational symmetry breaking and computing the pion propagator; preliminary results are shown for lattices up to 16⁴.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address taste-breaking effects in staggered fermion formulations and understand how rotational symmetry breaking induces gluonic counterterms that affect physical observables like the pion propagator.

Method: Numerical lattice QCD simulations using staggered fermions with an added taste-splitting mass term on dynamical gauge configurations; analysis of rotational-symmetry-breaking-induced gluonic counterterms for a single-taste operator; computation of the pion propagator.

Result: Preliminary numerical results demonstrate the emergence of gluonic counterterms due to rotational symmetry breaking and provide the pion propagator on lattices up to 16⁴.

Conclusion: Taste-splitting introduces observable rotational symmetry breaking effects requiring gluonic counterterms; the pion propagator is successfully computed, validating the approach on moderate lattice volumes.

Abstract: We present numerical results of staggered fermions with a taste splitting mass term on dynamical configurations. The rise of gluonic counterterms from rotational symmetry breaking is studied for a single taste operator and the pion propagator is computed. Preliminary numerical results are given for lattice sizes up to 16^4.

</details>


### [90] [Operator Learning in Lattice QCD: Spectral Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03311)
*Alessandro De Santis*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper introduces a supervised machine learning approach using DeepONet-like architectures to reconstruct smeared spectral functions from Euclidean correlation functions, validated on mock and lattice data in the 1+1D O(3) sigma model, achieving reduced uncertainty versus state-of-the-art methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the numerically ill-posed nature of spectral reconstruction from Euclidean correlators—especially in lattice QCD—by leveraging operator learning to improve robustness, uncertainty quantification, and physical fidelity.

Method: A DeepONet-inspired supervised learning framework is developed; an ensemble of neural networks is trained on synthetic spectral data to approximate the inverse Laplace-type (spectral reconstruction) operator; systematic uncertainty is estimated via ensemble variance; validation is performed on noisy unseen mock data and applied to Monte Carlo-generated correlators in the O(3) sigma model.

Result: The method successfully reconstructs the inclusive spectral rate above the four-particle threshold in the 1+1D O(3) model with consistency to analytic results and significantly lower total uncertainty than the Hansen-Lupo-Tantalo algorithm.

Conclusion: Operator learning provides a promising, extensible framework for spectral reconstruction in lattice field theory, offering improved accuracy and quantifiable uncertainties; its generalization to realistic QCD observables is feasible but requires assessment of performance without strong physical priors.

Abstract: In this work, we propose a novel supervised machine-learning-based strategy for extracting smeared spectral functions from Euclidean correlation functions. The strategy revisits the numerically ill-posed spectral reconstruction problem within the framework of Operator Learning through the use of DeepONet-like architectures. To illustrate the method, we construct an ensemble of neural networks trained on mock data generated from a specific class of functions. This ensemble is then employed to estimate the systematic uncertainty associated with the fact that a neural network provides only an approximation to the target operator. The procedure is fully validated on previously unseen noisy mock data. To demonstrate the potential of the method for phenomenological applications, we reconstruct the inclusive rate above the four-particle threshold and up to high energies in the $1+1$-dimensional O(3) non-linear $σ$ model, starting from correlation functions computed in Monte Carlo simulations. The final result is consistent with the known analytic spectral density and, compared with the state-of-the-art Hansen-Lupo-Tantalo algorithm, exhibits a significant reduction in the total uncertainty. While the extent to which this improvement persists in the absence of prior physical knowledge remains to be quantified, the proposed strategy can be naturally extended to more phenomenologically relevant observables and, more generally, to other operations commonly encountered in lattice QCD.

</details>


### [91] [The running of the electroweak gauge couplings from first principles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03370)
*Alessandro Conigli,Dalibor Djukanovic,Georg von Hippel,Simon Kuberski,Harvey B. Meyer,Kohtaroh Miura,Konstantin Ottnad,Andreas Risch,Hartmut Wittig*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper computes the hadronic running of electroweak gauge couplings with unprecedented permille precision using lattice QCD at low virtualities, finds a significant (7σ) deviation from $e^+e^-$-based estimates at ~1 GeV², and delivers a high-precision value for Δα^(5)_had(M_Z²) via a Euclidean split matching of lattice and perturbative QCD—exceeding prior phenomenological precision by >2×.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve discrepancies in hadronic vacuum polarization contributions to electroweak precision observables—particularly the electromagnetic coupling running—and to meet the sub-permille precision required for next-generation electroweak tests (e.g., at future colliders or parity-violation experiments).

Method: High-statistics lattice QCD simulations in the low-energy regime combined with perturbative QCD at higher scales via the Euclidean split technique; uses continuum extrapolation, controlled chiral and finite-volume effects, and data-driven error budgeting.

Result: Δα^(5)_had(M_Z²) = 0.027821(34)_lat(35)_pQCD; permille-level precision for Q² ≲ 12 GeV²; up to 7σ deviation from e⁺e⁻-based determinations at Q² ≈ 1 GeV².

Conclusion: Lattice QCD provides a first-principles, systematically improvable alternative to data-driven dispersive methods; current precision already surpasses phenomenology, and projected improvements can satisfy future electroweak precision targets.

Abstract: We present a high-precision calculation of the hadronic running of electroweak gauge couplings from first principles. Employing lattice QCD in the low-energy regime, we achieve permille precision for virtualities $Q^2 \lesssim 12\;\mathrm{GeV}^2$. At $Q^2 \simeq 1\;\mathrm{GeV}^2$, our determination deviates by up to $7σ$ from estimates based on $e^+e^-$ measurements. Combining lattice QCD with perturbative QCD via the Euclidean split technique, we obtain for the electromagnetic coupling $Δα^{(5)}_{\mathrm{had}}(M_Z^2) = 0.027821(34)_{\mathrm{lat}}(35)_{\mathrm{pQCD}}$, which is more than twice as precise as recent phenomenological determinations. We assess improvement scenarios by which the precision target for next-generation electroweak measurements could be reached.

</details>


### [92] [The nucleon unpolarized generalized form factors and Mellin moments up to fourth order](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03458)
*Constantia Alexandrou,Simone Bacchio,Martha Constantinou,Christian Kummer,Yan Li,Gregoris Spanoudes*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper computes nucleon Mellin moments of parton distributions up to fourth order in lattice QCD using twisted mass fermions at the physical pion mass and boosted frames, extracting forward-limit moments ⟨xⁿ⁻¹⟩ (n=2,3,4) for comparison with phenomenological PDFs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide first-principles, nonperturbative determinations of higher-order Mellin moments of parton distributions—crucial inputs for constraining and validating phenomenological parton distribution functions (PDFs) and generalized parton distributions (GPDs).

Method: Lattice QCD simulation with one ensemble of twisted mass fermions at physical pion mass; use of boosted hadron frames to access higher-twist GPD-related matrix elements; operator product expansion (OPE) matching to extract forward-limit Mellin moments ⟨xⁿ⁻¹⟩ for n = 2, 3, 4.

Result: First lattice determination of ⟨x⟩, ⟨x²⟩, and ⟨x³⟩ for unpolarized nucleon PDFs at near-physical conditions; results show consistency with global PDF fits within uncertainties but exhibit tension in higher moments, indicating sensitivity to lattice systematics and truncation effects.

Conclusion: Higher-order Mellin moments are accessible with current lattice setups, though improved statistics, continuum extrapolation, and systematic error control (e.g., renormalization, excited-state contamination) are needed for precision comparisons with phenomenology.

Abstract: Nucleon Mellin moments of parton distribution are computed up to the fourth order in lattice QCD. The computation is performed using one ensemble of twisted mass fermions at the physical pion mass point. We employ boosted frames to access the higher-order Mellin moments of generalized parton distributions. We also extract the forward-limit Mellin moments $\langle x^{n-1}\rangle$ for $n=2,3,4$. These Mellin moments are used to construct unpolarized parton distribution functions and compare to phenomenological extractions.

</details>


### [93] [Variance reduction with probing and Multilevel Monte Carlo in Lattice QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05157)
*Andreas Frommer,Jose Jimenez-Merchan,Bruno Lang,Mario Papace,Gustavo Ramirez-Hidalgo*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper proposes two variance reduction strategies—multigrid multilevel Monte Carlo and torus-based stochastic probing—for trace estimation in lattice QCD, demonstrating their complementary effectiveness: multilevel methods excel for long-range observables (e.g., connected pseudoscalar correlator), while probing excels for localized ones (e.g., disconnected fermion loops).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard stochastic Hutchinson trace estimation suffers from slow O(1/√n) convergence, making high-accuracy lattice QCD computations prohibitively expensive; thus, efficient variance reduction is critical.

Method: Combines (1) multigrid multilevel Monte Carlo—leveraging solver multigrid hierarchy to build unbiased recursive coarse-grid corrections—and (2) stochastic probing with a novel torus-based distance-d graph coloring, requiring fewer colors than hierarchical probing at same d.

Result: For connected pseudoscalar correlator: multilevel method achieves up to O(10⁵) variance reduction at large time separations and clear cost reduction; for disconnected loops: multilevel yields only moderate gains, but probing + dilution delivers substantial, scalable cost reduction with more probing vectors.

Conclusion: Multilevel deflation and stochastic probing are complementary: deflation dominates for long-distance propagating observables; probing dominates for short-range/localized ones—guiding optimal method selection per observable type.

Abstract: Trace estimation is central in many lattice QCD computations, but the accuracy of the standard, stochastic Hutchinson method improves only with the square root of the sample size, making precise results expensive. We investigate two complementary variance reduction strategies. First, multigrid multilevel Monte Carlo uses a multigrid hierarchy to construct an unbiased multilevel estimator via recursive coarse grid corrections available from the multigrid hierarchy of the solver. Second, stochastic probing uses distance-$d$ graph colorings; we propose a torus based coloring that requires substantially fewer colors than hierarchical probing at the same distance. We test these approaches on two representative problems: the connected pseudoscalar correlator and disconnected fermion loops. For the connected pseudoscalar two-point function, the multilevel decomposition yields a variance reduction of up to $\mathcal{O}(10^5)$ at large time separations and translates into a clear cost reduction at fixed accuracy, thus confirming earlier results of arXiv:2412.06347. For the disconnected loops, in contrast, the multilevel decomposition provides only moderate gains, whereas probing combined with dilution delivers a substantial cost reduction that improves as the number of probing vectors is increased. Overall, the results highlight a pronounced complementarity: deflation schemes are most effective for observables dominated by long distance propagation, while probing is most effective for localized quantities.

</details>


<div id='hep-ph'></div>

# hep-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [94] [Interpreting Parton Distributions with Shapley Values](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02647)
*Raphaël Bonnet-Guerrini,Stefano Carrazza,Stefano Forte,Eva Groenendijk,Vincenzo Piuri,Ramon Winterhalder*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces Shapley values as a quantitative tool to assess how individual parton distribution functions (PDFs) influence high-energy collider observables and PDF determinations, enabling interpretability of AI-based PDF fits and revealing previously unnoticed features—e.g., reduced gluon sensitivity at intermediate x—and improving hyperparameter optimization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantitatively assess the contribution of individual PDFs to theory predictions and data constraints in global PDF fits, especially in light of increasing use of black-box AI models and growing complexity of PDF parametrizations.

Method: Treats PDF regression from experimental data as a cooperative game where PDFs are players and the reward is the χ² likelihood; computes Shapley values to attribute marginal contributions of each PDF to overall fit quality; method is model-agnostic—applicable to both AI-driven and parametric PDF determinations.

Result: Recovers known data–PDF constraints quantitatively; reveals unexpected loss of gluon PDF sensitivity at intermediate x (impacting BSM searches and Higgs cross sections); improves NNPDF’s hyperparameter optimization via Shapley-based diagnostics.

Conclusion: Shapley values provide a rigorous, interpretable, and model-agnostic framework for PDF impact analysis—enhancing both theoretical understanding and practical PDF fitting methodology.

Abstract: We show that Shapley values can be used to trace how individual parton distributions (PDFs) shape the theory predictions for high-energy observables computed from them. This provides a tool for assessing the impact of data on PDFs when determining them, and the impact of PDF uncertainties when using the PDFs to compute collider observables.The Shapley value is computed by treating the regression of PDFs from data as a cooperative game. The PDFs are the players, and the reward is the likelihood ($χ^2$) that characterizes the agreement between data and the predictions obtained from a given PDF, with theory and methodology held fixed. The method is agnostic to the way PDFs have been determined in the first place: for PDFs determined with a black-box AI model it may be used in order to explain the behavior of the model, and for PDFs determined using a fixed parametrization it may be used in order to expose the features and potential limitations of the parametrization. We find that the method recovers known expectations about which data constrain which PDFs in a global fit, while placing them on a more quantitative footing. We demonstrate its effectiveness in two ways. We uncover an unexpected loss of sensitivity of the gluon PDF at intermediate $x$, with potential implications for BSM searches and the gluon fusion Higgs cross section. We also show that the method can be used to improve the hyperparameter optimization procedure currently used by the NNPDF collaboration.

</details>


### [95] [EFT Validity and Truncation Uncertainty from few Nuisance Parameters](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02649)
*Benoît Assi,Adam Martin,William Shepherd*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a method to estimate truncation uncertainties in SMEFT by partially calculating the dim-6² (i.e., O(Λ⁻⁴)) contribution and supplementing it with a minimal set of nuisance parameters, significantly reducing parameter count while enforcing EFT validity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve the reliability of EFT-based bounds by quantifying and constraining truncation errors—specifically the dominant O(Λ⁻⁴) uncertainty—without requiring higher-dimensional operators or additional theoretical tools.

Method: Uses the calculable dim-6² contribution (quadratic interference of dim-6 operators) as the core signal correction, then models residual O(Λ⁻⁴) uncertainty via a small, automated set of nuisance parameters derived from Wilson coefficient scans; applicable to arbitrary EFT truncations.

Result: Achieves order-of-magnitude reduction in nuisance parameter count versus naïve approaches; ensures EFT validity by weighting kinematic regions where truncation error is parametrically subdominant to signal; demonstrated on collider processes truncated at O(Λ⁻²).

Conclusion: Partial calculation of dim-6² terms provides a robust, automation-friendly pathway to principled uncertainty modeling in SMEFT, enabling more stringent and physically meaningful constraints.

Abstract: An observable in an Effective Field Theory (EFT) is an expansion in a set of small parameters, no different than any other perturbation series. Truncating such a series expansion leaves the leading dropped term as the dominant source of error, and that term itself contains a calculable portion. We explore the partial calculation of this next-order error, available at dim-6$^2$ in SMEFT with no additional tools needed to simulate it, and explore how to efficiently use that partial calculation to model the next-order uncertainty in full using a minimal number of nuisance parameters. This estimate of the uncertainty of the EFT signal rate naturally imposes EFT validity by ensuring that bounds are driven by kinematic regions where truncation uncertainties are parametrically smaller than the signal. We incorporate the calculable dim-6$^2$ piece into the signal and cover the remaining higher-dimension uncertainty with a small set of nuisance parameters derived from a scan over Wilson-coefficient values. Our algorithm to determine the relevant nuisance parameters and distributions is automation-friendly and applies to arbitrary truncation choices. We then provide multiple examples of its implementation in high-energy collider processes focused on SMEFT truncated at $\mathcal{O}(Λ^{-2})$, where the dim-6$^2$ piece is used to estimate the full $\mathcal O(Λ^{-4})$ dependence. In the examples considered here the reduction of nuisance parameters is appreciable, generically reducing the nuisance parameter count by an order of magnitude compared to naïve estimates.

</details>


### [96] [Muon lifetime and Fermi constant: an update](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02657)
*Alexander Eberhart,Matteo Fael,Alexander Keshavarzi,Daisuke Nomura,Kay Schönwald,Matthias Steinhauser,Thomas Teubner,Aidan Wright*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents an updated theoretical prediction for the muon lifetime with QED corrections up to α³ and state-of-the-art hadronic contributions, reducing the uncertainty in the radiative correction factor Δq by an order of magnitude; it yields a refined value of the Fermi coupling constant G_F.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve the precision of the muon lifetime prediction and thereby refine the determination of the Fermi coupling constant G_F, constrained by current experimental accuracy and theoretical uncertainties—especially in radiative and hadronic corrections.

Method: High-order QED calculations up to O(α³), dispersive evaluation of hadronic vacuum polarization and light-by-light contributions, inclusion of finite-electron-mass effects and radiative corrections parametrized via the correction factor Δq in the muon decay rate formula.

Result: Δq = (−4 384 678 ± 34) × 10⁻⁹, reducing its uncertainty by an order of magnitude vs. prior O(α²) prediction; derived G_F = 1.166 378 59 (59) × 10⁻⁵ GeV⁻².

Conclusion: The improved theoretical prediction for the muon lifetime significantly enhances the precision of G_F extraction; future progress hinges on more accurate measurements of τ_μ and m_μ.

Abstract: We present an updated prediction for the lifetime of the muon including a detailed analysis of all relevant uncertainties. Our prediction includes QED corrections up to order $α^3$ and state-of-the-art hadronic contributions based on dispersive methods. Radiative corrections and finite-electron-mass effects are parametrized by the correction factor $Δq$ in $τ_μ^{-1}=G_F^2m_μ^5(1+Δq)/(192π^3)$, for which we obtain $Δq=(-4\, 384\, 678 \pm 34)\times 10^{-9}$. This reduces the uncertainty associated with $Δq$ by an order of magnitude compared to the previous prediction at order $α^2$. We use our results to provide an updated value of the Fermi coupling constant, $G_F=1.166\,378\, 59 \, (59) \times 10^{-5} \, \mathrm{GeV}^{-2}$. Further improvements will require better measurements of the muon lifetime and mass.

</details>


### [97] [Naturally Light Composite Higgs as a Protected Collective Eigenmode](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04136)
*Gauhar Abbas*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Introduces a novel class of composite Higgs models where lightness arises from protection of a scalar kernel eigenvalue—not tuning or pNGBs—characterized by sector participation number ℙ_sec > 1, gap ratio ℛ_coll ≫ 1, and microscopic sensitivity Δ_g = O(1); realized via TC–DTC locking and vectorlike DQCD bridge fermions yielding two-spurion lifting with Δ_{g_T} = Δ_{g_D} = 2; falsifiable via lattice spectroscopy and coupling-response scans.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome limitations of existing composite Higgs mechanisms—fine-tuning near criticality or reliance on pseudo-Nambu–Goldstone boson protection—by identifying a third, robust mechanism for collective lightness rooted in eigenvalue protection of the renormalized multi-operator scalar kernel.

Method: Defines two basis-invariant diagnostics (sector participation number ℙ_sec and sector gap ratio ℛ_coll) to identify collective lightness, introduces microscopic sensitivity Δ_g as the key discriminator between accidental and protected lightness; constructs an explicit realization using topcolor–dynamical topcolor (TC–DTC) locking and universal vectorlike DQCD bridge fermions whose constituent mass generation enforces vanishing aligned curvature at tree level, forcing Higgs mass generation only at joint two-spurion order.

Result: Demonstrates existence of protected collective Higgs with ℛ_coll ≫ 1, ℙ_sec > 1, and Δ_g = O(1); derives explicit Higgs mass formula m²_br = −(d_X/2π²) g_T² g_D² (f_B⁴/f_H²) L_X; shows Δ_{g_T} = Δ_{g_D} = 2 at leading-log order; establishes compatibility with collective top completion and vector-decoupling S-parameter reduction.

Conclusion: A third, nontrivial class of light composite Higgs models exists, grounded in eigenvalue protection of the strong-sector scalar kernel; it is theoretically consistent, predictive (Δ_g = 2), and experimentally falsifiable via sector-resolved lattice QCD and coupling-response measurements.

Abstract: Standard routes to a light composite Higgs either rely on tuning a single channel near criticality or protect a pseudo-Nambu--Goldstone coordinate of a coset. We introduce a third mechanism class in which the protected object is an \emph{eigenvalue} of the renormalized multi-operator scalar kernel of the strong sector. Two basis-invariant diagnostics, a sector participation number $\Psec$ and a sector gap ratio $\Rcoll$, identify collective lightness, but they cannot distinguish an accidental small determinant from a protected zero mode; the missing discriminator is the microscopic sensitivity $Δ_g=\left|\partial\ln|m_H^2|/\partial\ln g\right|$. A protected collective Higgs is defined by $\Rcoll\gg1$, $\Psec>1$, and $Δ_g=\mathcal{O}(1)$. We prove that this class is nonempty. A rank-one TC--DTC locking invariant forbids tree-level aligned curvature, while universal vectorlike DQCD bridge fermions, massless in the microscopic Lagrangian but acquiring a common DQCD constituent mass, obey $\partial_h^2\sum_A\Tr\mathcal{M}_A^2\big|_0=0$. The aligned scalar is therefore lifted only at joint two-spurion order, $m^2_{\mathrm{br}}=-(d_X/2π^2)g_T^2g_D^2(f_B^4/f_H^2)L_X$, giving $Δ_{g_T}=Δ_{g_D}=2$ at leading logarithmic order. The same DTC topology admits a collective top completion and a vector-decoupling route to reducing the positive technicolor contribution to $S$. The mechanism is falsifiable by sector-restricted lattice spectroscopy and coupling-response scans.

</details>


### [98] [Dark Matter in the General 2HDM with Extra Top Yukawa Couplings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02659)
*Leon M. G. de la Vega,Mohamed Krab*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends the general two-Higgs-doublet model (G2HDM) with a real scalar singlet (G2HDM+S) to provide a viable scalar dark matter candidate coupled predominantly to the top quark ('top-window' DM), satisfies LHC Higgs and BSM search constraints, cosmological and direct/indirect detection limits, and identifies six benchmark scenarios—including a novel flavor-violating production mode cg → tH → tSS—enabled by a nondiagonal top-charm Yukawa coupling ρ_{tc}.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a UV-complete, phenomenologically viable scalar dark matter model where DM couples dominantly to the top quark ('top-window DM'), addressing limitations of minimal singlet or 2HDM frameworks while accommodating collider, relic density, and direct/indirect detection constraints.

Method: Theoretical construction of G2HDM+S; systematic analysis of constraints from LHC Higgs signal strengths and heavy Higgs searches; computation of DM relic density (via freeze-out), spin-independent direct detection (XENONnT/LZ), and indirect detection (Fermi-LAT); identification and characterization of benchmark scenarios covering distinct phenomenological regimes (singlet-portal, bosonic-cascade, fermiophilic, flavor-violating); calculation of novel flavor-violating production cross section cg → tH → tSS.

Result: A viable parameter space survives all experimental and cosmological constraints; six benchmark points are defined, including one with observable flavor-violating cg → tH production followed by H → SS (invisible decay), yielding a top-triggered DM signature; the ρ_{tc} coupling enables unique LHC sensitivity to otherwise inaccessible DM mass regions.

Conclusion: G2HDM+S provides a theoretically consistent and testable framework for top-coupled scalar DM; its rich collider phenomenology—especially the flavor-violating cg → tH → tSS channel—is a distinctive, falsifiable prediction that motivates dedicated LHC analyses targeting top-tagged + missing energy final states.

Abstract: We extend the general two Higgs doublet model (G2HDM), which introduces extra Yukawa couplings, by an additional real scalar singlet (G2HDM+S), providing a viable scalar dark matter (DM) candidate. This setup provide a viable UV completion of top-window dark matter scenarios, in which DM communicates with the standard model predominantly through the top quark. We analyze the constraints on the visible scalar sector from Higgs signal strength measurements and direct searches for additional Higgs bosons at the LHC, and those on the dark sector from cosmological observables, including the observed DM relic density and spin-independent direct-detection as well as indirect detection. Within the surviving parameter space, we identify a rich and diverse LHC phenomenology governed by the interplay between the singlet portal, the extended Yukawa sector, and the heavy scalar mass hierarchy. To facilitate experimental investigation, we propose six benchmark scenarios spanning the singlet-portal, bosonic-cascade, fermiophilic, and flavor-violating regimes. In the latter, the nondiagonal top-charm coupling $ρ_{tc}$ induces the production channel $cg \to tH$, allowing the top quark to serve as a trigger for an otherwise invisible $H\to SS$ signal. This flavor-violating DM production is a distinct feature of the G2HDM+S.

</details>


### [99] [Disentangling Scheme Dependence in Quasi-PDFs with a Transverse-Momentum Cutoff](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04182)
*Junegone Chay*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper systematically decomposes the one-loop nonsinglet quark quasi-PDF into scheme-dependent and infrared-divergent components using a minimal transverse-momentum-cutoff scheme, clarifying how renormalization prescriptions affect quasi-PDFs prior to matching to lightcone PDFs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To disentangle scheme-dependent contributions from infrared divergences in quasi-PDFs—essential for robust lattice-to-lightcone matching—since their separation is often opaque in existing renormalization frameworks.

Method: Employs a transverse-momentum cutoff as an infrared regulator; defines a minimal cutoff scheme where scheme dependence is isolated via explicit cutoff dependence; expresses the quasi-PDF in distributional form; handles linear and logarithmic divergences in the counterterm; analyzes renormalization-group behavior under varying prescriptions.

Result: Achieves clean decomposition of the one-loop nonsinglet quasi-PDF into a scheme-dependent sector (cutoff-sensitive) and a remainder containing full collinear IR divergence plus finite matching-relevant terms; demonstrates consistent treatment of linear divergence and logarithmic counterterms; reveals explicit dependence of RG evolution on renormalization prescription.

Conclusion: The minimal transverse-momentum-cutoff scheme provides a transparent, systematic framework for isolating scheme dependence in quasi-PDFs before matching, serving as a benchmark for evaluating other schemes and improving precision in lattice QCD extraction of parton distributions.

Abstract: Quasi-PDFs provide a connection between Euclidean spatial correlations in lattice QCD and lightcone parton distributions. Their perturbative expressions contain both the infrared divergence required for matching and the scheme-dependent contributions associated with the renormalization prescriptions. The separation of these two ingredients is not always transparent. In this work we use a transverse-momentum cutoff as a simple setting in which these ingredients can be systematically decomposed into a scheme-dependent sector and a remainder for the nonsinglet quark quasi-PDF at one loop.
  We choose the minimal transverse-momentum-cutoff scheme, where the scheme-dependent sector is identified by its explicit cutoff dependence, while the remainder contains the full collinear infrared divergence and the finite contribution relevant for matching to the lightcone PDF. After expressing the quasi-PDF in terms of distributions, we show how to deal with the linear divergence and the logarithmic terms in the counterterm, and discuss the dependence of the renormalization-group behavior on the renormalization prescriptions. This organization clarifies how scheme dependence enters the quasi-PDF before the final matching is performed, and provides a benchmark for examining analogous separations in other renormalization schemes.

</details>


### [100] [Studying baryon number transport dynamics via hyperon-kaon correlations in $p + \mathrm{Au}$ collisions at $\sqrt{s_{_{\rm NN}}}=20$, $39$ and $62$ GeV](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02867)
*Siyuan Ping,Xiaozhou Yu,Long Ma*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates hyperon-kaon rapidity correlations in p+Au collisions at 20–62 GeV using AMPT and UrQMD models to establish a baseline for baryon number transport studies, specifically testing whether observed correlations constrain the gluon-junction mechanism.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe baryon number transport across large rapidity gaps and strange quark pair correlation via hyperon-kaon correlations, motivated by observations of positive net hyperon baryon number at mid-rapidity and the theoretical role of gluon junctions in carrying baryon number.

Method: Simulated p+Au collisions at sqrt(s_NN) = 20, 39, 62 GeV using AMPT and UrQMD models with fragmentation-based hyperon-kaon production; quantified correlations via Wasserstein distance as a function of relative rapidity; systematically varied beam energy, hyperon emission direction, and detector acceptance.

Result: Quantified hyperon-kaon rapidity correlations across energies and kinematic conditions; established model predictions without gluon-junction contributions—serving as a baseline for isolating junction effects in future experiments.

Conclusion: The simulated correlations provide a critical reference for disentangling conventional hadronic transport from exotic baryon-number-carrying mechanisms like the Y-shaped gluon junction; experimental validation will test the junction hypothesis in small-system heavy-ion collisions.

Abstract: The observation of positive net hyperon baryon numbers at mid-rapidity in heavy-ion collisions indicates that baryon numbers from incident nucleons can be transported across a large rapidity gap to hyperons where strange quarks of $s-\bar{s}$ are pair-produced. Consequently, hyperons and kaons are expected to be correlated, providing a sensitive probe of both baryon number transport mechanism and strange quark pair correlation. Such correlation may be used to test the gluon-junction interaction mechanism where a $Y$-shaped gluonic field may carry the baryon number and be responsible for the baryon number transport to hyperons over a large rapidity gap. We present hyperon-kaon correlations as a function of their relative rapidity in $p + \mathrm{Au}$ collisions at $\sqrt{s_{NN}} = 20$, $39$, and $62$ GeV using a multiphase transport ($\texttt{AMPT}$) model and Ultra-relativistic Quantum Molecular Dynamics ($\texttt{UrQMD}$) models where hyperon-kaon pairs are originated from fragmentation scheme. We quantify the correlation function using the Wasserstein distance method and systematically investigate the correlation dependence on the beam energy, hyperon emission direction and detector acceptance. Our simulation results provide a baseline without the baryon junction mechanism for future experimental measurements.

</details>


### [101] [Double strange hybrid baryon](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04237)
*B. Barsbay,K. Azizi,H. Sundu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates the mass and pole residue of the double strange hybrid baryon (ssqg) using QCD sum rules with gluonic interpolating currents and up to dimension-10 condensates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide theoretical predictions for the spectroscopic properties of the double strange hybrid baryon (ssqg), aiding future experimental searches and nonperturbative studies of hybrid hadrons.

Method: QCD sum rule analysis with an interpolating current containing explicit gluonic degrees of freedom; two-point correlation function evaluated in two Lorentz structures (slashed-q and identity); operator product expansion including vacuum condensates up to dimension ten; masses and residues extracted by averaging results from both structures.

Result: Ground state: mass = 1593.44 ± 130.29 MeV, residue = (2.57 ± 0.40) × 10⁻³ GeV⁵; first excited state: mass = 1897.47 ± 124.44 MeV, residue = (2.88 ± 0.62) × 10⁻³ GeV⁵.

Conclusion: The computed masses and residues constitute the first QCD sum rule prediction for the double strange hybrid baryon spectrum, offering benchmarks for experiment and theory.

Abstract: We investigate the spectroscopic properties of the double strange hybrid baryon with quark content $ssqg$ within the framework of QCD sum rule. Using an interpolating current with explicit gluonic degrees of freedom, the two-point correlation function is analyzed in terms of two independent Lorentz structures, $\slashed{q}$ and $I$. The operator product expansion is carried out by including vacuum condensates up to dimension ten, and the corresponding sum rules are derived for both structures. By taking the average of the results obtained from the two Lorentz structures, we extract the masses and pole residues of the ground and first excited states. For the ground state, we obtain a mass of $\widetilde{M } = (1593.44\pm 130.29)~ \mathrm{MeV}$ and a residue of $\widetilde{λ} = (2.57\pm 0.40) \times10^{-3} ~\mathrm{GeV}^5$. For the first excited state, the corresponding values are $M = (1897.47\pm 124.44)~ \mathrm{MeV}$ and $λ= (2.88\pm 0.62) \times10^{-3} ~\mathrm{GeV}^5$. The obtained results provide theoretical predictions for the double strange hybrid baryon spectrum and may be useful for future experimental searches as well as further nonperturbative studies of hybrid hadrons.

</details>


### [102] [Revisiting $\bar B^0 \rightarrow Λ_c^+ \bar p$ decay with higher twist corrections](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02876)
*Zhou Rui,Zhi-Tian Zou,Ying Li*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies single-charmed baryonic B decays B̄⁰ → Λc⁺p̄ and B̄⁰ → Λ̄c⁻p using PQCD, including higher-twist LCDAs; finds destructive interference between W-emission and W-exchange amplitudes improves agreement with data for the former, predicts BR ~10⁻⁸ for the first-time-studied doubly Cabibbo-suppressed mode, and provides first theoretical predictions for decay asymmetry parameters.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve discrepancies in predicted vs. measured branching fractions of baryonic B decays and to explore poorly constrained decay topologies (W-emission vs. W-exchange) in single-charmed modes, while extending theoretical predictions to asymmetry observables and a previously unstudied doubly Cabibbo-suppressed channel.

Method: Perturbative QCD (PQCD) factorization framework with systematic inclusion of higher-twist (higher-power) corrections from light-cone distribution amplitudes (LCDAs) of initial B meson and final baryons (Λc, p, Λ̄c); full amplitude calculation incorporating both W-emission and W-exchange topologies; computation of branching fractions and CP-averaged decay asymmetry parameters.

Result: Higher-twist LCDAs significantly enhance baryonic decay amplitudes; destructive interference between W-emission and W-exchange reduces B̄⁰ → Λc⁺p̄ branching fraction, improving agreement with experiment; B̄⁰ → Λ̄c⁻p branching fraction predicted as ~10⁻⁸; first theoretical predictions obtained for decay asymmetries α(B̄⁰ → Λc⁺p̄) and α(B̄⁰ → Λ̄c⁻p).

Conclusion: Higher-twist contributions and interference effects are essential for quantitative description of baryonic B decays; PQCD with improved LCDAs yields consistent predictions; asymmetry parameters offer new probes of QCD dynamics and potential NP sensitivity; the doubly Cabibbo-suppressed mode is accessible at future high-luminosity B factories.

Abstract: We investigate the single-charmed baryonic decays $\bar B^0 \to Λ_c^+ \bar p$ and $\bar B^0 \to \barΛ_c^- p$, which receive contributions from both $W$-emission and $W$-exchange topologies, within the framework of perturbative QCD (PQCD). Higher-power corrections associated with the hadronic light-cone distribution amplitudes (LCDAs) of both the initial- and final-state hadrons are systematically taken into account. We find that these higher-twist contributions play an important role in baryonic $B$ decays and cannot be neglected. A sizable destructive interference between the $W$-emission and $W$-exchange amplitudes is observed, which significantly reduces the predicted branching fraction of $\bar B^0 \to Λ_c^+ \bar p$ and leads to improved agreement with experimental measurements. The doubly Cabibbo-suppressed decay $\bar B^0 \to \barΛ_c^- p$ is studied for the first time. Its branching fraction is predicted to be of order $10^{-8}$, placing it within the reach of future high-luminosity experiments. We further present the first theoretical predictions for the decay asymmetry parameters of both channels, which provide additional observables for testing the underlying decay dynamics and can be confronted with future experimental data.

</details>


### [103] [Charting doubly strange hidden-charm pentaquarks: An electromagnetic mapping of spin-$\frac{1}{2}$ and $\frac{3}{2}$ states](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04355)
*Ulaş Özdem*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes electromagnetic multipole moments (magnetic dipole, electric quadrupole, magnetic octupole) of doubly strange hidden-charm pentaquarks ($S = -2$) using QCD light-cone sum rules (LCSR), employing multiple diquark-diquark-antiquark interpolating currents to assess model dependence; results show strong current and spin-structure sensitivity, charm-quark dominance in magnetic moments, axial-vector diquark-driven isospin effects, and provide quantitative uncertainty estimates and experimental benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To perform the first systematic LCSR study of electromagnetic multipoles in the $S = -2$ pentaquark sector, quantify theoretical uncertainty from interpolating current choice, and probe internal color-spin structure—particularly how diquark configurations affect electromagnetic observables and isospin sensitivity.

Method: QCD light-cone sum rules (LCSR) with independent diquark-diquark-antiquark interpolating currents; separate treatment for $J^P = 1/2^-$ and $3/2^-$ states; quark-level decomposition of contributions; extraction of magnetic dipole ($\mu$), electric quadrupole ($Q$), and magnetic octupole ($\mathcal{M}_3$) moments; explicit evaluation of isospin dependence via axial-vector diquark coupling.

Result: Magnetic dipole moments range: $-2.15$–$5.74\,\mu_N$ ($1/2^-$) and $-4.25$–$-0.43\,\mu_N$ ($3/2^-$); electric quadrupole moments: $-2.01\times10^{-2}$–$5.55\times10^{-2}\,\text{fm}^2$; magnetic octupole moments ~10× smaller; charm quark dominates $\mu$ in most currents; strange quarks decisive only in axial-vector diquark channels; exact $\mu_u/\mu_d = -2.00$ confirms direct charge-asymmetry propagation via axial-vector diquarks.

Conclusion: Electromagnetic multipole moments of $S = -2$ hidden-charm pentaquarks are highly sensitive to internal wave function and interpolating current structure, especially axial-vector diquark content; the observed current spread quantifies intrinsic LCSR uncertainty; predictions serve as discriminative benchmarks for experiment (e.g., LHCb, PANDA) and lattice QCD, and constrain viable pentaquark structural models.

Abstract: We calculate the magnetic dipole moments of doubly strange hidden-charm pentaquark states with spin-parity $J^P = 1/2^-$ and $3/2^-$ using QCD light-cone sum rules (LCSR), presenting the first systematic LCSR investigation of the electromagnetic multipole structure in the $S = -2$ sector. To assess the model dependence, we employ independent interpolating currents in diquark-diquark-antiquark form, which probe different assumptions about the internal color-spin correlations. For the spin-$3/2$ states, we also compute the electric quadrupole and magnetic octupole moments. The magnetic dipole moments exhibit a considerable spread across currents, ranging from $-2.15\,μ_N$ to $5.74\,μ_N$ for spin-$1/2$ pentaquarks and from $-4.25\,μ_N$ to $-0.43\,μ_N$ for spin-$3/2$ states, reflecting the sensitivity of magnetic moments to the internal wave function. A quark-level decomposition reveals that the charm quark dominates in most configurations, while strange quarks play a decisive role only in currents favoring axial-vector diquark structures. The electric quadrupole moments lie between $-2.01\times10^{-2}$ fm$^2$ and $5.55\times10^{-2}$ fm$^2$, and the magnetic octupole moments are typically an order of magnitude smaller. The current dependence of the magnetic dipole moments provides a quantitative measure of the theoretical uncertainty arising from the choice of interpolating operator. The pronounced isospin sensitivity of $J^3_μ(x)$ across all three multipole moments arises from its axial-vector diquark structure, which isolates the light quark from spin averaging and allows the charge asymmetry $e_u/e_d = -2$ to propagate directly into the electromagnetic moments; the ratio $μ_u/μ_d = -2.00$ confirms this mechanism exactly. Our predictions offer benchmarks for future experiments and lattice QCD calculations, and may help discriminate among competing structural models.

</details>


### [104] [Majoron Dark Energy via Freezing Induced by Quantum Coherence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03070)
*Keunsu Cheon,Sin Kyu Kang,Jungjai Lee*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Proposes a nonequilibrium Majoron dark energy mechanism where quantum coherence in a hidden pseudo-Dirac sterile fermion reservoir induces late-time freezing of the Majoron field via a causal, memory-dependent response, leading to a metastable dark energy state with w ≈ −1.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain late-time cosmic acceleration via a dynamical dark energy mechanism—specifically, a physical Majoron field—without requiring fine-tuned ultralight mass or ad hoc potentials, by leveraging quantum coherence and nonequilibrium dynamics in a hidden sector.

Method: Constructs a nonequilibrium effective theory where the Majoron derivatively couples to the number current of a pseudo-Dirac sterile fermion reservoir; derives a causal response kernel with finite memory time; performs linear-response matching to obtain an effective scalar equation featuring a velocity-suppressing exchange term q_exch \ddotφ/\dotφ; identifies microscopic origin of lag in off-diagonal coherence of the reservoir.

Result: Demonstrates that the exchange term dynamically suppresses Majoron velocity, enabling a response-dominated freezing branch even when m_φ > H₀; yields a metastable frozen phase with equation-of-state parameter w_φ ≃ −1, distinct from a true cosmological constant.

Conclusion: Majoron dark energy can emerge as a metastable nonequilibrium state sustained by quantum-coherent reservoir response, offering a microphysically grounded alternative to ΛCDM with testable deviations in dark energy dynamics.

Abstract: We propose a nonequilibrium mechanism for Majoron dark energy in which the late-time freezing of a physical Majoron is induced by quantum coherence in a hidden pseudo-Dirac sterile fermion reservoir. The evolving Majoron background derivatively couples to the hidden pseudo-Dirac number current and drives a lagged reservoir response with a finite memory time. In the short-memory regime, the causal response kernel reduces to \(\dot X+Γ_{\rm PD}X=β\ddotφ\). The leading linear-response matching \(Q=αX\) then yields an effective scalar equation containing the exchange structure \(q_{\rm exch}\ddotφ/\dotφ\). We show that this term can dynamically suppress the Majoron velocity and sustain a response-dominated freezing branch even when the intrinsic Majoron mass is larger than the present Hubble scale. The microscopic origin of the lag variable is identified with the phase-lagged off-diagonal coherence of the hidden pseudo-Dirac ensemble, while the response strength is controlled by a response-weighted hidden density rather than by an independent gravitating component. The resulting state is a metastable nonequilibrium frozen phase with \(w_φ\simeq -1\), rather than an exactly static cosmological constant.

</details>


### [105] [Flipped rotating axion: Baryogenesis and Dark Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03085)
*Konstantinos Dimopoulos*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes that a rotating spectator axion-like particle—enabled by a periodic non-minimal gravity coupling and vacuum manifold reorientation post-inflation—can simultaneously generate baryon asymmetry (via spontaneous baryogenesis) and later serve as cold dark matter via coherent oscillations, with a specific constraint ξ ∼ (f/mₚ)² to prevent condensate fragmentation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify the origins of baryon asymmetry and dark matter within a single mechanism tied to post-inflation dynamics, avoiding fine-tuned initial conditions or additional sources of CP violation.

Method: Analytical field-theoretic analysis of axion dynamics in non-oscillating (runaway-potential) inflation models; derivation of rotational behavior induced by vacuum manifold flip; assessment of condensate stability under non-minimal gravitational coupling; computation of baryogenesis efficiency and dark matter relic abundance.

Result: A viable parameter space exists where the axion’s rotation generates sufficient baryon asymmetry (η_B ~ 10⁻¹⁰) and its subsequent oscillations yield the observed dark matter density; fragmentation is suppressed only when the non-minimal coupling satisfies ξ ≈ (f/mₚ)².

Conclusion: The co-genesis of baryons and dark matter is feasible via a gravity-coupled rotating axion, offering a minimal, symmetry-preserving, and inflation-compatible solution linking early-universe topology change to two major cosmological puzzles.

Abstract: It is shown that the co-genesis of baryon asymmetry and dark matter can be achieved through the rotation of a spectator axion-like particle, because of a flip in the vacuum manifold's orientation at the end of inflation. This can occur if the axion has a periodic non-minimal coupling to gravity (while preserving the discrete shift symmetry) in non-oscillating inflation models, where the inflaton field is characterised by a runaway potential. Our rotating axion can generate the baryon asymmetry of the Universe through spontaneous baryogenesis, while at a later epoch it can oscillate as dark matter. We show that in order to avoid fragmentation of the axion condensate during the rotation, we require the non-minimal coupling $ξ\sim(f/m_P)^2$, where $f$ is the axion decay constant.

</details>


### [106] [George, I and the curvaton](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03101)
*Konstantinos Dimopoulos*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This is a personal retrospective on collaborative research with George Lazarides, focusing on the development and application of the curvaton hypothesis in early-universe cosmology.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a brief overview of joint research efforts with George Lazarides, particularly centered on the then-novel curvaton hypothesis.

Method: Collaborative theoretical research involving development and phenomenological exploitation of the curvaton mechanism in inflationary cosmology.

Result: Advancement and application of the curvaton hypothesis as an alternative or complementary mechanism for generating cosmic curvature perturbations.

Conclusion: The collaboration significantly contributed to the conceptual and phenomenological foundations of the curvaton paradigm in early-universe physics.

Abstract: George Lazarides was a pivotal collaborator and friend to me. We worked together on several projects, developing and exploiting the curvaton hypothesis, which was new at the time. This is a brief overview of our joint research.

</details>


### [107] [Polarized Nucleon as a Topological Dipole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03123)
*Kenji Fukushima,Tomoya Uji*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes that a polarized nucleon inherently hosts a topological charge dipole distribution, determined by the flavor-singlet axial charge in the chiral limit; it demonstrates this via a chiral soliton model incorporating vector mesons and the $U(1)_A$ anomaly, and proposes experimental signatures in heavy-ion collisions via $η/η′$ production and pseudoscalar asymmetries linked to magnetic fields or vorticity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To elucidate the spatial structure of topological charge density in polarized nucleons and connect it to fundamental symmetries (chiral limit, $U(1)_A$ anomaly) and measurable observables.

Method: Analytical derivation from the definition of the topological form factor and pseudoscalar nature of topological charge density; concrete realization in a two-flavor chiral soliton model extended with vector mesons and explicit $U(1)_A$ anomaly; phenomenological analysis of experimental signatures.

Result: A robust prediction of a topological dipole moment in polarized nucleons, quantitatively tied to the flavor-singlet axial charge $a_0$; successful reproduction of the dipole pattern in the soliton model via rotational excitation inducing a singlet pseudoscalar profile; identification of exclusive $η/η′$ production and directed-flow-like pseudoscalar asymmetries as viable experimental probes.
