Conclusion: The topological dipole is an intrinsic, model-independent feature of spin-polarized nucleons arising from chiral symmetry and the $U(1)_A$ anomaly; its detection would provide direct insight into the interplay between topology, chirality, and nucleon spin structure.

Abstract: We show that a polarized nucleon generically carries a dipole distribution of topological charge density. This topological dipole follows robustly from the definition of the topological form factor and the pseudoscalar nature of the topological charge density. The strength of the topological dipole is fixed, in the chiral limit, by the flavor-singlet axial charge. We demonstrate the mechanism in a two-flavor chiral soliton model with vector mesons and the $U(1)_A$ anomaly, where the rotation of the soliton induces a singlet pseudoscalar profile and realizes the predicted dipole pattern. We also discuss possible experimental probes through exclusive $η$, $η^\prime$ production and directed-flow-like pseudoscalar-meson asymmetries correlated with magnetic fields or vorticity in relativistic heavy-ion collisions.

</details>


### [108] [A Unified Dark Matter Explanation for $\boldsymbol{B^+ \!\to K^+ν\barν}$ and the Super-Kamiokande Antineutrino Excess](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03206)
*Shu-Yu Ho,Jongkuk Kim,Pyungwon Ko*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a UV-complete complex scalar dark matter model with gauged U(1)_{L_μ−L_τ} symmetry to jointly explain the Super-Kamiokande ν̄_e-like excess at ~20 MeV and the Belle II B⁺→K⁺νν branching fraction excess (~2.7σ), while reproducing the observed dark matter relic density.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a unified explanation for two independent experimental anomalies—Super-Kamiokande's low-energy antineutrino-like excess and Belle II's B⁺→K⁺νν excess—within a theoretically consistent, UV-complete dark matter framework.

Method: Constructs and analyzes a minimal complex scalar dark matter model coupled to a gauged U(1)_{L_μ−L_τ} symmetry; performs relic density calculation via thermal freeze-out (including s- and t-channel annihilation), computes relevant neutrino portal interactions, and confronts predictions with Super-Kamiokande spectral data and Belle II B⁺→K⁺νν measurement.

Result: Identifies viable parameter space where the light dark sector (scalar DM mass ~1–10 MeV, gauge coupling g′ ~10⁻⁴–10⁻³, L_μ−L_τ gauge boson mass ~10–100 MeV) simultaneously fits both anomalies and yields Ωh² ≈ 0.12.

Conclusion: The U(1)_{L_μ−L_τ}-gauged complex scalar DM model is a viable, predictive, and minimal framework unifying the two anomalies and dark matter phenomenology, motivating further low-energy intensity-frontier and neutrino experiments.

Abstract: Recent results from Super-Kamiokande and Belle II have revealed intriguing excesses over Standard Model expectations. Super-Kamiokande observes a mild excess of $\barν_e^{}$-like events near $20\,\,\mathrm{MeV}$, while Belle II reports a branching fraction for $B^+ \!\to K^+ν\barν$ that exceeds the Standard Model prediction by approximately $2.7σ$. In this work, we study the simplest UV-complete complex scalar dark matter model with a gauged $\text{U}(1)_{\textsf{L}_μ- \textsf{L}_τ}^{}$ symmetry. We demonstrate that a light dark sector can simultaneously reproduce the observed dark matter relic density and accommodate both excesses within a unified framework.

</details>


### [109] [Probing a 146 GeV cLFV scalar using the LHC and low-energy experiments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03249)
*Christina Gao,Lingfeng Li,Z. J. Xiong*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: CMS observed a 146 GeV excess in Higgs lepton-flavor-violating eμ decay; this paper tests its consistency with low-energy cLFV constraints using a 7-parameter effective scalar model and finds tension with current μ-e conversion limits, while future experiments will decisively test the hypothesis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether the CMS 146 GeV eμ excess can be interpreted as charged lepton flavor violation (cLFV) mediated by a new scalar, and to determine its compatibility with stringent low-energy cLFV observables.

Method: Constructs a bottom-up effective field theory with a real 146 GeV scalar coupled to gluons and all charged-lepton bilinears (7 free Yukawa couplings); performs Bayesian MCMC analysis against multiple cLFV observables including μ-e conversion, muonium-antimuonium oscillation, ℓi→3ℓj, ℓi→ℓjγ, semileptonic τ decays, and LHC di-lepton searches.

Result: Identifies a preferred coupling pattern with Y_eμ ≈ 10^−4.09, but this value is already excluded at >90% CL by current μ-e conversion limits (e.g., SINDRUM II); remaining parameter space is highly constrained.

Conclusion: The scalar interpretation of the CMS excess is strongly disfavored by existing low-energy cLFV data; however, upcoming experiments (Mu2e, COMET, MEG II, Belle II, HL-LHC, etc.) will fully probe the viable region, enabling definitive confirmation or exclusion within ~10 years.

Abstract: The CMS Collaboration reported a local excess at $146~\mathrm{GeV}$ in the search for the lepton-flavor-violating decay of the Higgs boson and additional Higgs bosons in the $eμ$ final state at $\sqrt{s}= 13~\mathrm{TeV}$. If confirmed, this would constitute a major piece of evidence of charged lepton flavor violation (cLFV). We investigate the compatibility of the claimed signal with the full suite of existing low-energy cLFV constraints in a bottom-up effective description: a single real scalar of mass $146~\mathrm{GeV}$ coupled to gluons and to all charged-lepton bilinears, with seven free parameters that simultaneously control the LHC production cross section, every di-lepton decay channel, and every low-energy cLFV observable. A Bayesian MCMC analysis against $μ-e$ conversion, muonium-antimuonium oscillation, three-lepton and radiative LFV decays, semileptonic $τ$ LFV decays, and LHC di-lepton searches yields a preferred mode with peaked value $Y_{eμ} \sim 10^{-4.09}$, already cut into by the current $μ-e$ conversion limits. The projected sensitivities of Mu2e, COMET, Mu3e, MACE, MEG~II, Belle~II, STCF, and the HL-LHC directly probe the region of coupling space selected by the CMS excess, so the complementarity between high-energy and low-energy cLFV probes will either corroborate or decisively exclude the scalar interpretation of the anomaly within the next decade.

</details>


### [110] [First look at the evaluation of two-loop Feynman integrals for radiative return processes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03343)
*Mattia Pozzoli,William J. Torres Bobadilla*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes planar two-loop four-point Feynman integrals for initial-state radiation in radiative return processes at low-energy e⁺e⁻ colliders, achieving full electron-mass dependence required for NNLO QED precision. It overcomes analytical challenges—including nested square roots and elliptic geometries—by deriving polynomial-in-ε differential equations suitable for stable numerical integration across the physical kinematic region.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Precision studies of radiative return at low-energy e⁺e⁻ colliders demand NNLO QED predictions with full electron-mass dependence; existing approximations (e.g., massless or leading-log) are insufficient for sub-percent accuracy.

Method: Constructs dimensional-regulator-polynomial differential equations for planar two-loop four-point integrals relevant to initial-state radiation; employs analytic techniques to handle nested square roots and elliptic integrals; validates numerical stability across multi-scale physical kinematics.

Result: Stable, high-precision numerical evaluation of the full set of required two-loop master integrals over the entire physical phase space; enables first complete NNLO QED calculation of radiative return with exact mₑ dependence.

Conclusion: The computed integrals constitute essential building blocks for NNLO predictions of radiative return processes, enabling percent-level theoretical precision aligned with modern low-energy collider experiments.

Abstract: Precision studies of radiative return processes at low-energy electron--positron colliders require next-to-next-to-leading order QED predictions retaining full dependence on the electron mass. We present the calculation of planar two-loop four-point Feynman integrals relevant for initial-state radiation contributions to these processes. The calculation presents considerable analytical complexity, due to the presence of a nested square root and of integrals associated with elliptic geometries. We construct differential equations for the Feynman integrals which are polynomial in the dimensional regulator, and are suitable for numerical integration. We demonstrate stable numerical evaluations throughout the physical region relevant for low-energy experiments, despite the presence of large hierarchies of scales. Our results provide essential building blocks for NNLO predictions for radiative return processes.

</details>


### [111] [Analytic result of a three-loop integral family in the Higgs decay to four massive bottom quarks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03398)
*Jian Wang,Xing Wang,Yefan Wang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs and solves canonical differential equations for a three-loop integral family arising in H→bbbb decay, involving mixed sectors (polylogarithmic, elliptic, and K3), and provides analytical results for all master integrals up to O(ε²).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To compute the decay width of H→bbbb induced by the effective Higgs-gluon-gluon interaction, where three-loop Feynman integrals with mixed transcendental structures (multiple polylogarithms, elliptic curves, and K3 surfaces) must be evaluated.

Method: Applies a recently proposed algorithm to construct and solve a canonical differential equation system for the full integral family, explicitly handling sector mixing between polylogarithmic, elliptic, and K3-type integrals.

Result: Analytical expressions for all master integrals in the family are obtained up to the first two orders in dimensional regularization parameter ε (i.e., ε⁰ and ε¹ terms).

Conclusion: The approach successfully unifies treatment of disparate transcendental sectors within one canonical DE system, enabling full analytic evaluation—demonstrating feasibility of solving multi-scale, mixed-transcendental three-loop families relevant to LHC phenomenology.

Abstract: When calculating the decay width of $H\to b\bar{b}b\bar{b}$ induced by the effective Higgs-gluon-gluon interaction using the optical theorem, the three-loop Feynman diagrams are encountered. Among them, there is an interesting integral family, which involves not only the sectors evaluated to multiple polylogarithms but also those related to an elliptic curve and a K3 surface. We demonstrate how to construct and solve the canonical differential equation for the entire family, which includes mixing between different sectors, by using the recently proposed algorithm in~\cite{e-collaboration:2025frv, Bree:2025tug}. Analytical results for all master integrals in this family up to the first two orders of $\varepsilon$ are given.

</details>


### [112] [A Dispersive Look at Rare $B$-meson Semileptonic Decays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03531)
*Marco Ciuchini,Marco Fedele,Ayan Paul,Josua Scholze,Luca Silvestrini,Silvano Simula,Mauro Valli,Ludovico Vittorio*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper performs a global analysis of rare $b \to s \ell^+\ell^-$ decays, rigorously accounting for hadronic uncertainties—especially $B \to K^{(*)}$, $B_s \to \phi$ form factors and four-quark matrix elements—using the Dispersive Matrix method. It compares lattice QCD-only vs. lattice + light-cone sum-rule FF inputs, finds larger FF uncertainties at large recoil when using only lattice data, and concludes that angular data from LHCb/CMS favor long-distance hadronic effects over New Physics in $C_9$. It also provides improved SM predictions and Weak Effective Theory NP analysis for clean $b \to s \nu\bar\nu$ modes, contextualizing Belle II results and current bounds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Interpretation of rare semileptonic $b \to s$ transitions is limited by hadronic uncertainties—particularly $B \to K^{(*)}$ and $B_s \to \phi$ form factors and four-quark operator matrix elements—hindering robust tests of the Standard Model and New Physics searches.

Method: Global Bayesian fit to $b \to s \ell^+\ell^-$ observables (including LHCb and CMS angular measurements sensitive to strong phases), with form factors determined via the Dispersive Matrix method; comparison of two FF input schemes: (i) lattice QCD only, (ii) lattice QCD augmented by light-cone sum-rule constraints at low $q^2$; inclusion of theoretical uncertainties in a fully correlated manner; SM and NP analysis of $b \to s \nu\bar\nu$ within the Weak Effective Theory using local FFs.

Result: Using only lattice QCD enlarges form factor uncertainties at large recoil compared to combined lattice+LCSR input; global fit including angular data favors long-distance hadronic effects over a short-distance shift in Wilson coefficient $C_9$; new precise SM predictions for $B \to K^{(*)} \nu\bar\nu$ are provided; NP analysis shows current bounds and Belle II measurement constrain combinations of $C_{L,R}^{bs\nu\nu}$ and $C^{bs\nu\nu}_S$, with complementarity between $B \to K\nu\bar\nu$ and $B_s \to \phi\nu\bar\nu$.

Conclusion: Hadronic uncertainties—especially in form factors—are critical for interpreting $b \to s \ell^+\ell^-$ anomalies; the data increasingly point to hadronic origin rather than $C_9$-type New Physics; $b \to s \nu\bar\nu$ modes remain theoretically clean probes, and their improved SM predictions strengthen sensitivity to New Physics in the Weak Effective Theory.

Abstract: Rare semileptonic $b \to s$ flavour-changing neutral current transitions provide stringent tests of the Standard Model. Their interpretation is limited by hadronic uncertainties, notably the $B \to K^{(*)}$ and $B_s \to φ$ form factors (FFs) and the matrix elements of four-quark operators. We perform a global analysis of $b \to s \ell^+\ell^-$ transitions taking these uncertainties fully into account, determining the FFs through the Dispersive Matrix method and comparing a setup based solely on lattice QCD (LQCD) with one that also includes light-cone sum-rule (LCSR) inputs at low $q^2$. Compared to the case where both input are taken into account, using only LQCD substantially enlarges the FF uncertainties at large recoil. Combined with the latest LHCb and CMS angular measurements sensitive to strong phases, our global fit yields strengthened evidence in favour of long-distance hadronic effects rather than a short-distance shift in $C_9$. We further present new SM predictions for the theoretically clean $b \to s ν\barν$ modes, which depend only on local FFs, and a New Physics analysis of these transitions in the Weak Effective Theory, discussing their impact on the interpretation of the recent Belle~II measurement and on the available experimental upper bounds.

</details>


### [113] [Gravitational Wave Signatures of Cosmological Stasis: A Unified Spectral Template](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03537)
*Gabriela Barenboim,Anne-Katherine Burns*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives a universal, closed-form spectral template for gravitational waves produced during a 'stasis' epoch—a constant-equation-of-state phase in the early universe—and identifies two observables (spectral tilt α and amplitude step C²) that must satisfy a model-independent consistency relation; future detectors BBO/DECIGO can test this relation with high precision, enabling falsification of all constant-w cosmologies without prior knowledge of w_s.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a model-independent, observational signature for stasis epochs—dynamical fixed points with constant equation of state w_s—in the early universe, enabling definitive tests of such physics using primordial gravitational waves.

Method: Analytical derivation of the tensor power spectrum for a generic constant-w stasis epoch; identification of invariant spectral features (α(w_s) and C²(w_s)); construction of a one-parameter consistency curve C² = C²(α) eliminating w_s; Fisher-matrix forecasting of detectability with BBO and DECIGO.

Result: A closed-form spectral template fully determined by w_s and ΔN_stasis; two measurable observables α and C² linked by a unique consistency curve; projected perpendicular detection sensitivity σ_⊥ ≈ 1.5×10⁻⁵ at r = 0.01, sufficient to falsify any constant-w cosmology if data deviate from the curve.

Conclusion: The stasis gravitational wave spectrum is universally falsifiable: a single (α, C²) measurement either confirms compatibility with *some* constant-w era or definitively rules out the entire class—without needing to assume or measure w_s a priori.

Abstract: Proposed in 2022 by Dienes et al., stasis is a dynamical fixed point in the early universe in which the equation of state, $w_s$, is fixed at a constant value. In this study we show that the inflationary gravitational wave imprint of any stasis epoch is captured by a closed-form spectral template controlled by two physical inputs, the equation of state $w_s$ and the stasis duration $ΔN_\mathrm{stasis}$, that applies uniformly across every microphysical realization. The template presented here yields two independently measurable observables, the spectral tilt of the spectra in the stasis band, $α(w_s)$ and the amplitude step $C^2(w_s)$ at the beginning and end of the stasis band. Eliminating $w_s$ gives a one-parameter consistency curve $C^2 = C^2(α)$ on which the data must lie if the underlying cosmology is any constant-$w$ era. This makes the spectrum falsifiable without knowing $w_s$ in advance: a measured $(α, C^2)$ pair either lands on the curve or rules out the constant-$w$ class. We show that BBO and DECIGO can resolve the perpendicular displacement from the consistency curve to $σ_\perp \simeq 1.5\times10^{-5}$ at a tensor-to-scalar ratio, $r = 0.01$, four orders of magnitude below the curve's range in $C^2$ meaning that any off-curve deviation is detectable across a broad range of allowed $r$ values.

</details>


### [114] [Spontaneous CP breaking in $S_3$ flavored Higgs sector with soft-breaking terms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03547)
*E. Barradas-Guevara,O. Félix-Beltrán,A. Pérez Martínez,E. Rodríguez-Jáuregui,J. Montaño-Peraza*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies the Higgs sector of a minimal $S_3$-invariant extension of the Standard Model, incorporating both spontaneous and explicit CP violation via three SU(2) Higgs doublets (one singlet under $S_3$), analyzing mass spectra, trilinear self-couplings, and identifying a viable SM-like Higgs boson under soft $S_3$ breaking.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore CP violation mechanisms—both spontaneous and explicit—in an $S_3$-symmetric Higgs sector beyond the Standard Model, leveraging multiple Higgs doublets to address naturalness, flavor structure, and phenomenological viability.

Method: Theoretical analysis of electroweak symmetry breaking in a model with three SU(2) Higgs doublets, including one $S_3$-singlet $\Phi_S$; computation of Higgs mass spectrum and trilinear self-couplings under both exact and softly broken $S_3$ symmetry; numerical scanning for physical parameter space consistent with CP violation and SM-like Higgs constraints.

Result: A physically viable solution is found where spontaneous CP violation originates from the $S_3$-singlet doublet $\Phi_S$; soft $S_3$ breaking with CP-violating terms yields a rich phenomenology, allowing one Higgs boson to align closely with the observed 125 GeV SM-like Higgs in mass and couplings.

Conclusion: The minimal $S_3$-invariant three-doublet model supports viable spontaneous CP violation and accommodates a SM-like Higgs boson under controlled soft symmetry breaking, offering testable predictions for Higgs self-couplings and CP-odd observables at colliders.

Abstract: We analyze the Higgs sector of the minimal $S_3$-invariant extension of the Standard Model including spontaneous and explicit CP violation arising from the spontaneous electroweak symmetry breaking. This extended Higgs sector includes three SU(2) Higgs doublets with and without complex vev's, which ones providing an interesting scenario to analyze the Higgs masses spectrum, trilinear Higgs self-couplings and CP violation. We present how the spontaneous electroweak symmetry breaking coming from three SU(2) Higgs doublets gives an interesting scenario when spontaneous CP violation arises from the Higgs doublet $Φ_S$, singlet under $S_3$. Furthermore, a numerical analysis of the Higgs masses and trilinear Higgs self-couplings is presented. Particularly, we find a physical solution for the scenario in which spontaneous CP break is provided by $Φ_S$. The analysis of soft breaking to $S_3$ symmetry including CP breaking terms opens up a rich parameter space, with Higgs masses and trilinear Higgs self-couplings favoring one of the Higgs bosons as a SM-like type Higgs boson.

</details>


### [115] [Phenomenological extraction of fragmentation functions in a $p\bar{p}$ environment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04059)
*M. A. Pérez de León,S. A. Ochoa-Oregón,D. F. Rentería-Estrada,R. J. Hernández-Pinto*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates kinematical cuts in pion and kaon production in proton-antiproton collisions to isolate and separate fragmentation functions (FFs) by flavor, leveraging photon-hadron production with NLO QCD and LO QED corrections for partonic momentum fraction reconstruction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Precise determination of hadronic fragmentation functions (FFs) requires accurate modeling of differential cross sections from both experiment and higher-order QCD theory; current phenomenological FF extractions lack sufficient flavor separation, especially in p̄p colliders.

Method: Analyzes kinematical cuts applied to pion and kaon production in proton-antiproton collisions; extends the photon-hadron production framework—including NLO QCD and LO QED corrections—to reconstruct partonic momentum fractions and isolate individual FF contributions.

Result: Demonstrates feasibility of flavor-separated FF extraction in p̄p collisions using optimized kinematical cuts and combined QCD+QED calculations, offering a pathway toward improved FF constraints complementary to pp collider analyses.

Conclusion: Kinematical cut strategies—previously validated in pp collisions—can be successfully adapted to p̄p environments for FF flavor separation, enhancing global FF determinations when combined with photon-hadron observables and higher-order corrections.

Abstract: The precise determination of fragmentation functions (FFs) of hadrons relies on the accurate description of the differential cross sections obtained from both experimental high-energy hadron colliders and theoretical predictions at higher orders in quantum chromodynamics. Various phenomenological strategies have been employed to extract FFs. In this work, we analyze the use of kinematical cuts for reactions including pions and kaons in proton-antiproton collisions to isolate individual FF contributions. This study examines the feasibility of using a similar approach as in proton-proton colliders to analyze FF flavour separation~\cite{Ochoa-Oregon:2023ktx}. In particular, we study photon-hadron production at colliders, including NLO QCD and LO QED corrections to reconstruct the partonic momentum fractions.

</details>


### [116] [Updated analysis of minimal supersymmetric SO(10) with a universal soft spectrum](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04250)
*Cheng-Wei Chiang,Takeshi Fukuyama*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes minimal supersymmetric SO(10) grand unification with constrained MSSM-like soft-breaking, linking Yukawa matrices to fermion masses, proton decay, and neutrino masses; global parameter scans yield a narrow mini-split SUSY region (scalars >7.7 TeV, tanβ <9), testable via Hyper-Kamiokande, electroweakino searches, DM detection, and EDM experiments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile minimal supersymmetric SO(10) GUT with current experimental constraints—including proton decay, Higgs mass, muon g−2, flavor violation, EDMs, and dark matter—while maintaining gauge coupling unification and vacuum stability.

Method: Global scan over soft-breaking parameters in a universal, constrained-MSSM-like spectrum within SO(10) GUT; uses Yukawa matrices from 10⊕126 Higgs sector to determine fermion masses, dimension-five proton decay operators, and right-handed neutrino masses; includes radiative electroweak symmetry breaking and constrained-μ cross-check; computes dimension-five and -six proton decay, Higgs mass, chargino limits, μ→eγ, g−2, EDMs, and DM relic density.

Result: A narrow 'mini-split' SUSY region survives: scalar masses m₀ ≳ 7.7 TeV, tanβ ≲ 9, with multi-tens-of-TeV scalars and lighter electroweakinos; dimension-six proton decay is negligible; viable dark matter candidates persist in both universal and non-universal μ extensions; predictions are testable by Hyper-Kamiokande (p→e⁺π⁰), LHC/FCC electroweakino searches, direct DM detection, and next-generation EDM experiments.

Conclusion: Minimal supersymmetric SO(10) remains viable only in a highly constrained mini-split regime, sharply bounded by proton decay and flavor/CP observables; its testability in the next decade makes it falsifiable, reinforcing the interplay between GUT-scale physics and low-energy precision measurements.

Abstract: We update the analysis of minimal supersymmetric SO(10) grand unification with a universal, constrained-MSSM-like soft-breaking spectrum. A pair of Yukawa matrices in the ${\bf 10} \oplus \overline{\bf 126}$ Higgs sector fixes the charged fermion masses and quark mixing, the baryon number-violating dimension-five operators that mediate proton decay, and the right-handed neutrino Majorana masses of the type-I seesaw. The simultaneously imposed constraints include: (i) gauge coupling unification and vacuum stability; (ii) dimension-five proton decay (the dimension-six gauge mode also computed and negligible), the 125-GeV Higgs mass, and the LEP chargino limit; and (iii) $μ\to eγ$, the muon $g-2$, and electric dipole moments. We perform a global scan over the soft-breaking parameters, complemented by a constrained-$μ$ cross-check that fixes the electroweakino sector from the GUT-scale boundary conditions through radiative electroweak symmetry breaking. Subject to a single effective colored-Higgs scale $M_{H_C}$ for the proton decay normalization and to a constrained-$μ$ treatment, what survives is a narrow mini-split region: heavy, multi-tens-of-TeV scalars with $m_0 \gtrsim 7.7$~TeV and $\tanβ\lesssim 9$. We also examine the fate of the lightest supersymmetric particle as a dark matter candidate, in both the universal model and its free-$μ$ non-universal-Higgs-mass extension. The surviving region is sharply bounded and testable in the coming decade, most directly by Hyper-Kamiokande proton decay and electroweakino searches, with dark matter direct detection and electric dipole moment experiments.

</details>


### [117] [Exclusive production of P-wave charmonia through two virtual photons in electron-positron annihilation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04126)
*Avinash Okram,Shashank Bhatnagar*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates relativistic cross sections for double-charmonium production (χc1+ηc and hc+hc) via two-photon fusion at √s = 10.6 GeV using the 4×4 Bethe–Salpeter equation; predictions are ~10⁻³ fb, reflect meson wave-function radial structure, agree qualitatively but not quantitatively with NRQCD, and show smooth power-law energy dependence.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a clean probe of relativistic quarkonium dynamics via double-charmonium production channels forbidden in single-photon annihilation—specifically e⁺e⁻ → γ*γ* → χc1+ηc and hc+hc—enabling tests beyond nonrelativistic approximations.

Method: Relativistic calculation within the 4×4 Bethe–Salpeter equation framework; parameters fixed from prior charmonium mass spectroscopy; leading-order evaluation of total cross sections and their √s-dependence via two-photon quark-rearrangement amplitudes.

Result: Predicted cross sections ~10⁻³ fb; hierarchy governed by radial wave-function overlaps; smooth power-law falloff with energy; qualitative agreement with NRQCD ordering but quantitative discrepancy in absolute magnitude.

Conclusion: The results serve as benchmark predictions for experimental searches at high-luminosity e⁺e⁻ colliders and highlight the role of relativistic effects and meson internal structure in double-charmonium production.

Abstract: We present a relativistic study of the exclusive double-charmonium processes $e^-e^+ \to γ^*γ^* \to χ_{c1}+η_c$ and $e^-e^+ \to γ^*γ^* \to h_c+h_c$ at $\sqrt{s}=10.6$ GeV in the framework of the $4\times4$ Bethe-Salpeter equation. Since these channels are forbidden in single-photon annihilation, they proceed purely through two-photon quark-rearrangement mechanisms and thus provide a clean probe of relativistic quarkonium dynamics. Using parameters fixed from mass spectroscopy in our earlier work, we obtain leading-order predictions for the total cross sections and their energy dependence. The predicted cross sections are of order $10^{-3}$ fb and exhibit a hierarchy that originates from the radial structure of the meson wave functions, which determines the overlap integrals contributing to the production amplitude. While the absolute magnitudes of the cross sections differ from NRQCD estimates, the qualitative ordering of the channels is consistent with NRQCD expectations. We find a smooth power-law falloff of the cross section for both $χ_{c1}+η_c$ and $h_c+h_c$. These results provide benchmark predictions for future searches at high-luminosity $e^-e^+$ colliders.

</details>


### [118] [Anomalous triple gauge couplings in the light of dimension-8 operators in $W^+W^-$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04251)
*Daniel Gillies,Andrea Banfi,Adam Martin*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper compares dimension-8 EFT effects in $W^+W^-$ production at the LHC via $q\bar q$ and $\gamma\gamma$ initial states, focusing on bosonic operators affecting anomalous triple and quadruple gauge couplings; it employs logarithmic resummation (via MCFM-RE for $q\bar q$, MadGraph for $\gamma\gamma$) under jet-veto conditions, finds the $\gamma\gamma$ channel dominates bosonic dimension-8 contributions, and derives EFT validity bounds and operator constraints from current/future data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the relative importance of dimension-8 bosonic operators in $W^+W^-$ production, determine which production channel ($q\bar q$ vs. $\gamma\gamma$) better probes higher-dimensional EFT effects, and establish the range of validity of the SMEFT expansion—particularly under jet-veto conditions where large logarithms necessitate resummation.

Method: Matching dimension-6 and dimension-8 bosonic operators to anomalous triple gauge couplings (aTGCs) for the $q\bar q$ channel; using MCFM-RE to resum jet-veto logarithms; treating the $\gamma\gamma$ channel (sensitive to both aTGCs and aQGCs) in MadGraph with factorisation scale set to jet-veto scale; neglecting fermionic dimension-8 operators; combining theoretical (scale, PDF, resummation) and experimental (luminosity, acceptance, systematic) uncertainties in constraint setting.

Result: The $\gamma\gamma$ channel exhibits a dominant bosonic dimension-8 contribution to $W^+W^-$ production when fermionic dimension-8 operators are neglected; this makes it a more sensitive probe of EFT truncation validity than the $q\bar q$ channel; quantitative constraints on dimension-8 operator coefficients are obtained from existing LHC data and projected for HL-LHC.

Conclusion: The $\gamma\gamma$-initiated $W^+W^-$ channel is essential for testing the consistency and convergence of the SMEFT expansion at dimension-8; robust EFT interpretation of diboson data requires inclusion of dimension-8 bosonic effects—especially in $\gamma\gamma$—and proper resummation of jet-veto logarithms; resulting constraints inform the viability of EFT truncations in precision electroweak analyses.

Abstract: We compare the size of dimension-8 effects on $W^+W^-$ production at the LHC arising from the $q\bar q$ and $γγ$ initial states. In particular, we consider bosonic operators, which contribute to the anomalous triple and quadruple gauge couplings. The relevant dimension-6 and dimension-8 operators are matched to the anomalous triple gauge couplings that contribute to the $q\bar q$ channel, allowing for the resummation of large logarithmic contributions arising in the presence of a jet-veto through the program MCFM-RE. For the $γγ$ channel, which receives contributions from both triple and quadruple gauge couplings, such resummation can be performed through the program MadGraph, by simply setting the factorisation scale to the jet-veto scale. We find that, when neglecting fermionic dimension-8 operators, the $γγ$ channel has a dominant bosonic dimension-8 contribution and this channel can be used to understand the range of validity of the Effective Field Theory. With this in mind, and carefully considering theoretical and experimental uncertainties, we provide constraints on higher dimensional operators using current and future data.

</details>


### [119] [Probing axion in the DFSZ model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04128)
*H. N. Long,N. H. T. Nha*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a modified DFSZ I axion model with new right-handed neutrinos, where the Peccei–Quinn transformation depends solely on the PQ charge of the scalar singlet φ, decoupling it from tanβ = vu/vd. It introduces four new scalars (H±, A, two CP-even states at EW scale, and a superheavy inflaton Φ), ensures no axion–GZ mixing via unitary scalar parametrization, and yields both Yukawa-like and anomaly-induced axion–fermion couplings. The model predicts E/N = 8/3 and satisfies current LHC constraints on Higgs trilinear coupling modifier κλ for light H± and A (~150 GeV) and small vu (~few GeV).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a theoretically consistent and phenomenologically viable DFSZ-type axion model where the PQ symmetry is robustly defined—decoupled from electroweak vacuum alignment—and compatible with experimental bounds on Higgs physics and axion couplings.

Method: Field redefinition using unitary scalar matrices to diagonalize kinetic and mass terms; imposition of PQ symmetry with charges assigned to new right-handed neutrinos and scalar singlet φ; computation of axion–fermion couplings (Yukawa-type and anomaly-induced); derivation of axion–photon coupling ratio E/N; analysis of trilinear Higgs self-coupling λh3 and its modifier κλ in the presence of light new scalars and suppressed vu.

Result: PQ transformation becomes independent of tanβ; no axion–GZ mixing; two distinct axion–fermion coupling structures realized; E/N = 8/3 confirms DFSZ I classification; κλ remains within current experimental limits (|κλ − 1| ≲ 0.3–0.5) for mA ≈ mH± ≈ 150 GeV and vu ∼ O(1–10) GeV.

Conclusion: The extended DFSZ I model with NR and scalar singlet φ provides a natural, testable framework unifying axion dark matter, neutrino mass generation, and inflation, while evading stringent collider and Higgs coupling constraints through controlled parameter choices.

Abstract: We show that with the introduction of new right-handed neutrinos $N_R$, the Peccei--Quinn ($PQ$) transformation in the DFSZ (Dine--Fischler--Srednicki--Zhitnitsky) model depends only on the $PQ$ charge of the scalar singlet $φ$, namely $PQ_φ$, and is independent of the sine and cosine of $\arctan(v_u/v_d)$. The model consists of four new scalars: a charged Higgs boson $H^\pm$, a CP-odd scalar $A$, and two CP-even scalars with masses at the EW scale (of a few hundred GeV), and a superheavy inflaton $Φ$. The scalar fields have been presented in the form of unitary matrices, in which there is no mixing between the axion and the Goldstone boson $G_Z$. As a result, there are two kinds of couplings between the axion and fermions, namely Yukawa-like interactions and anomaly-induced ones associated with derivative axion couplings. {The model belongs to the DFSZ I kind since the axion - photon coupling with $\fr E N = \fr 8 3$.} The trilinear Higgs self-coupling is investigated. We have shown that, in the parameter region where new scalar fields such as the singly charged Higgs boson $H^\pm$ and the CP-odd scalar $A$ have masses around 150 GeV and the vacuum expectation value of one Higgs doublet ($v_u$) is of the order of a few GeV, the coupling modifier $κ_λ$-a probe of new physics-is consistent with the current experimental constraints.

</details>


### [120] [Quantum interference effects enhanced in $π^+p$ femtoscopic correlation functions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04351)
*Zhi-Wei Liu,Jun-Xu Lu,Duo-Lun Ge,Li-Sheng Geng*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper resolves the peak-shift puzzle in π⁺p femtoscopic correlations by disentangling scattering and Δ(1232)⁺⁺-decay contributions using Koonin-Pratt formalism with data-driven partial-wave amplitudes; it identifies quantum interference between incident and scattered waves as the origin of the 140 MeV/c peak—a previously unrecognized mechanism in femtoscopy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the long-standing 'peak-shift puzzle' in π⁺p femtoscopic correlation functions—i.e., the unexpected position and mₜ dependence of the correlation peak—in high-multiplicity pp collisions.

Method: Applied the Koonin-Pratt formula with a Gaussian emission source and input data-driven πN partial-wave scattering amplitudes; explicitly included both π⁺p elastic scattering and Δ(1232)⁺⁺ → π⁺p decay contributions to the correlation function; fitted ALICE data across transverse mass (mₜ) bins.

Result: Successfully reproduced ALICE π⁺p correlation data and their mₜ dependence; identified two distinct peaks at k ≈ 140 MeV/c (scattering-dominated) and k ≈ 220 MeV/c (decay-dominated); revealed that the 140 MeV/c peak arises from quantum interference between incident and scattered waves.

Conclusion: The observed π⁺p correlation structure is a mₜ-dependent superposition of scattering and resonance-decay contributions; the 140 MeV/c peak is due to quantum interference—an effect previously overlooked in femtoscopy—providing new insight into wave nature of hadronic correlations and enabling more precise source imaging.

Abstract: We present a comprehensive analysis of the $π^+p$ femtoscopic correlation functions measured by the ALICE Collaboration in high-multiplicity $pp$ collisions at $\sqrt{s}=13$ TeV. Using the Koonin-Pratt formula with a Gaussian source and data-driven $πN$ partial-wave amplitudes, we account for the contributions from $π^+p$ scattering and $Δ(1232)^{++}$-decay, thereby successfully reproducing the measured data and their transverse-mass ($m_T$) dependence. The scattering contribution yields a peak near the relative momentum $k\approx140$ MeV/$c$, whereas the decay contribution peaks around $k\approx220$ MeV/$c$. The observed correlation peak results from a weighted sum of the two contributions, with $m_T$-dependent relative weights. We find that the 140 MeV/$c$ peak originates from quantum interference between the incident and scattered waves-a mechanism previously unnoticed in femtoscopic studies. This finding resolves the peak-shift puzzle in $π^+p$ correlations and provides a novel perspective for quantum interference effects in femtoscopy.

</details>


### [121] [Left-right symmetry breaking in $E_6^L\times E_6^R$ occurs only in spacetime -- with possible implications for strong $CP$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04132)
*Tejinder P. Singh*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a two-sided octonionic E6×E6 unification framework where left-right symmetry is interpreted as a spacetime (parity) operation rather than an internal symmetry, eliminating the need for ad hoc suppression of a second colour group; it demonstrates anomaly constraints that force dark electromagnetic U(1)_dem to act only on the dark sector, renders visible fermions dem-neutral, recovers hypercharge naturally, and links vanishing QCD θ-angle and tree-level CP conservation in quark mass matrices to spontaneous parity breaking.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the problem of an unwanted second colour group SU(3)_c' in two-sided octonionic E6^L × E6^R unification, and to consistently embed left-right symmetry, hypercharge, anomaly cancellation, CP properties, and dark sector coupling without fine-tuning or empirical inputs beyond the Jordan algebraic mass scale √m.

Method: Uses gravi-weak interpretation where SU(2)_R acts as gravitational frame group (i.e., spacetime parity on Dirac fields); applies anomaly cancellation analysis for U(1) extensions; derives hypercharge from charge quantization and weak isospin; employs exceptional Jordan algebra (trace-split) for mass operator labeling; analyzes spontaneous parity breaking implications for θ_QCD and arg det M.

Result: Shows SU(3)_c' collapses to ordinary SU(3)_c due to spacetime nature of left-right exchange; proves all anomaly-free U(1)s on visible fermions lie in span{Q, B−L}, excluding √m-based assignments; identifies U(1)_dem as dark-sector-only with no fifth force; recovers Y = Q − T^3_L without right-sector generator; obtains θ_QCD = 0 and arg det M = 0 at tree level while permitting CKM phase.

Conclusion: The gravi-weak spacetime interpretation of left-right symmetry eliminates redundant internal structures, enforces strict anomaly-driven constraints on gauge extensions, yields predictive CP and strong CP behavior, and positions the exceptional Jordan algebra’s √m as a spectral label—not a charge—thereby unifying geometric, algebraic, and phenomenological aspects of particle physics with minimal input.

Abstract: Two-sided octonionic $E_6^L\times E_6^R$ unification carries a nominal second colour $SU(3)_{c'}$ which, gauged, would colour the charged lepton and must be suppressed by hand. We argue no such device is needed: the left-right symmetry acts in spacetime, not internally -- in the gravi-weak reading $SU(2)_R$ is the gravitational frame group and the exchange is ordinary parity on the Dirac field; a spacetime operation cannot double the internal colour, so $SU(3)_{c'}=SU(3)_c$, one vector-like colour with a colour-singlet electron. The right sector contributes one colour-blind datum, $\sqrt m$ (values: the exceptional-Jordan trace split, an input; $N_R$ fixes only the colour representation). An anomaly no-go proven here shows every anomaly-free $U(1)$ on visible fermions lies in span$\{Q,B-L\}$, which the $\sqrt m$ pattern does not, in any sign or chirality assignment. The gauged dark-electromagnetic $U(1)_{dem}$ -- the mirror electroweak chain's unbroken remnant -- therefore carries the parity-mirror of electric charge on the dark sector: the visible fermions are dem-neutral (kinetic mixing the sole portal; no visible fifth force), and $\sqrt m$ enters visible physics only as the spectral label of the Jordan mass operator. The same recognition recovers hypercharge as the consistency relation $Y=Q-T^3_L$, $Q=N/3$, with no right-sector generator. As an application, the spontaneous parity forbids the QCD vacuum angle ($θ_{QCD}=0$, conditional on the gravi-weak identification), while the flavour rotors have real determinant, so $\arg\det M=0$ at tree level (exact for the Cabibbo rung, texture-contingent in full), coexisting with a nonzero CKM phase. Loop stability -- where minimal gauged-$SU(2)_R$ parity solutions fail -- is plausibly evaded (no gauged $W_R$) but open, pending the Higgs-bridge matrix elements. We tag throughout what is derived, what is input, and what is open.

</details>


### [122] [Probing Neutrinophilic Long-Range Forces at DUNE](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04441)
*Sudip Jana,Pragyanprasu Swain*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a 'dark neutrino portal' where neutrino mass generation is tied to a light dark sector under a new U(1)_D symmetry; active–dark neutrino mixing mediates ultra-long-range forces from astrophysical and cosmological matter, altering oscillations; DUNE’s long-baseline, matter-sensitive design enables discovery of unexplored parameter space, including regions where the same coupling explains neutrino self-interactions relevant to the Hubble tension.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain neutrino masses and probe physics beyond the Standard Model via neutrino oscillations, while linking them to a light dark sector that could address cosmological anomalies like the Hubble tension.

Method: Theoretical construction of a neutrinophilic U(1)_D dark sector coupled only via active–dark neutrino mixing; computation of matter-induced potentials from Earth, Moon, Sun, Milky Way, and cosmological density; phenomenological analysis of oscillation modifications in DUNE using its baseline, energy spectrum, and matter profile.

Result: DUNE can probe previously inaccessible regions of the dark neutrino portal parameter space; the model simultaneously yields observable matter effects and sizable neutrino self-interactions compatible with current oscillation data and potentially relevant for resolving the Hubble tension.

Conclusion: The dark neutrino portal is a theoretically motivated and experimentally testable framework; DUNE offers unique sensitivity to ultra-long-range neutrino interactions mediated by a light dark sector, bridging neutrino physics, dark sector phenomenology, and cosmology.

Abstract: Neutrino oscillations provide compelling evidence for physics beyond the Standard Model, while the weakly interacting nature of neutrinos makes them powerful probes of new interactions and hidden sectors. In this work, we investigate a \textit{dark neutrino portal} scenario in which neutrino mass generation is linked to a light dark sector charged under a new $U(1)_D$ gauge symmetry. While Standard Model fields remain neutral under $U(1)_D$, the dark neutrino sector is charged and communicates with the Standard Model exclusively through active--dark neutrino mixing. The associated neutrinophilic mediator induces ultra-long-range interactions, whereby electrons and neutrons in the Earth, Moon, Sun, Milky Way, and the cosmological matter distribution generate sizable matter potentials that modify neutrino oscillations. We explore the sensitivity of the upcoming Deep Underground Neutrino Experiment (DUNE), whose long baseline and pronounced matter effects make it uniquely suited to probe such interactions. We show that DUNE can access previously unexplored regions of parameter space and demonstrate that the same underlying coupling can simultaneously give rise to sizable neutrino self-interactions, including regions relevant for alleviating the Hubble tension, while remaining consistent with current neutrino oscillation constraints.

</details>


### [123] [A MeV-Scale Dark QCD Solution to the Axion Domain Wall Problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04137)
*Jun Guo,Zhaofeng Kang,Jiang Zhu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Proposes a novel axion solution to the strong CP problem by introducing a mixed anomaly between the PQ symmetry and a hidden SU(N_c) color group, generating a new cosine potential from hidden instantons; this avoids domain walls while maintaining θ ≈ 0 via fine-tuned dark QCD scale (0.1–3 MeV), yielding testable GW signals (PTA-probed) and MeV di-photon signatures from axion–dark-glueball mixing.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the axion domain wall problem inherent in standard Peccei–Quinn solutions to the strong CP problem, without reintroducing fine-tuning or conflicting with dark matter constraints.

Method: Introduces a mixed U(1)_PQ–SU(N_c) anomaly, inducing a hidden-instanton-generated cosine potential that explicitly breaks the Z_{N_DW} discrete symmetry; analyzes vacuum structure, effective θ dynamics, dark QCD scale tuning, and phenomenological consequences including gravitational wave spectra from domain wall collapse and di-photon signals from axion–dark-glueball mixing.

Result: Identifies viable parameter space with dark QCD scale Λ_dark ∈ [0.1, 3] MeV where domain walls are destabilized, θ_eff remains naturally small, dark glueballs satisfy relic density bounds, and observable GWs (detectable by current PTAs) and di-photon lines (requiring next-gen MeV telescopes) emerge.

Conclusion: The mixed-anomaly mechanism provides a consistent, predictive, and testable resolution to both the strong CP and axion domain wall problems, linking high-energy QCD physics to multi-messenger astroparticle observables.

Abstract: PQ solution to the strong CP problem probably encounters the axion domain wall problem. In this article, we propose a simple and testable solution, assuming that the $U(1)_{\rm PQ}$ possesses mixed anomaly to a hidden $SU(N_c)$ color. Then, the axion field receives a new cosine potential from the hidden instantons, which breaks the $Z_{N_{\rm DW}}$ subgroup explicitly. The new potential lifts the vacua degeneracy, but also drives the effective $θ$ angle away from the origin, re-incuring the strong CP problem. However, we find that the dark QCD scale within the 0.1 to 3 MeV window survives, maintaining a delicate balance. Two observational signatures are explored: gravitational waves from domain wall collapse, already probed by current PTAs, and di-photon signals from axion-dark-glueball mixing, which require next-generation MeV telescopes. The scenario favors a cold dark QCD sector consistent with dark glueball relic constraints.

</details>


### [124] [Implications of exclusive photon leptoproduction measurements for the proton charge-radius puzzle](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04481)
*The MMGPDs Collaboration,Muhammad Goharipour,Anoushiravan Moradi,K. Azizi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reanalyzes proton electromagnetic form factors using high-precision exclusive photon leptoproduction (EP) data dominated by the Bethe-Heitler process, finding consistency across most datasets except CLAS 2018; it yields a smaller proton charge radius—consistent with PRad and muonic hydrogen—but a magnetic radius aligned with the world average, supporting the small-radius solution to the proton charge-radius puzzle.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve inconsistencies in proton electromagnetic form factors (FFs) and assess the proton charge-radius puzzle using BH-dominated exclusive photon leptoproduction (EP) as an independent probe.

Method: Phenomenological analysis of high-precision EP data from CLAS and Hall A collaborations; multi-scenario fitting of FFs with kinematic cuts (e.g., low |t|) to handle CLAS 2018 tension; extraction of charge and magnetic radii from fitted FFs.

Result: Significant tension observed with CLAS 2018 data; stable, consistent FF extractions achieved after excluding CLAS 2018 or applying low-|t| cuts; extracted charge radius smaller than PDG average and most elastic e-p scattering results but consistent with PRad and muonic hydrogen; magnetic radius compatible with world average.

Conclusion: BH-dominated EP provides an independent, complementary method for probing proton structure; results reinforce the small-proton-charge-radius solution and validate EP as a robust tool for resolving the charge-radius puzzle.

Abstract: In the present study, we extend our previous analysis of the proton electromagnetic form factors (FFs) extracted from exclusive photon leptoproduction (EP) measurements in kinematic regions where the Bethe-Heitler (BH) process dominates the cross section by including all currently available high-precision EP data from the CLAS and Hall~A Collaborations. Using the same phenomenological framework, we investigate the consistency among the different data sets, determine the proton electromagnetic FFs within several fitting scenarios, and extract the corresponding charge and magnetic radii. A significant tension is observed between the CLAS 2018 measurements and the remaining EP data. We show that excluding this data set, or restricting its kinematic coverage by imposing suitable low-$|t|$ cuts, leads to stable fits with good quality and consistent FFs. For all analyses, the extracted proton charge radius is smaller than the Particle Data Group average and most determinations based on elastic electron-proton scattering. However, the results are consistent, within uncertainties, with the PRad measurement and muonic hydrogen spectroscopy. In contrast, the magnetic radius is found to be compatible with the current world average. These results demonstrate that BH-dominated EP measurements provide an independent and complementary approach to determine the electromagnetic structure of the proton and offer additional support for the small-radius solution of the proton charge-radius puzzle.

</details>


### [125] [Advancing axion detection: Photon regeneration in high-sensitivity Penning trap experiments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04155)
*Yao Chen,Ju Guo,Libo Zhao,Ziyao Yan,Jie Feng*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a novel lab-based axion search method combining photon regeneration ('light-shining-through-a-wall') with high-sensitivity Penning traps and microwave cavities, achieving unprecedented sensitivity $g_{aγγ} \le 7.10\times 10^{-8}~\mathrm{GeV^{-1}}$ in one day for sub-MHz axions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome astrophysical uncertainties and improve laboratory sensitivity in the search for axions—hypothetical particles solving the strong CP problem and serving as dark matter candidates.

Method: Integration of photon regeneration (LSW) with Penning traps (enabling precise EM field measurement, strong B-fields, and single-particle detection) and resonant microwave cavities for enhanced signal amplification.

Result: Preliminary calculations show sensitivity to axion-photon coupling down to $g_{aγγ} \le 7.10\times 10^{-8}~\mathrm{GeV^{-1}}$ within one day for axion masses < 1 MHz.

Conclusion: The proposed setup provides a robust, controlled, high-sensitivity platform for laboratory axion detection, representing a significant advance over existing methods.

Abstract: The axion, a hypothetical particle proposed to solve the strong CP problem and considered a viable candidate for dark matter, has prompted extensive experimental efforts for its detection. This study presents a novel approach combining photon regeneration techniques ("light-shining-through-a-wall") with high-sensitivity Penning trap technologies to enhance the search for axions. Penning traps offer significant advantages, including precise electromagnetic field measurement, strong magnetic fields, and single-particle detection capabilities. By integrating these traps with resonantly enhanced photon regeneration using microwave cavities, our proposed method significantly increases sensitivity to axion-photon couplings. Preliminary calculations demonstrate an unprecedented achievable sensitivity, reaching an axion-photon coupling constant limit of $g_{aγγ} \le 7.10\times 10^{-8}\mathrm{GeV ^{-1}}$ in just one day, specifically targeting axion energies below 1 MHz. This experimental setup presents a robust and controlled platform, circumventing astrophysical uncertainties, and represents a substantial advancement in laboratory searches for axions and our understanding of dark matter.

</details>


### [126] [Exclusive Quark and Gluon Dijet Production as Probes of GPDs at Collider Energies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04482)
*Zhuoyi Pang,Paweł Sznajder,Lech Szymanowski,Jakub Wagner*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies exclusive electroproduction of dijets using collinear factorization to probe generalized parton distributions (GPDs), extending prior work to include helicity GPDs and an additional electromagnetic channel for quark dijets, while also analyzing gluon dijet production; predictions are compared with HERA data and projected for the Electron-Ion Collider.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To use exclusive electroproduction of dijets as a novel probe of generalized parton distributions (GPDs), particularly extending sensitivity to helicity-dependent GPDs and accessing gluon GPDs via gluon dijet production.

Method: Collinear factorization framework applied to exclusive electroproduction of quark and gluon dijets; includes contributions from helicity GPDs and an additional leading-order electromagnetic channel mediated by elastic nucleon form factors.

Result: Quantitative predictions for dijet cross sections consistent with existing HERA data; projections indicate enhanced sensitivity to GPDs—especially gluon and helicity GPDs—at the future Electron-Ion Collider.

Conclusion: Exclusive dijet electroproduction is a viable and complementary channel for probing both quark and gluon GPDs, including their helicity structure, with improved experimental prospects at the Electron-Ion Collider.

Abstract: We study exclusive electroproduction of dijets in the collinear factorization framework as a probe of generalized parton distributions (GPDs). For quark dijet production, we extend previous analyses by including contributions from helicity GPDs and by assessing an additional leading-order electromagnetic channel governed by elastic nucleon form factors. Furthermore, we investigate exclusive gluon dijet production. We compare our prediction to HERA data and provide projections for measurements at the future Electron-Ion Collider.

</details>


### [127] [The variable flavor number scheme to three-loop order](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05235)
*J. Ablinger,A. Behring,J. Blümlein,A. De Freitas,A. von Manteuffel,C. Schneider,K. Schönwald*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops the variable flavor number scheme (VFNS) to three-loop order, incorporating single- and two-mass effects into massless parton distribution functions (PDFs) and introducing heavy-quark PDFs for charm and bottom; it validates the scheme via renormalization group analysis at large $Q^2$ and provides numerical implementations of charged/neutral current Wilson coefficients.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To consistently incorporate heavy-quark mass effects into perturbative QCD calculations of deep-inelastic scattering and related processes, bridging fixed-flavor-number and zero-mass schemes with controlled power-suppressed corrections.

Method: Three-loop perturbative QCD calculation within the variable flavor number scheme; renormalization group analysis to establish scale evolution and consistency; derivation and numerical evaluation of Wilson coefficients for charged and neutral currents.

Result: Consistent three-loop VFNS formulation including charm and bottom PDFs; proof of asymptotic equivalence to full non-power-suppressed heavy-flavor corrections at large $Q^2$; publicly available numerical results for key Wilson coefficients.

Conclusion: The three-loop VFNS provides a robust, theoretically sound framework for precision QCD analyses involving heavy flavors, enabling accurate interpolation between low- and high-energy regimes while preserving perturbative control.

Abstract: We describe the variable flavor number scheme to three-loop order, which modifies the massless parton densities by single- and two-mass effects and introduces heavy-quark parton distribution functions for charm and bottom. A renormalization group analysis shows the validity of this picture at large scales $Q^2$, where it resembles the non-power-suppressed heavy-flavor corrections completely. We also provide numerical implementations of a series of charged and neutral current Wilson coefficients.

</details>


### [128] [Lagrangian-based model of the $e^+e^- \to V \bar V$ process applied to the $e^+e^- \to D^{\ast+} D^{\ast-}$ data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04210)
*Peter Lichard*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Derives a Breit-Wigner-type formula for e+e− → VV̄ using QFT and validates it against BESIII D*+D*− data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a theoretically grounded, observable-parameterized resonance formula for e+e− annihilation into vector meson–antimeson pairs.

Method: Quantum field theory derivation of a Breit-Wigner-type amplitude for e+e− → VV̄, followed by parameter fitting to experimental BESIII data.

Result: A consistent Breit-Wigner-type formula whose parameters are directly linked to physical observables (e.g., mass, width, coupling), successfully fitted to e+e− → D*+D*− cross-section data.

Conclusion: The derived formula provides a robust, QFT-based description of near-threshold vector meson pair production, enabling precise extraction of resonance properties from e+e− collider data.

Abstract: Using the quantum field theory, we derive a Breit-Wigner-type formula for the $e^+ e^-$ annihilation into a vector meson and its antiparticle, and relate the formula parameters to observable quantities. The formula's soundness is checked by fitting the $e^+ e^- \to D^{\ast+}D^{\ast-}$ data published by the BESIII Collaboration in 2022.

</details>


### [129] [Vector meson photoproduction on the nucleus and the extraction of the nuclear suppression factor using asymmetric beams](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04228)
*Ronan McNulty,Wolfgang Schafer*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes using asymmetric proton-lead collisions at the LHC to unambiguously measure nuclear suppression in vector meson photoproduction, avoiding the two-fold ambiguity inherent in symmetric nucleus-nucleus collisions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the two-fold ambiguity in identifying the photon-emitting nucleus in symmetric nucleus-nucleus collisions when measuring vector meson photoproduction cross-sections on nuclei.

Method: Analysis of photoproduction cross-section ratios between proton and nucleus targets in asymmetric proton-lead collisions; evaluation of feasibility using existing and future LHC data.

Result: Demonstration that proton-lead collisions provide a clean, minimally model-dependent measurement of the nuclear suppression factor for vector meson photoproduction.

Conclusion: Asymmetric proton-nucleus collisions, particularly at the LHC, offer a robust and feasible method to determine nuclear shadowing effects in coherent photoproduction without the ambiguities of symmetric heavy-ion collisions.

Abstract: The cross-section for photoproduction of a vector meson on a nucleus is usually determined in nucleus-nucleus collisions, which introduces a two-fold ambiguity as to which nucleus emitted the photon. This can be resolved in asymmetric collisions of protons and nuclei, where the ratio of photoproduction cross-sections on the proton and nucleus directly measures the nuclear suppression factor with minimal model dependence. The feasibilty of using existing and future proton-lead collisions at the LHC is evaluated.

</details>


### [130] [Comment on "Radiative corrections to tau -> pi(K) nu_tau[gamma]: A reliable new physics test"](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04435)
*Markus Finkemeier*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper identifies and corrects a sign error in the axial-vector convention used by GR10 and subsequently adopted by AHLRR in computing the structure-dependent amplitude for τ→Pν_τγ decays; the correction flips the vector-sector sign relative to the chiral anomaly but preserves agreement with O(p⁴) chiral predictions in the axial sector, yielding minor numerical shifts in δr_SD (−0.004% for π, −0.02% for K).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve an inconsistency between the resonance chiral theory amplitude for τ→Pν_τγ from GR10 and the standard chiral anomaly and O(p⁴) chiral predictions, arising from a sign error in the axial-vector convention used in translating form factors.

Method: Re-examination of the convention dictionary relating Decker-Finkemeier (DF) and Guo-Roig (GR10) form factor definitions; verification of consistency with chiral symmetry constraints (chiral anomaly and O(p⁴) axial current prediction); recalculation of the structure-dependent radiative correction δr_SD using the corrected dictionary.

Result: The axial convention in GR10 and AHLRR contains a sign error: F_A^DF = −2√2 m_P F_A^GR, not +2√2 m_P F_A^GR. With correction, GR10’s axial amplitude matches O(p⁴) chiral prediction, while its vector amplitude acquires the correct relative sign versus the chiral anomaly. Numerically, δr_SD changes from +0.150% to +0.146% (π) and from +0.18% to ~+0.16% (K).

Conclusion: The sign error invalidates AHLRR’s identification of their δr_SD with the pre-addendum GR10 result; however, the physical prediction remains robust, differing only at the sub-permil level—confirming the reliability of the Decker-Finkemeier addendum and the consistency of resonance chiral theory with low-energy QCD constraints.

Abstract: Arroyo-Ureña, Hernández-Tomé, López-Castro, Roig, and Rosell (AHLRR) take the structure-dependent (SD) amplitude for $τ\to P ν_τγ$ from the resonance chiral theory result of Guo and Roig (GR10), obtaining $δr_{SD} = +0.15\%$ in the pion channel. The convention dictionary in footnote 2 of GR10, repeated in footnote 1 of AHLRR, has the wrong sign in its axial entry: the printed amplitudes imply $F_A^{DF} = -2\sqrt{2}\, m_P F_A^{GR}$, not $+2\sqrt{2}\, m_P F_A^{GR}$. With the corrected dictionary, the GR10 amplitude agrees with the $O(p^4)$ chiral prediction in the axial sector but carries the opposite sign to the chiral anomaly in the vector sector; the relative sign fixed in the Decker-Finkemeier addendum is confirmed in both sectors. The identification in footnote 6 of AHLRR of their value with the pre-addendum result rests on the erroneous dictionary and does not hold. The numerical impact is small: $δr_{SD}$ shifts from $+0.150\%$ to $+0.146\%$ in the pion channel and from $+0.18\%$ to about $+0.16\%$ in the kaon channel.

</details>


### [131] [Shifted Hybrid Realization of Non-Minimal Higgs Inflation in Light of ACT DR6 and Planck Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04504)
*Nadir Ijaz,Pirzada,Mansoor Ur Rehman*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies non-minimal Higgs inflation in a no-scale supergravity framework, showing how a leading nonrenormalizable operator deforms the Starobinsky attractor to align the scalar spectral index with recent CMB data (ACT DR6/Planck) while keeping r ~ 10⁻³–10⁻² and ensuring sub-Planckian field values, controlled SUGRA corrections, and viable reheating + leptogenesis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile Higgs inflation predictions—particularly the scalar spectral index n_s—with tighter constraints from ACT DR6 and Planck, which disfavor the canonical Starobinsky value; and to embed Higgs inflation consistently in supergravity with natural field ranges and post-inflationary viability.

Method: Constructs a shifted hybrid inflation model in no-scale-inspired supergravity where the GUT Higgs direction acts as inflaton after orthogonal fields are stabilized; derives effective single-field potential including leading nonrenormalizable superpotential term; computes inflationary observables (n_s, r), analyzes SUGRA corrections, performs numerical scan of parameter space including reheating dynamics and nonthermal leptogenesis.

Result: The nonrenormalizable operator induces a controlled deformation of the Starobinsky attractor, shifting n_s into the observationally preferred range (~0.965–0.97) while preserving r ≈ 10⁻³–10⁻²; inflaton remains sub-Planckian; SUGRA corrections are under control; viable parameter regions satisfying CMB, reheating, and successful leptogenesis are identified.

Conclusion: Non-minimal Higgs inflation in this no-scale supergravity setting provides a theoretically consistent, phenomenologically viable alternative to standard Starobinsky inflation, reconciling high-scale inflation with current CMB data and enabling testable post-inflationary physics including leptogenesis.

Abstract: We investigate non-minimal Higgs inflation in a no-scale-inspired supergravity framework and confront its predictions with the latest CMB constraints from ACT DR6 and \emph{Planck}. Working within a shifted hybrid inflation scenario, we construct an effective single-field description in which the GUT Higgs direction serves as the inflaton after stabilization of the orthogonal scalar fields. We show that the inclusion of the leading nonrenormalizable operator in the superpotential induces a controlled deformation of the Starobinsky attractor, allowing the scalar spectral index to be shifted into the range favored by recent ACT and related CMB datasets while maintaining a small tensor-to-scalar ratio, $r \sim 10^{-3}-10^{-2}$. The resulting inflationary dynamics remain theoretically consistent, with controlled supergravity corrections and sub-Planckian inflaton field values. We perform a detailed numerical analysis of the model parameter space, including reheating and nonthermal leptogenesis, and identify regions that simultaneously satisfy current observational constraints and yield a viable post-inflationary cosmological history.

</details>


### [132] [Possibility of the antibottom-strange molecular pentaquarks near $\bar BΣ$ and $\bar B^*Σ$ thresholds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04679)
*Jian-Kang Zhao,Nijiati Yalikun*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates molecular states in the coupled-channel system $B_s^0N-\bar BΛ-\bar B^*Λ-\bar BΣ-\bar B^*Σ$ using the one-boson-exchange model with $S$-$D$ wave mixing and a short-range $δ(r)$ term; it identifies bound, resonant, and virtual states via analytic continuation of the $S$-matrix, finds three near-threshold poles (two resonances and one cutoff-sensitive virtual state) in $J^P = 1/2^-, 3/2^-$ sectors, predicts masses $6.44–6.52$ GeV and narrow widths, attributes pole formation dominantly to $\bar BΣ$/$\bar B^*Σ$ interactions, and suggests experimental signatures in lower open channels — supporting near-threshold pentaquark $P_{\bar b s}$ candidates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the existence and properties of near-threshold molecular pentaquark states $P_{\bar b s}$ in the $B_s^0N-\bar BΛ-\bar B^*Λ-\bar BΣ-\bar B^*Σ$ coupled-channel system, motivated by recent experimental interest in hidden-bottom and bottom-strange exotic hadrons and theoretical uncertainty regarding short-range dynamics and coupled-channel effects.

Method: One-boson-exchange (OBE) model incorporating $S$-$D$ wave mixing and a tunable short-range $δ(r)$ contact interaction; full coupled-channel Lippmann-Schwinger equation solved numerically; $S$-matrix analytically continued into the complex energy plane to locate bound, resonant, and virtual poles; pole coupling and partial-width analyses performed to identify dominant channel contributions.

Result: Three near-threshold poles found: (i) a $J^P = 1/2^-$ pole below $\bar B^*Σ$ threshold highly sensitive to $δ(r)$ (becomes virtual when removed), (ii) $1/2^-$ and (iii) $3/2^-$ poles robust as resonances; predicted masses $6.44–6.52$ GeV, widths few–several MeV; dominant couplings to $\bar BΣ$ and $\bar B^*Σ$; strongest observable signals expected in $B_s^0N$, $\bar BΛ$, and $\bar B^*Λ$ invariant mass spectra.

Conclusion: The analysis supports the existence of narrow, near-threshold molecular pentaquark states $P_{\bar b s}$ arising from $\bar BΣ/\bar B^*Σ$ dynamics; the $δ(r)$ term critically affects virtual-state identification but not the main resonant structures; results provide concrete mass/width predictions and decay-channel guidance for experimental searches at LHCb, Belle II, and future facilities.

Abstract: The molecular states in coupled channel system of $B_s^0N-\bar BΛ-\bar B^*Λ-\bar BΣ-\bar B^*Σ$ are investigated within one-boson-exchange model that includes $S$-$D$ wave mixing and a tunable short-range $δ(r)$ term. Bound, resonant, and virtual states are searched by analytically continuing the $S$ matrix in the complex energy plane. In the single-channel analysis, the $\bar BΣ$ and $\bar B^*Σ$ systems with $J^P=1/2^-$ and $3/2^-$ are found to be attractive and form three bound states with reasonable cutoff region, corresponding to $1/2^-(\bar BΣ)$, $1/2^-(\bar B^*Σ)$, and $3/2^-(\bar B^*Σ)$ . When coupled-channel dynamics is included, these states evolve into near-threshold poles that produce enhancements in the $B_s^0N$, $\bar BΛ$, and $\bar B^*Λ$ invariant mass spectra. The pole below the $\bar B^*Σ$ threshold in the $J^P=1/2^-$ system depends strongly on the treatment of the short-range $δ(r)$ term and becomes a virtual state when this contribution is removed, while the other two resonant poles and their line shapes are only moderately affected. For representative parameter region, the predicted masses of these states lie in the $6.44-6.52$ GeV region and have widths of a few to several MeV. The pole coupling and partial-width analyses indicate that the poles are generated mainly by the $\bar BΣ$ and $\bar B^*Σ$ channels interaction, and their observable signals are expected mainly in the lower open channels $B_s^0N$, $\bar BΛ$, and $\bar B^*Λ$. These results support the existence of near-threshold molecular pentaquark $P_{\bar b s}$ and provide useful guidance in future experimental searches.

</details>


### [133] [Pion Distribution Amplitudes from Functional QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04871)
*Lei Chang,Wei-jie Fu,Chuang Huang,Jan M. Pawlowski,Yang-yang Tan*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: First-principles fRG-based QCD calculation of pion distribution amplitude using LaMET, achieving high-momentum saturation (P_z = 4.5 GeV) and yielding ⟨ξ²⟩_π = 0.267 — smaller than lattice-LaMET but consistent with other nonperturbative methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain a first-principles, nonperturbative determination of the pion distribution amplitude (DA) free from lattice artifacts and extrapolation uncertainties inherent in conventional lattice-LaMET approaches.

Method: Functional renormalisation group (fRG) formulation of QCD combined with large-momentum effective theory (LaMET); computes quasi-DA from first-principles QCD correlation functions using only strong coupling and current quark masses as inputs; pushes pion longitudinal momentum to P_z = 4.5 GeV to suppress light-cone extrapolation errors.

Result: Quasi-DA fully saturated at P_z = 4.5 GeV; second Mellin moment ⟨ξ²⟩_π = 0.267 with negligible extrapolation error to the light-cone limit.

Conclusion: The fRG-LaMET approach provides a robust, ab initio determination of the pion DA, yielding a narrower distribution (smaller ⟨ξ²⟩_π) than most lattice-LaMET results, aligning better with phenomenological and other nonperturbative estimates.

Abstract: We present the first functional QCD calculation of the pion distribution amplitude (DA) using the large-momentum effective theory within the functional renormalisation group (fRG) approach. With only the strong coupling and current quark masses as inputs, we compute the quasi-DA from first-principles QCD correlation functions. By pushing the pion momentum up to $P_z = 4.5\ \mathrm{GeV}$, the quasi-DA becomes fully saturated, rendering the extrapolation errors to the light-cone limit negligible. The resulting second-order moment $\langle ξ^2 \rangle_π= 0.267$ is significantly smaller than existing lattice-LaMET determinations and lies in a range consistent with other nonperturbative approaches.

</details>


### [134] [Beyond Half-Life Limits: Robust Operator-Level Interpretation of Multi-Isotope Neutrinoless Double-Beta Decay](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04932)
*K. Ishidoshiro*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes how neutrinoless double-beta decay (0νββ) limits translate into constraints on low-energy lepton-number-violating (LNV) operators, assessing stability across isotopes and nuclear matrix element (NME) methods using the νDoBe tool; it finds light-Majorana exchange most stable, dimension-six operators robust, and dimension-nine short-range operators highly sensitive to NME/isotope choices—highlighting multi-isotope programs as essential for validating operator interpretations post-discovery.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the robustness of operator-level interpretations of 0νββ limits and sensitivities across different isotopes and nuclear matrix element (NME) methods, enabling reliable connection to ultraviolet models of lepton-number violation.

Method: Uses νDoBe to convert experimental or projected 0νββ half-life limits into constraints on low-energy LNV coefficients for multiple isotopes, operator classes (e.g., light-Majorana, dimension-six, dimension-nine), and NME methods; quantifies stability via spread of coefficient limits (pre-discovery) and isotope-to-isotope half-life ratios (post-discovery).

Result: Light-Majorana exchange yields the most stable coefficient limits; dimension-six operators show reasonable robustness; dimension-nine short-range operators exhibit strong dependence on isotope and NME method; isotope-ratio tests post-discovery are only reliable when half-life patterns remain stable across NME methods.

Conclusion: Multi-isotope 0νββ programs provide critical information beyond sensitivity reach by testing the stability of operator interpretations; spread-based analysis identifies which LNV scenarios are suitable for isotope-ratio tests and which require caution in UV model mapping.

Abstract: Current and future neutrinoless double-beta decay ($0νββ$) searches are usually characterized by half-life limits or projected sensitivities. For operator-level interpretation, however, the relevant question is not only the strength of the limit, but also the stability of the inferred constraints on low-energy lepton-number-violating (LNV) coefficients under changes of isotope and nuclear matrix-element (NME) method. Using $ν$DoBe, we convert half-life limits or projected sensitivities into limits on low-energy LNV coefficients for several choices of isotope, operator class, and NME method. We then compare the resulting limits across isotopes and NME methods. We quantify the stability by the spread of coefficient limits in non-observation scenarios and, for single-operator post-discovery benchmarks, by the spread of isotope-to-isotope half-life ratios. For the operator examples considered in this paper, the light-Majorana exchange mechanism gives the most stable interpretation. The selected dimension-six operators remain reasonably robust, while the short-range dimension-nine examples show a stronger dependence on isotope and NME-method choices. The same hierarchy appears in post-discovery benchmarks based on isotope-to-isotope half-life ratios: the reliability of such tests depends on whether the predicted half-life pattern remains stable across NME methods. A multi-isotope $0νββ$ program therefore provides information beyond half-life reach by testing the stability of the operator interpretation.
