This spread-based analysis provides a practical way to identify which low-energy operator scenarios are suitable for isotope-ratio tests after a possible discovery, and which require more care before being connected to ultraviolet models of lepton-number violation.

</details>


### [135] [Flavor decomposition of the gravitational form factors and mechanical structure of the proton](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05079)
*Hyun-Chul Kim,June-Young Kim,Ho-Yeon Won*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reviews recent work on flavor decomposition of gravitational form factors and mechanical properties of the proton using the chiral quark-soliton model, with twist projection of the energy-momentum tensor to isolate the $\overline{c}$ form factor from twist-4 contributions; it presents flavor-separated mass, spin, pressure, and shear-force distributions and form factors, revealing up-quark dominance in mass/spin and significant strange-quark contribution to the $D$-term, and analyzes mechanical stability via pressure and shear-force distributions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the internal flavor structure of the proton's gravitational form factors and its mechanical properties—particularly how different quark flavors contribute to mass, spin, pressure, shear force, and stability—within a theoretically grounded framework.

Method: Uses the chiral quark-soliton model; derives the energy-momentum tensor operator from the QCD instanton vacuum; performs twist projection into twist-2 and twist-4 components to isolate the $\overline{c}$ form factor (twist-4 origin); computes flavor-decomposed gravitational form factors and spatial distributions (mass, spin, pressure, shear force).

Result: Up quark dominates proton mass and spin; strange quark contributes significantly to the $D$-term (related to internal stress/pressure distribution); full flavor-decomposed mass, spin, pressure, and shear-force distributions and associated form factors are obtained; mechanical stability conditions (e.g., von Mises criterion or equilibrium equations) are discussed based on pressure and shear-force profiles.

Conclusion: Flavor decomposition reveals distinct roles of u, d, and s quarks in proton mechanics: while valence quarks drive mass and spin, sea quarks—especially strange—play a non-negligible role in mechanical stability via the $D$-term and stress distributions; this supports the necessity of including higher-twist (e.g., twist-4) effects for a complete picture of proton structure.

Abstract: We review in the present talk a series of recent works on the flavor decomposition of the gravitational form factors of the proton and its mechanical properties within the framework of the chiral quark-soliton model. Starting from the energy-momentum tensor operator derived from the QCD instanton vacuum, we carry out the twist projection of the energy-momentum tensor operator into its twist-2 and twist-4 components, which enables us to isolate the $\overline{c}$ form factor originating from the twist-4 operator. We present the flavor-decomposed mass, spin, pressure, and shear-force distributions of the proton, together with the corresponding form factors. While the up quark dominates both the mass and the spin of the proton, the strange quark is found to contribute sizably to the $D$-term form factor. We also discuss the mechanical stability of the proton governed by the pressure and shear-force distributions.

</details>


### [136] [Insight on confinement from the QCD effective charge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05144)
*A. Deur*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper proposes α_{g_1} as a physical, observable-based running coupling for QCD—consistent with RG evolution, light-front holography, and data—and links its analytic structure (complex conjugate singularities at Q² = ±iΛₛ²) to confinement via suppression of parton propagators at distances ≳ 1/Λₛ.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To define a physically observable, analytic QCD coupling α_s that reflects confinement through its infrared analytic structure, bridging phenomenology (world α_s data), theory (RG, light-front holography), and confinement mechanisms.

Method: Analytic continuation of the effective charge α_{g_1}, identification of its complex-conjugate singularities in the Euclidean momentum-squared plane, and mapping these singularities to dressed parton propagators (not vertices) using known relations between α_s and Green's functions; derivation of asymptotic long-distance propagator behavior from singularity location.

Result: α_{g_1} exhibits two imaginary conjugate singularities at Q² = ±iΛₛ², leading to parton propagators decaying as e^{-Λₛ|x|/√2}|x|^{-5/2+d_a}, implying exponential suppression beyond ~1/Λₛ — a direct analytic signature of confinement.

Conclusion: Confinement can be intuitively interpreted as the long-distance suppression of QCD Green’s functions, encoded in the analytic structure of an observable coupling α_{g_1}; this bridges nonperturbative dynamics with measurable running coupling behavior.

Abstract: Like the Gell-Mann--Low coupling $α$ of QED, the effective charge $α_{g_1}$ is a physical, observable-defined running coupling that can serve as the QCD coupling $α_s$. It can be represented by an analytic form consistent with renormalization-group evolution, light-front holographic QCD, and the world data on $α_s$. As an observable, $α_{g_1}$ has a physically relevant analytic structure that reflects the underlying partonic dynamics. It displays, in the long-distance regime, two imaginary conjugate singularities at $Q^2=\pm iΛ_s^2$, with $Λ_s$ the QCD scale. To connect this structure to confinement, we use known relations between $α_s$ and the parton dressed propagators and vertices. Since, unlike vertices, propagators characterize a field variation between separate locations, the two singularities are assigned to the propagators. This results in the parton propagators displaying a long-distance behavior $e^{-Λ_s|x|/\sqrt{2}}|x|^{-5/2+d_a}$ where propagation is suppressed beyond distances of order $1/Λ_s$, as expected from confinement. This offers an intuitive interpretation of confinement as the suppression of QCD Green's functions at long distance.

</details>


### [137] [Pathways and impediments towards a detection of the relic neutrino wind](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05221)
*Andrew J. Long,Michiru Uwabo-Niibo,Masahide Yamaguchi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores the feasibility of detecting the cosmic neutrino background (CNB) 'wind'—a dipolar anisotropy in CNB flux due to relative motion between the CNB rest frame and Earth—via angular analysis of electron recoils at the tritium β-decay endpoint; it finds that detection requires ~10⁵× greater exposure than total CNB flux detection, with additional suppression for Majorana neutrinos and stringent demands on energy resolution and systematics control.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To go beyond mere detection of the cosmic neutrino background (CNB) and probe its kinematic structure—specifically the dipolar anisotropy ('CNB wind') arising from Earth's motion relative to the CNB rest frame—as a novel window into neutrino properties and cosmology.

Method: Analytical and phenomenological modeling of the angular distribution of electrons recoiling near the tritium β-decay endpoint, incorporating CNB wind effects; calculation of required exposure (mass × time) for 3σ detection, including treatment of Dirac vs. Majorana neutrino helicity correlations and impact of finite energy resolution.

Result: Detecting the CNB wind requires ≥10⁵× larger exposure than detecting the isotropic CNB flux; for nonrelativistic neutrinos, the Majorana wind signal is suppressed by ~(m_ν/T_ν)² due to helicity-odd correlation cancellation; sub-eV energy resolution and exceptional control of systematics are essential if resolution exceeds the neutrino mass scale.

Conclusion: CNB wind detection is extraordinarily challenging with current or near-future technology, demanding orders-of-magnitude improvements in exposure, energy resolution, and systematic control—especially for Majorana neutrinos—making it a long-term goal for next-generation neutrino experiments.

Abstract: A direct detection of the cosmic neutrino background (CNB) in laboratories on Earth has been called the ``holy grail'' of experimental neutrino physics, but a still more glorious prize awaits. Beyond simply detecting the presence of relic neutrinos and measuring their flux, one may aspire to measure their energy distribution, polarization, anisotropies, temporal variation, and other properties. In this work we focus on the CNB wind, which is the approximately dipolar anisotropy in the CNB flux resulting from the relative velocity of the CNB rest frame and the lab frame. We consider a CNB detection strategy based on measuring the angular distribution of recoiling electrons at the tritium $β$-decay endpoint. In order to quantify the difficulty of detecting the CNB wind, we calculate the required exposure (detector mass times observation duration) for a $3σ$ discovery. We find that detecting the CNB wind would require an exposure that is at least $10^{5}$ times larger than what's required for detecting the CNB flux alone. Additionally if the experimental energy resolution were to exceed the neutrino mass scale, then an exceptionally good control of systematic uncertainties would also be required. For nonrelativistic neutrinos, the Majorana wind signal is suppressed relative to the Dirac case by the cancellation of the leading helicity-odd angular-correlation term, leading parametrically to an exposure penalty of order $(m_ν/T_ν)^2$.

</details>


### [138] [Spin Polarization of Proca Stars Formed by Gravitational Bose--Einstein Condensation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05278)
*Jiajun Chen*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates the internal spin polarization of Proca stars formed from gravitational Bose–Einstein condensation of a three-component nonrelativistic vector field, showing that core spin arises from the polarization state (especially ellipticity and coherence) of the dominant condensed mode—not a universal value—with coherent net spin fraction averaging ~0.62 and varying due to random elliptical polarization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether internal spin polarization is an intrinsic, controllable degree of freedom in gravitationally condensed nonrelativistic Proca stars—and whether it is governed by universal physics or initial-mode polarization properties.

Method: Performed idealized periodic-box numerical simulations of gravitational Bose–Einstein condensation; decomposed aperture-averaged spin into coherent net fraction, local polarization fraction, and their ratio; analyzed the core polarization matrix, its leading eigenvector, and eigenvalue spectrum; compared outcomes across ensembles with independent, correlated, and circular initial vector-field configurations.

Result: Proca stars exhibit sizable but non-maximal coherent core spin (⟨χ_net⟩ ≈ 0.62) with substantial realization-to-realization scatter; scatter is explained by random elliptical polarization of the dominant component-space mode; leading-eigenvector spin fractions align with isotropic random-complex-vector statistics, not equal-amplitude random-phase; circular/fully correlated initial data push dominant mode toward maximal (circular) polarization.

Conclusion: Internal spin polarization is a genuine vector degree of freedom in nonrelativistic Proca stars, controlled by the polarization (particularly ellipticity and coherence) of the dominant condensed mode—not by a fixed universal value.

Abstract: We study the internal spin polarization of Proca stars formed by gravitational Bose--Einstein condensation of a three-component nonrelativistic vector field. In idealized periodic-box simulations, we decompose the aperture-averaged spin into a coherent net fraction, a local polarization fraction, and their ratio, thereby distinguishing genuine coherent core polarization from local spin density whose direction cancels inside the aperture. For independent vector components, condensation produces Proca stars that are sizably but not maximally polarized. Across an independent-component simulation ensemble, the coherent core-spin fraction has mean $\langleχ_{\rm net}\rangle\simeq0.62$, with substantial realization-to-realization scatter. We interpret this scatter as the outcome of random elliptical polarization of the dominant component-space mode, rather than as evidence for a universal Proca-star spin fraction. This interpretation is supported by the core polarization matrix: its leading eigenvector provides an estimate of the ideal single-mode spin fraction, while the difference between this estimate and the directly integrated coherent spin tracks the departure of the core from a rank-one component-space state. The measured leading-eigenvector spin fractions are broadly compatible with an isotropic random-complex-vector reference and less compatible with an equal-amplitude random-phase reference. Correlated and circular initial data drive the dominant component-space mode toward the circular-polarization bound, giving the ordering independent $\rightarrow$ correlated $\rightarrow$ circular. These results show that internal polarization is a genuine vector degree of freedom of gravitationally condensed nonrelativistic Proca stars, and that the resulting core spin is controlled by the polarization of the dominant condensed mode rather than by a fixed universal value.

</details>


### [139] [Parametric Resonance of Higgsed Vector Dark Matter: Inflationary Initial Conditions and Sourced Displacements](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05309)
*Imtiaz Khan,Salvatore Capozziello,G. Mustafa,Niamat Ullah,Farruh Atamurotov*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the initial-condition problem for parametric resonance production of vector dark matter in a Higgsed Abelian sector, showing stochastic inflation fails to generate sufficient dark-Higgs displacement; it identifies a viable 'classically sourced' branch driven by a negative Hubble-induced mass and derives comprehensive consistency conditions linking dark-matter mass to inflationary parameters.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the initial-condition problem for parametric resonance production of vector dark matter — specifically, the origin of the required large initial dark-Higgs field displacement φ₀ that seeds resonance — within a weakly coupled Abelian-Higgs theory.

Method: Combines calibrated nonlinear broad-resonance relic mapping with stochastic inflationary analysis of the dark-Higgs condensate; contrasts stochastic (quantum diffusion) and classically sourced (Hubble-induced mass-driven tracking) mechanisms; imposes and solves simultaneous physical consistency constraints (broad resonance, adiabatic tracking, perturbativity, sub-Planckian displacement, thermal non-erasure, backreaction, vector fluctuation control).

Result: Stochastic inflation generically yields φ/H_I = O(1), insufficient to satisfy broad-resonance and isocurvature bounds requiring φ₀/H_I ≳ 3.3×10⁴ — a robust, model-independent obstruction. A viable alternative — the 'classically sourced branch' — arises from a negative Hubble-induced mass, leading to φ₀ = κH_*/√λ₄ and modified relic scaling m_X ∝ κ⁻³/² λ₄ H_*⁻³/²; full consistency requires simultaneous satisfaction of six distinct physical constraints.

Conclusion: Higgsed-vector parametric resonance is not just a dark-matter production mechanism but a sensitive probe of inflationary and reheating dynamics; its viability hinges on specific initial-condition generation mechanisms, with only the classically sourced branch surviving all theoretical and phenomenological constraints.

Abstract: Parametric resonance in a Higgsed Abelian sector provides an efficient mechanism for producing vector dark matter, but its viability depends crucially on the origin of the initial dark-Higgs displacement that seeds the resonance. In this work, we investigate this initial-condition problem in a weakly coupled Abelian-Higgs theory with potential $V=λ_4(φ^2-v^2)^2/4$, using the calibrated nonlinear broad-resonance relic map together with a stochastic inflationary analysis of the dark-Higgs condensate. We show that a minimal light-spectator realization fails under standard inflationary duration: while broad resonance and isocurvature constraints require \( φ_0/H_I \gtrsim 3.3\times10^4, \) the stochastic equilibrium and finite-duration random walk produce only \( φ/H_I=\mathcal O(1). \) This large displacement mismatch is robust against order-of-magnitude variations in the resonance efficiency and broadness threshold, establishing a model-independent obstruction to the stochastic branch. We then identify a distinct classically sourced branch, generated by a negative Hubble-induced mass, in which the condensate tracks a time-dependent minimum, \( φ_0=κH_*/\sqrt{λ_4}, \) and the radial fluctuation remains heavy during inflation. In this case, the fixed-$e/λ_D$ relic scaling shifts from \( m_X\propto λ_4^{5/8}H_I^{-3/2} \) to \( m_X\propto κ^{-3/2}λ_4 H_*^{-3/2}. \) We derive the simultaneous consistency conditions for this sourced branch, including broad resonance, adiabatic tracking, perturbativity, sub-Planckian displacement, thermal non-erasure, spectator backreaction, and control of inflationary vector fluctuations. Our results establish that Higgsed-vector resonance is not merely a dark-matter production mechanism, but a sensitive probe of the inflationary and reheating dynamics that determine its initial conditions.

</details>


### [140] [Galactic Center Neutrinos from Cosmic Ray-Dark Matter Interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05335)
*Jorge Terol Calvo,Pedro de la Torre Luque,Mainak Mukhopadhyay,Chris Cappiello,Gonzalo Herrera*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses IceCube and ANTARES neutrino data to constrain sub-GeV dark matter via cosmic-ray–DM deep inelastic scattering, deriving competitive 99% C.L. upper limits on the DM-nucleon cross section down to keV-scale masses.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe light (sub-GeV to keV-scale) dark matter using high-energy neutrinos from deep inelastic scattering between Galactic cosmic rays and dark matter in the halo—a novel channel complementary to direct detection.

Method: Combined analysis of high-energy neutrino data from IceCube and ANTARES, incorporating detailed Galactic cosmic-ray distribution maps and modeling neutrino production from meson decays following cosmic-ray–DM deep inelastic scattering.

Result: Derivation of 99% confidence level upper limits on the DM-nucleon scattering cross section, reaching unprecedented sensitivity for DM masses down to ~keV; constraints are competitive with and complementary to existing probes.

Conclusion: Galactic neutrino telescopes provide a powerful new avenue for detecting light dark matter; future observatories (IceCube-Gen2, KM3NeT) will significantly improve sensitivity.

Abstract: The IceCube and ANTARES collaborations have recently reported evidence for high-energy neutrinos associated with the Galactic plane and the Galactic Ridge, offering a new pathway to search for dark matter (DM). Deep inelastic scattering of cosmic rays with sub-GeV DM in the Galactic halo produces a distinctive neutrino signature from meson decays. Using detailed Galactic cosmic-ray maps and ANTARES observations, we derive 99% C.L. upper limits on the DM-nucleon cross section that extend down to keV-scale masses. These results establish Galactic neutrino telescopes as a powerful, complementary probe of light DM, with substantial improvements expected from upcoming IceCube-Gen2 and KM3NeT observations.

</details>


<div id='hep-ex'></div>

# hep-ex [[Back]](#toc)

### [141] [RTL Fault Injection of a Deployed Graph Neural Network Trigger for Belle II](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03153)
*Georgios Sotiropoulos,Marc Neu,Tanja Harbaum,Torben Ferber,Jürgen Becker*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper conducts the first RTL-level fault-injection study on GNN-ETM, a deployed hardware neural-network Level-1 trigger in Belle II, quantifying susceptibility to radiation-induced deadlocks, timeouts, and packet-integrity violations; it reveals a monitoring asymmetry in current verification and proposes inter-stage liveness monitoring—improving MTTF estimation accuracy by up to 78.7% and enabling prioritized hardening.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Radiation-induced faults (e.g., SEUs) in FPGA-based Level-1 triggers threaten real-time data filtering reliability, risking scientific loss and hardware damage; existing verification relies on output-only monitoring, which fails to capture internal pipeline stalls or timing violations before output corruption.

Method: RTL-level fault injection targeting 211,245 signals across the GNN-ETM trigger; two complementary campaigns injecting 1,442,840 Single-Event Upsets; analysis of three critical failure modes—deadlocks, timeouts, and packet-integrity violations; comparison of output-only vs. inter-stage liveness monitoring for MTTF estimation.

Result: Identified significant monitoring asymmetry: output-only observation underestimates failure likelihood; inter-stage liveness monitoring yields up to 78.7% higher MTTF estimates; per-stage fault susceptibility data enables precise hardening prioritization.

Conclusion: Inter-stage liveness monitoring is essential for accurate radiation resilience assessment in real-time neural-network triggers; the empirical fault-injection dataset and methodology establish a new benchmark for dependable hardware AI in high-radiation environments.

Abstract: As particle physics detectors grow in scale, High Energy Physics experiments must process ever-increasing data volumes. Level-1 trigger systems, implemented on Field-Programmable Gate Arrays and increasingly using neural-network algorithms, filter this data in real time. However, their proximity to the interaction point exposes them to radiation, which can corrupt outputs, stall processing pipelines, or damage hardware, with significant financial and scientific consequences. In this work, we present the first Register Transfer Level fault-injection study of a deployed Level-1 hardware neural-network trigger, GNN-ETM in the Belle II trigger system. We target three failure modes most consequential to a real-time trigger pipeline: deadlocks, timeouts, and packet-integrity violations. Through two complementary campaigns, we inject 1 442 840 Single-Event Upsets across 211 245 signals. We find a monitoring asymmetry in the existing verification infrastructure and propose inter-stage liveness monitoring as a more accurate alternative to output-only observation, showing that Mean Time To Failure estimates from the two approaches differ by up to 78.7%. The resulting per-stage data identifies the highest-priority hardening targets.

</details>


### [142] [Searching for Dark Photons with a room-temperature dielectric haloscope](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03240)
*Siyin Li,Paschos Ioannis,Zhengyin Yang,Alexandros Spiliotis,Husheng Guan,Pavlos G. Savvidis,Shengchao Li*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports a search for dark-photon dark matter using a room-temperature dielectric multilayer haloscope coupled to a CMOS focal plane; no signal was detected, yielding a 90% CL upper limit of κ < 4.0×10⁻¹³ at 1.9 eV/c².


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe sub-eV to few-eV dark-photon dark matter, which couples weakly to photons and may constitute galactic dark matter, using a scalable, room-temperature experimental approach.

Method: A dielectric multilayer haloscope enhances photon conversion from dark photons near 2 eV; emitted photons are imaged with spatial resolution and few-photon sensitivity on a calibrated CMOS focal plane; template-based inference uses in situ calibration of the full optical chain (stack–lens–CMOS) and distinguishes signal from background using dedicated control data.

Result: No statistically significant excess observed; 90% confidence-level upper limit on kinetic mixing parameter κ = 4.0×10⁻¹³ at dark-photon mass mₐ = 1.9 eV/c².

Conclusion: The experiment sets the strongest direct laboratory constraint to date on dark-photon dark matter in the ~2 eV mass range, demonstrating the viability of dielectric haloscopes with CMOS readout for optical-wavelength axion-like particle searches.

Abstract: We present a search for dark-photon dark matter with a room-temperature dielectric multilayer haloscope. The dielectric stack enhances photon conversion near 2 eV, and a spatially resolved CMOS focal plane records the emitted photons with few-photon sensitivity. We calibrate the stack-lens-CMOS response in situ and use the calibrated focal-plane pattern in a template-based inference. With 904 h of search data and 404 h of background-control data, we observe no excess and set a 90% confidence-level upper limit of $κ< 4.0\times10^{-13}$ for dark-photon dark matter with mass 1.9$\,\mathrm{eV}/c^2$.

</details>


### [143] [Flow-Based Surrogates for High-Dimensional Likelihoods in Experimental Neutrino Physics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03477)
*Mathias El Baz,Lorenzo Giannessi,Adrien Blanchet,Federico Sánchez*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper introduces normalizing flows—specifically a hybrid coupling + autoregressive spline architecture—to model high-dimensional, non-Gaussian near-detector likelihoods in long-baseline neutrino experiments, achieving 98% effective sample size vs. 5% for Gaussian approximations, while retaining closed-form evaluation and sampling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Precision neutrino oscillation measurements require accurate modeling of systematic uncertainties in near-detector data; standard Gaussian approximations fail when the likelihood is high-dimensional and non-Gaussian, necessitating flexible, tractable statistical models.

Method: A hybrid normalizing flow architecture combining invertible coupling layers and autoregressive rational quadratic spline flows, initialized from a Gaussian approximation, is trained on a 110-parameter near-detector likelihood replica with 10 explicitly non-Gaussian nuisance parameters.

Result: The trained flow achieves 98% relative effective sample size versus MCMC reference (vs. ~5% for Gaussian), accurately reproduces posterior marginals and correlations, and remains fully differentiable, samplable, and pointwise evaluable.

Conclusion: Normalizing flows provide a faithful, portable, and computationally efficient alternative to MCMC for constrained likelihood modeling in neutrino experiments, enabling robust uncertainty propagation and scalable joint near–far detector fits.

Abstract: Precision long-baseline neutrino experiments use near-detector data to constrain systematic uncertainties on the unoscillated neutrino flux, a prerequisite for accurate oscillation parameter measurements at the far detector. When the constrained likelihood is high-dimensional and non-Gaussian, this procedure demands advanced statistical treatment. Here we show that normalizing flows provide faithful and portable likelihood models for this problem. Leveraging an initial Gaussian approximation of the likelihood, we train a hybrid architecture combining coupling transformations and autoregressive spline flows. We demonstrate the method on a representative near-detector likelihood replica with 110 systematic uncertainty parameters, 10 of which explicitly introduce non-Gaussianities in the posterior. The trained model achieves a relative effective sample size of 98%, compared with about 5% for the Gaussian approximation, and reproduces a Markov chain Monte Carlo reference while remaining closed-form, samplable, and pointwise evaluable, making it suited to downstream uncertainty propagation and future near-detector to far-detector fits.

</details>


### [144] [GPU-Accelerated Matrix-Based Hough Transform for Online Track Reconstruction in the STCF MDC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04067)
*Liang Peng,Baolin Zhang,Zhaoli Guo,Jiarui Zhao,Yizhen Ma,Aonan Zhu,Huilin Li,Zhujun Fang*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper proposes a GPU-accelerated matrix-based Hough transform for real-time track reconstruction in the Super Tau-Charm Facility's main drift chamber, achieving 151.57× speedup over CPU and retaining 93.04% of signal tracks while reducing hit volume to 34.92%.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The STCF's high luminosity leads to high event rates, detector occupancy, and background levels, imposing stringent requirements on real-time track reconstruction—especially for low-pT particles with curved or multi-turn trajectories in the MDC.

Method: A GPU-accelerated, matrix-formulated conformal Hough transform is developed via algorithm-architecture co-design: irregular parameter-space computations are restructured into regular matrix operations; core kernels are optimized for CUDA thread mapping and GPU memory hierarchy to maximize parallelism and minimize data-transfer overhead.

Result: On five representative simulated physics channels with nominal background, the method achieves 93.04% average signal retention ratio, reduces retained hit volume to 34.92% of original, and processes 1,000 events in ~0.14 s (151.57× speedup over CPU baseline).

Conclusion: The proposed method significantly enhances throughput and efficiency of online track reconstruction for STCF, offering a scalable, parallelizable framework applicable to future high-luminosity collider experiments.

Abstract: The Super Tau-Charm Facility (STCF) is a proposed next-generation high-luminosity electron-positron collider operating at center-of-mass energies of 2-7 GeV for precision studies of tau-charm physics. Its high event rate, detector occupancy, and background level impose stringent requirements on real-time track reconstruction in MDC, particularly for low-transverse-momentum particles with strongly curved or multi-turn trajectories. To address this challenge, we develop a GPU-accelerated matrix-based Hough transform method for online track reconstruction in the STCF MDC. Following an algorithm-architecture co-design paradigm, the data representation and computational workflow of the conformal Hough transform are reformulated for GPU execution. The original irregular parameter-space computations are organized into regular matrix-based operations, and the core computations are adapted to CUDA thread organization and the GPU memory hierarchy to exploit the inherent parallelism of the Hough transform and reduce computational and data-transfer overhead. Tests on five representative simulated physics channels with nominal background overlay show an average signal retention ratio of 93.04%, while reducing the retained hit volume to 34.92% of the original level. The GPU implementation processes 1,000 events in approximately 0.14s, achieving a speedup of 151.57 x compared with the CPU baseline. These results demonstrate that the proposed method substantially improves track reconstruction throughput while preserving track-associated hits, providing a new methodological perspective for real-time track reconstruction in future high-luminosity particle-collider experiments.

</details>


### [145] [Neural-Network-Assisted Binary Template Construction for Matrix-Based Pattern Matching in the STCF MDC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04271)
*Zhaoli Guo,Qingyu Li,Baolin Zhang,Jiarui Zhao,Aonan Zhu,Liang Peng,Huilin Li*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper proposes a neural-network-assisted framework to optimize binary template libraries for local track segment finding in the Main Drift Chamber of the Super Tau-Charm Facility, enabling high-efficiency, deterministic, and fast online pattern matching without runtime inference.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: High background rates and limited detection efficiency in the high-luminosity Super Tau-Charm Facility degrade signal retention and increase false triggers in conventional matrix-based pattern matching for local track segment finding.

Method: Formulates template construction as a differentiable multi-objective optimization problem; uses a neural network to jointly learn binary template parameters under physics and hardware constraints; exports only binary templates—no NN inference at runtime.

Result: The optimized binary template library achieves high and robust signal retention across transverse momentum ranges and background levels in simulation; supports flexible coverage tuning; preserves bitwise speed, determinism, and parallelizability.

Conclusion: The approach successfully decouples offline AI-driven physics optimization from online deterministic processing, offering a scalable paradigm for AI-enhanced real-time trigger systems in future high-luminosity colliders.

Abstract: The Super Tau-Charm Facility, operating at high luminosity, will produce high event rates and high data throughput, imposing stringent requirements on fast track finding and data reduction and compression algorithms in the High-Level Trigger. Local track segment finding in the Main Drift Chamber underpins subsequent segment combination and full track reconstruction, yet high background rates and limited detection efficiency can significantly increase the risk of false triggers and signal loss in pattern matching algorithms. This paper presents a neural-network-assisted framework for constructing binary template libraries used in matrix-based pattern matching for MDC local track segment finding. The framework formulates template construction as a differentiable multi-objective optimization problem, employing a neural network to jointly learn template parameters under multiple constraints. After training, only binary template pairs are exported and deployed into the existing bitwise pattern matching routine, requiring no neural network inference at runtime and thus preserving the deterministic, fast, and parallelizable nature of the online algorithm. Experimental results based on simulation samples demonstrate that, under limited detection efficiency, the resulting template library maintains relatively high signal retention across different transverse momentum ranges and background levels, and can be flexibly tailored to adjust the coverage range according to practical requirements. The proposed approach decouples the physics performance from the computational speed by combining the improved physics performance brought by offline neural-network-based optimization with the determinism and high speed of a conventional online algorithm, suggesting a new research direction for artificial-intelligence-enhanced online data processing in high-luminosity particle collider experiments.

</details>

Z. Yuan,J. Yuan,L. Yuan,Y. Yusa,L. Zani,M. Zeyrek,B. Zhang,X. Zhao,V. Zhilich,J. S. Zhou,Q. D. Zhou,L. Zhu,R. Žlebčík*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports measurements of e+e- → Υ_J(1D)η and e+e- → Υ_J(1D)π+π- cross sections near the Υ(10860) resonance using 142.5 fb⁻¹ of Belle data, yielding precise masses, mass splittings, and branching fractions for the three Υ(1D) states (J=1,2,3), with significances up to >10σ.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To precisely determine properties (masses, mass splittings, production cross sections, and radiative decay branching fractions) of the bottomonium Υ_J(1D) (J = 1, 2, 3) states, which are crucial for testing quark potential models and QCD-based predictions of heavy quarkonia.

Method: Analysis of 142.5 fb⁻¹ e⁺e⁻ collision data collected with the Belle detector at KEKB, centered at √s = 10.73–11.02 GeV (mainly at 10.866 GeV, the Υ(10860) peak); reconstruction of Υ_J(1D) via χ_bJγ decays, followed by η or π⁺π⁻ in final state; extraction of Born cross sections × branching fractions and mass parameters using unbinned maximum-likelihood fits including systematic uncertainties.

Result: Significant observations (>3σ) of all three Υ_J(1D) states: Υ₁(1D) (4.8σ), Υ₂(1D) (>10σ), Υ₃(1D) (3.0σ); m(Υ₂(1D)) = 10167.0 ± 1.0 ± 0.2 MeV/c²; Δm₁₂ = 11.8 ± 1.5 ± 0.4 MeV/c²; Δm₂₃ = 7.6 ± 2.4 ± 0.6 MeV/c²; first measurement of energy-dependent cross sections for e⁺e⁻ → Υ_J(1D)η and e⁺e⁻ → Υ_J(1D)π⁺π⁻.

Conclusion: The results provide the most precise mass and mass-splitting determinations for the Υ(1D) triplet to date, constrain theoretical models of bottomonium spectroscopy, and demonstrate that Υ(10860) serves as an effective source for producing P-wave bottomonium states via initial-state radiation or direct production.

Abstract: We study the processes $e^+e^-\toΥ_J(1D)η$ and $e^+e^-\toΥ_J(1D)π^+π^-$ at center-of-mass energies $\sqrt{s}$=(10.73 -- 11.02) GeV using a $142.5\,\mathrm{fb}^{-1}$ data sample, including 122~fb$^{-1}$ near the $Υ$(10860) peak ($\sqrt{s}$ = 10.866 GeV), collected with the Belle detector at the KEKB asymmetric-energy $e^+e^-$ collider. From the peak sample, the products of Born cross section times branching fraction are obtained for $σ_{\rm Born}(e^+e^-\toΥ_J(1D)η)$ or $σ_{\rm Born}(e^+e^-\toΥ_J(1D)π^+π^-)$ and ${\cal B}(Υ_J(1D)\toχ_{b1}γ)$ or ${\cal B}(Υ_J(1D)\toχ_{b2}γ)$ for each $Υ_J(1D)$ state. The corresponding branching fractions for $Υ(10860)$ decays are also obtained. The significances of the $Υ_1(1D)$, $Υ_2(1D)$, and $Υ_3(1D)$ signals are 4.8$σ$, ${>}10σ$, and 3.0$σ$, respectively, including systematic uncertainties. The mass for $Υ_2(1D)$ is measured to be $(10167.0\pm 1.0\pm 0.2)$ MeV/$c^2$, where the first and second uncertainties are statistical and systematic. The mass splittings $Δm_{12}=m(Υ_2(1D))-m(Υ_1(1D))$ and $Δm_{23}=m(Υ_3(1D))-m(Υ_2(1D))$ are $(11.8\pm1.5\pm0.4)$ MeV/$c^2$ and $(7.6\pm2.4\pm0.6)$ MeV/$c^2$, respectively.~We determine the energy dependence of the cross sections for $e^+e^-\toΥ_J(1D)η$ and $e^+e^-\toΥ_J(1D)π^+π^-$ for the $Υ_1(1D)$, $Υ_2(1D)$, and $Υ_3(1D)$ states, combined.

</details>


### [147] [Standard Model Soft and Hard QCD at ATLAS and CMS](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04767)
*Maciej Trzebinski*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reviews recent QCD measurements from ATLAS and CMS at the LHC, covering soft (event shapes, hadronisation), hard (di-jet production), and multi-parton (double-parton scattering in same-sign WW) regimes, plus forward proton detector applications to diffractive and photon-induced processes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To comprehensively test Quantum Chromodynamics across diverse energy and momentum scales—from non-perturbative soft dynamics to high-precision perturbative regimes—and probe proton substructure via multi-parton interactions and diffractive phenomena.

Method: Analysis of proton-proton collision data from ATLAS and CMS experiments at the LHC; includes event-shape observables in minimum-bias events, inclusive di-jet cross sections, double-parton scattering signatures in same-sign WW production, and forward proton tagging for diffractive/photon-induced processes.

Result: New precision constraints on soft QCD models and hadronisation; improved validation of pQCD predictions in di-jet kinematics; evidence and quantification of double-parton scattering contributions; demonstration of forward detectors’ capability to isolate exclusive and diffractive processes.

Conclusion: QCD remains robustly validated across all accessible scales at the LHC; complementary measurements from different observables and detector systems deepen understanding of proton structure, parton dynamics, and non-perturbative effects.

Abstract: Quantum Chromodynamics (QCD) is explored at the Large Hadron Collider (LHC) over a wide range of energy scales, from soft hadronic interactions to hard parton scattering. This contribution presents a selection of recent measurements from the ATLAS and CMS experiments probing QCD in proton--proton collisions at the LHC. The results span a broad range of momentum scales, from studies of charged-particle event-shape observables in minimum-bias events, which are sensitive to soft QCD dynamics and hadronisation, to measurements of inclusive di-jet production that provide stringent tests of perturbative QCD. Recent studies of double-parton scattering in same-sign $WW$ production are also discussed, offering new insight into multi-parton interactions inside the proton. Finally, the capabilities of the forward proton detectors operated by the ATLAS and CMS collaborations are briefly reviewed, highlighting their contribution to the LHC physics programme through measurements of diffractive and photon-induced processes.

</details>

Zhu,Lin Zhu,S. H. Zhu,T. J. Zhu,W. D. Zhu,W. J. Zhu,W. Z. Zhu,Y. C. Zhu,Z. A. Zhu,X. Y. Zhuang,M. Zhuge,J. H. Zou,J. Zu*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: First observation of χ_cJ → pK⁻Λ̄η + c.c. (J=0,1,2) decays using 2.71 billion ψ(3686) events at BESIII; branching fractions measured with >5σ significance; evidence for Λ(1520) and Λ̄(1690) in invariant mass spectra; results inform charmonium hadronic decays and nonperturbative QCD baryon production.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe hadronic decays of P-wave charmonia (χ_cJ), test QCD models of baryon production and hadronization in the nonperturbative regime, and search for hyperon resonance contributions in multi-baryon final states.

Method: Analysis of (2712.4 ± 14.3) × 10⁶ ψ(3686) decays collected with the BESIII detector at BEPCII; full reconstruction of χ_cJ → pK⁻Λ̄η + c.c.; background suppression via kinematic fits and particle identification; invariant mass spectra examined for hyperon resonances; branching fractions extracted using ψ(3686) → γχ_cJ radiative transition yield and efficiency-corrected event counts.

Result: First observation of χ_c0, χ_c1, χ_c2 → pK⁻Λ̄η + c.c. with significances >5σ; measured branching fractions: ℬ(χ_c0) = (5.3 ± 0.7 ± 0.5) × 10⁻⁵, ℬ(χ_c1) = (9.8 ± 0.6 ± 0.6) × 10⁻⁵, ℬ(χ_c2) = (9.3 ± 0.6 ± 0.6) × 10⁻⁵; clear signals of Λ(1520) in pK⁻ and Λ̄(1690) in Λ̄η spectra.

Conclusion: These measurements provide new constraints on theoretical models of charmonium decay dynamics and baryon formation; the relative suppression of χ_c0 vs χ_c1,2 supports expectations from helicity selection rules and quark-pair creation models; inclusion of both resonant and non-resonant contributions enables more robust comparisons to lattice QCD and effective field theory predictions.

Abstract: By analyzing $(2712.4 \pm 14.3) \times 10^{6}$ $ψ(3686)$ events collected with the BESIII detector operating at the BEPCII collider, the decays $χ_{cJ} \to pK^{-}\barΛη+ \mathrm{c.c.}$ ($J=0,1,2$) are observed for the first time, with statistical significances exceeding $5σ$ for all three $χ_{cJ}$ states. The measured branching fractions are $\mathcal{B}(χ_{c0} \to pK^{-}\barΛη+ \mathrm{c.c.}) = (5.3 \pm 0.7 \pm 0.5) \times 10^{-5}$, $\mathcal{B}(χ_{c1} \to pK^{-}\barΛη+ \mathrm{c.c.}) = (9.8 \pm 0.6 \pm 0.6) \times 10^{-5}$, and $\mathcal{B}(χ_{c2} \to pK^{-}\barΛη+ \mathrm{c.c.}) = (9.3 \pm 0.6 \pm 0.6) \times 10^{-5}$, where the first uncertainties are statistical and the second are systematic. Structures consistent with the known hyperon resonances $Λ(1520)$ and $\barΛ(1690)$ are seen in the $pK^{-}$ and $\barΛη$ invariant mass spectra, respectively. The reported branching fractions include both resonant and non-resonant contributions. These results provide new experimental information on hadronic decays of $P$-wave charmonium states and contribute to the understanding of baryon production and hadronization dynamics in the nonperturbative QCD regime.

</details>


### [149] [Search for nonresonant triple Higgs boson production in the final state with six bottom quarks in proton-proton collisions at $\sqrt{s}$ = 13 TeV](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05145)
*CMS Collaboration*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: CMS searches for nonresonant triple Higgs (HHH) production in the 6b final state at 13 TeV with 138 fb⁻¹; no excess observed; sets strongest limits to date on σ(HHH) and Higgs self-couplings κ₃ and κ₄, excluding part of the perturbative unitarity–allowed (κ₃, κ₄) parameter space.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe the Higgs trilinear (κ₃) and quartic (κ₄) self-couplings—key predictions of the Standard Model—and test electroweak symmetry breaking via direct observation of nonresonant triple Higgs production.

Method: Event selection targeting six b-jets from H→bb decays in pp collisions at √s = 13 TeV; background estimation using data-driven and simulation-based techniques; statistical inference with profile likelihood ratio to set upper limits on σ(HHH) and constraints on κ₃ and κ₄ under fixed assumptions for the other coupling.

Result: Observed (expected) 95% CL upper limit on σ(HHH): 44 (43) fb (588×/572× SM); κ₃ constrained to [−7.4, 12.4] ([−6.4, 11.2]) assuming κ₄=1; κ₄ constrained to [−177, 185] ([−180, 190]) assuming κ₃=1.

Conclusion: First exclusion of part of the (κ₃, κ₄) parameter space permitted by perturbative unitarity; establishes the most stringent direct limits on nonresonant HHH production to date, tightening constraints on Higgs self-interactions beyond SM expectations.

Abstract: A search for nonresonant triple Higgs boson (HHH) production in the final state with six bottom quarks is performed using proton-proton collisions at $\sqrt{s}$ = 13 TeV corresponding to an integrated luminosity of 138 fb$^{-1}$ recorded by the CMS experiment. No significant excess of events over the background prediction is seen. Observed (expected) 95% confidence level upper limits on the signal cross section are set at 44 (43) fb, corresponding to 588 (572) times the standard model expectation. The observed (expected) constraint on the trilinear coupling modifier $κ_3$ is $-$7.4 $\lt$ $κ_3$ $\lt$ 12.4 ($-$6.4 $\lt$ κ_3 $\lt$ 11.2), assuming the quartic coupling modifier $κ_4$ = 1. The corresponding constraint on $κ_4$ is $-$177 $\lt$ $κ_4$ $\lt$ 185 ($-$180 $\lt$ $κ_4$ $\lt$ 190), assuming $κ_3$ = 1. This analysis provides the most stringent constraint to date on nonresonant HHH production and excludes, for the first time, part of the $(κ_3,κ_4)$ space allowed by the perturbative unitarity bound.

</details>


### [150] [A three-step proposal for searching for light shining through walls in the X-ray band at the High Energy Photon Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05288)
*R. T. Chang,S. Feng,X. P. Geng,C. Y. Hu,H. T. Hu,Y. Y. Hu,Y. H. Huang,B. Liao,F. L. Liu,P. Luan,S. S. Lv,M. L. Qiu,S. K. Shao,Q. L. Shuai,Q. Tang,H. R. Wang,D. Wu,M. Y. Wu,J. S. Xie,Z. H. Zhang,Z. H. Zhang*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: Proposes a novel three-step light-shining-through-walls (LSW) experiment at HEPS ID21 beamline to search for dark photons and WISPs in the 1 eV–400 keV mass range, extending coverage beyond current X-ray LSW experiments with high sensitivity and minimal model dependence.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The fundamental nature of dark matter remains unknown; dark photons and other WISPs are well-motivated light dark sector candidates requiring laboratory probes with enhanced sensitivity and broader mass coverage.

Method: A three-step LSW scheme using high-brilliance, high-energy X-rays from HEPS ID21: (1) short-term dedicated exposure, (2) long-term synchronous accompanying measurement, and (3) WISP detection under strong magnetic fields.

Result: Projected sensitivity constrains dark photon parameter space in the 1 eV–400 keV mass range, covering previously unexplored regions by existing X-ray LSW experiments.

Conclusion: The HEPS-based LSW experiment provides a model-independent, purely laboratory-based probe of dark sector particles, advancing beyond-Standard-Model physics.

Abstract: Despite compelling observational evidence for dark matter (DM), its fundamental physical properties remain poorly understood. In this report, we propose a three-step light-shining-through-walls (LSW) experimental scheme utilizing the high-brilliance, high-energy X-rays from the ID21 Hard X-ray Imaging Beamline at the High Energy Photon Source (HEPS) to search for signatures of dark photons (DPs) and other weakly interacting slim particles (WISPs). The scheme includes three steps of LSW experiments: a short-term (several days) dedicated exposure experiment, a long-term (several years) synchronous accompanying experiment, and a WISP detection with strong magnetic fields. Projection results show that this HEPS-based LSW experiment can effectively constrain DP parameters in the 1 eV--400 keV mass range, covering unexploited parameter space of the existing X-ray LSW experiments. It provides a least model-dependent and most purely-laboratory approach for probing dark sector particles and advancing new physics research beyond the Standard Model gradually.

</details>


### [151] [Measurement of the $b$-jet identification efficiency in dileptonic $t\bar{t}$ events using proton-proton collision data at $\sqrt{s}=13.6$ TeV collected with the ATLAS detector](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05322)
*The ATLAS Collaboration*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper evaluates the GN2 transformer-based jet flavour tagging algorithm for b-jet identification using 56 fb⁻¹ of ATLAS data at √s = 13.6 TeV; it reports improved rejection of light- and charm-jets over DL1d, measures b-jet efficiency vs. pT and cumulative efficiency bins, and provides data–MC correction factors (0.9–1.3) with ~1% total uncertainty for pT > 60 GeV.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve b-jet identification performance beyond existing deep-learning models (e.g., DL1d) in high-energy physics analyses, especially for precision measurements and new physics searches at the LHC.

Method: GN2—a transformer-based neural network—is trained and evaluated on ATLAS proton-proton collision data (2022–2023, √s = 13.6 TeV, 56 fb⁻¹). b-jet efficiency and jet flavour composition are extracted simultaneously from a tt̄-enriched sample. Efficiency is measured differentially in jet transverse momentum (20–400 GeV) and six cumulative efficiency intervals derived from simulated tt̄ events. Data–MC discrepancies yield correction factors.

Result: GN2 achieves up to 2× (3×) higher rejection of light-flavour (charm-flavour) jets than DL1d at equal b-jet efficiency. Measured b-jet efficiencies agree with simulation within correction factors of 0.9–1.3. Total uncertainty is ~1% for pT > 60 GeV in the lowest-efficiency bin ([65%, 0%]).

Conclusion: GN2 significantly outperforms DL1d in b-jet tagging, demonstrates robust calibration against data, and is validated for use in ATLAS physics analyses with quantified systematic uncertainties.

Abstract: This paper presents the performance of the identification of jets containing $b$-hadrons ($b$-jets) for the GN2 algorithm, a transformer-based model for jet flavour tagging, using data collected by the ATLAS detector at the LHC. The analysis uses proton-proton collision data recorded in 2022 and 2023 at a centre-of-mass energy of $\sqrt{s} = 13.6$ TeV, corresponding to an integrated luminosity of 56 fb$^{-1}$. The $b$-jet identification efficiency and jet flavour composition are extracted simultaneously from a sample enriched in top-quark pair events ($t\bar{t}$). This efficiency is measured as a function of the jet transverse momentum in the range of 20-400 GeV and across six intervals of cumulative efficiency as derived in simulated $t\bar{t}$ events: [100%, 90%], [90%, 85%], [85%, 77%], [77%, 70%], [70%, 65%], and [65%, 0%]. The GN2 algorithm demonstrates significant performance improvements over its predecessor DL1d, achieving up to a factor of two (three) higher rejection of light-flavour (charm-flavour) jets at the same $b$-jet efficiency. The measured efficiencies in data are compared with simulation to derive correction factors ranging from 0.9 to 1.3. The total uncertainty is around 1% for jets with transverse momentum larger than 60 GeV in the [65%, 0%] interval of cumulative efficiency.

</details>
